# worldd

The C++ world server (architecture spec §3.2). Plan A4 built its connection layer: a game client
placed on worldd connects over UDP, its join ticket is redeemed with the gateway, its character is
loaded, and it logs in through the control channel up to `NMT_Join`. Plan A5 spawns the player after
that (the native bootstrap, "The player's session after Join" below): its actors go out on actor
channels, the client's requests are answered and each move it makes is checked: acknowledged when it is plausible,
corrected when it is not (plan A6, "Movement and saving" below), and the player's last good position is saved through the
character service. Plan A7 adds the abilities Sprint, Dodge and Rest with the player's Stamina: costs, cooldowns,
effects and regeneration run on the server and are replicated to the client ("Abilities and stamina" below). Plan B3 computes the character's stats
with the stat program of `content.db` and keeps them right as the gear changes ("Stats" below). It builds on the
replication library (`libs/aocnet/protocol/repl`, plan A4R) and the connection's actor channels (below).

## Modules

| File | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `net/udp_socket` | IPv4 UDP socket (Windows and POSIX); the `Address` sessions are keyed by |
| `net/cookie` | The handshake cookie: HMAC-SHA1 of timestamp and address under two secrets rotated every 15 s; valid for 40 s |
| `net/endpoint` | The stateless handshake, one session per client address and a cap on all sessions, logins, handshake retries, duplicate logins, timeouts |
| `net/connection` | Packet acks, reliable sequences, resends, in-order delivery, partial bunches, keep-alives; actor channels: chains split into partial bunches, open and close, ack events; a bound on the unreliable bunches queued behind a full packet window |
| `net/control_channel` | NMT: Hello -> Challenge, Login -> Welcome (once the login is accepted) or Failure, Join |
| `login/login_service`, `login/login_worker` | RedeemTicket with the gateway, then GetCharacter, on a worker thread |
| `svc/grpc_services` | gRPC clients for gateway, character and mesh; every call carries the service token and a 5 s deadline (`save_position` and `save_inventory`: 2 s) |
| `svc/service_token` | Reads `dist\services\service.token` |
| `bootstrap/` | The native bootstrap of one joining player (plan A5): `PlayerSetup` in, the ordered chains out; pure, no sockets (below) |
| `nav/` | The area's ground (plan E1, stage 2): the nav file, the Recast builder `aoc_navbuild` runs and the Detour queries (below) |
| `npc/` | The NPCs' standing and walking (plan E1, stage 2): `NpcWorld`, `Mover`, the motion table and the loader that gives each NPC its life (below) |
| `session/player_session`, `session/player_world` | The player after `NMT_Join` (plan A5, E3): the setup from the record and content.db, the four actors' channels, the join sequence through `Connection::send_chain`, the client's requests answered, acks fed back (below) |
| `session/move_checker` | The plausibility checks of plan A6 as pure logic: a move against the last good position, the limits and where each number comes from (below) |
| `items/inventory` | The inventory rules of plan B2 as pure logic: the three storages, wearing, taking off and moving items, the events the client is told in the retail order, the visible gear (`HeldItems`, `AppearanceIDs`) an inventory shows; the equip facts come from an `ItemCatalog` (`content.db`'s `item` and `item_equip_slot`) (below) |
| `session/abilities` | The ability runtime of plan A7 as pure logic on a clock the caller gives: Sprint, Dodge and Rest, Stamina and its regeneration, cooldowns, effects, and what to send (`bootstrap::AbilityChange`, `ClientEndAbility`); numbers from `content.db` (below) |
| `bootstrap/ability_replication.h` | What the runtime changes on the pawn as plain data (stat deltas, `CurrentEffects`, `ReplicatedActiveAbilities` and `Cooldowns` items); `PlayerBootstrap::apply_ability_change`, `ability_update` and `end_ability` turn it into chains |
| `session/position_saver` | `CharacterSaver`: the worker thread behind `PositionSink`, which saves positions and inventories through the character service (below) |
| `content/content_db` | A read-only reader of `content.db` (plan C1's SQLite file): a class by archetype id, the six primary attributes by class and level (below); since plan B3 also the stat program, the items' stat data and the equip slot names, and `ContentGearCatalog` (the stat engine's view of the items); since plan D1 the item uses, action responses and reward tables, the leveling types with their curves and the auto-granted ranks |
| `session/leveling` | Plan D1: the Adventuring level curve and its cap, the level scrolls' XP formula and what a grant of XP does (the levels crossed, the ranks they grant), as pure logic |
| `session/item_use` | Plan D1: what using an item does (GiveItem's rewards, SetPrimaryArchetype), planned over `content.db` and applied by the session |
| `session/talents` | Plan D1, stage 2: the skill trees, nodes and ranks of `content.db` (`Catalog`), a character's bought ranks (`Book`), what a `ServerPurchaseSkillBuild` may do (`Book::plan`) and the abilities the ranks and the weapon in hand make (`join_abilities`), as pure logic |
| `session/action_bar` | Plan D1, stage 2: the character's action bar as pure data: the slots a client may set, the default, placing and removing skills |
| `world/actor_registry`, `world/relevancy` | Plan E1: the world's other actors (NPCs, resource nodes, a player's lingering zones) held once for the whole worldd, each as the replicated state a connection opens plus what the server knows of it (kind, position, owner, counters of change), a grid of 100 m cells for the radius query, listeners on removal; and the arithmetic of the two rings (awake about 150 m, snapshot about 350 m, 10 m of hysteresis) (below) |
| `world/interaction_state` | Plan E1 (fix 3): the interaction gate's state of an interactable NPC: `CanInputActivate` (the GM vendors', in the registry) and the per-player entries in the two state maps (made per connection by `session/actor_view` on a copy, see "The interaction gate") |
| `session/actor_view` | Plan E1: what one connection has of the registry's actors: a channel for each one it holds open (the lowest free from 3, held until its close is acknowledged), the shadow of what the client was sent, awake / dormant-snapshot / out states, the Dormancy, Relevancy and Destroyed closes, the cap on opens per 67 ms pass (below) |
| `spawns/` | Plan E1: the area (`area.h`: centre, radius, the one place its size is set), the retail opens parsed into templates (`captured_spawns`, from the generated `area_spawns.h`), the data's NPCs chosen from content.db's population chain (`population`), and `load_area` filling the registry from the three sources (below) |
| `combat/rewards`, `combat/aftermath`, `world/corpse` | Plan E1, stage 3, part 2: the reward tables evaluated for a kill (the loot, the experience, its decay), what follows an NPC's death (the experience, the loot, the corpse's life, the respawn) and the corpse's and the pile's loot components (retail's blocks) (below) |
| `spawns/mount_spawn`, `session/mounts` | The mounts milestone, stage 2: a summoned mount as a world actor made from a retail donor (`MountBook`: the data's mount tables, the three captured donors, the archetypes, `Mount_Base` stats at the owner's level), and one player's mount as logic on the session's clock (`MountController`: the summon, the refusal, the desummon and their retail timeline; below) |
| `vendor/gm_vendor` | Plan E1: the GM vendors as pure logic over content.db: the `PTR_ONLY_Vendor_*` defs and their stock, the layout in front of the login spot, and the purchase plan (below) |
| `world/city_nodes`, `world/zoi_surface`, `svc/city_node_feed` | Nodes 1, stage 1: the city node registry (content.db's nodes and zones plus the live levels), the whole-world zone lookup, the thread that watches the citynode service; the node reads of the reward tables (below) |
| `world/node_wire`, `world/node_control`, `svc/city_node_control`, `libs/aocnet` `repl/node_rpc` | Nodes 1, stage 2: what the client is told about the nodes (the GameState's maps for retail's 46 nodes, the summaries and idle siege summaries, OwnerInfo's account characters), the live delta of the maps after a level change, and the client's node GM console through the citynode service (README, "The nodes the client is told of") |
| `world/node_layout_data`, `world/node_scenery`, `world/node_origins`, `libs/aocnet` `repl/node_layout` | Nodes 1, stage 3a: the towns' scenery: the items of a node's `ANodeLayoutReplicator.LayoutAssetSetGuids` built from content.db (the layout's props and the pre-built plots' buildings, filtered by the asset definitions' level, culture, type, season and biome), the payload writer (the custom `NetDeltaSerialize` header on the fast array), one replicator actor per node awake within 1.45 km, a level change as a new full payload (README, "The towns' scenery") |
| `spawns/node_population`, `spawns/node_populations`, `world/roads` | Nodes 1, stage 3b: the towns' people: the layout and building population sets of a node at its level (placed at the record's frame and the plots' transforms, judged in the node's own spawn context, the siege tags any-of), the live manager that makes the registry match when a node's level changes (the same NPC stays, a gone point's NPC leaves, a new one arrives; respawns from the current selection; the ground's pending rules), and the roads by node level (README, "The towns' people and the roads by node level") |
| `session/gm_travel`, `svc/world_admin`, `world/places` | The GM travel tools (teleport and run speed): the admin API (`WorldAdminService`, served on `AOC_WORLD_ADMIN_ADDR`, default `127.0.0.1:50056`, service token; every call is applied on the network thread through `AdminQueue`), the places (the city nodes that have a place on the Verra map, `login`, and `garden`, the centre of G1's GM garden, when `--gm-garden` placed one), `GmTravel` (the work, shared with the client's own GM RPCs behind `--gm-commands`), and `services\cmd\worldctl` as its command line (below) |
| `vendor/node_tax` | Plan E1 (fix 4): the tax table the vendor window waits for: retail's 78 tags (`node_tax_table.h`, generated from capture 1536), every tax 0%, for the node the client asked for (below) |
| `stats/` | The stat engine (plan B3; a library of its own, `worldd_stats`): the reader and interpreter of the compiled stat program (`stat_program.h`, `stat_engine.h`), the worn items' contribution (`gear.h`) and one player's stats as the client holds them (`character_stats.h`); [README](stats/README.md), and "Stats" below |

The wire formats live in libaocnet (`libs/aocnet/protocol/conn`): the handshake with Intrepid's
94-byte extension, the packet notify header, data-packet framing, and the NMT messages, each
tested against the retail client's captured packets.

## Handshake retries and the session cap

A client whose first round trip takes over a second retries its Initial and answers the newer
Challenge as well, so a second Response arrives from the same address. A newer Response replaces the
session (an older one is ignored); when it carries the same character id and join ticket, **its login goes
on instead of starting over**: a login still running is not submitted again, and one that succeeded is
applied to the new session, because the first redemption spent the ticket and the gateway would refuse a
second ("ticket not accepted"). A refused login and a session that is closing (kicked for another client's
login of the character) are not carried over: the new session asks again.

At most `--max-sessions` sessions exist at once (default 1024, 0 is no limit; closing ones count). Beyond
it an Initial gets no Challenge and a valid Response no Ack, and nothing is allocated; an address that
already has a session may still replace it. The refusals are logged as a warning, at most one line every
10 s.

Each established session's datagrams are rate limited (`EndpointConfig::max_session_bytes_per_second`, default 262,144, 0 is no limit;
a token bucket on the caller's clock that holds `session_burst_seconds`, 2, of them): a datagram that finds the bucket short is dropped
before anything reads it, so a flood costs a bucket check and not a parse. 30 retail captures show the client sending 1-3 KB a second
(5 KB in the busiest second), so the limit is far above any real client. The first drop of a session is a warning, the rest are counted
(`Endpoint::datagrams_dropped_for_rate`). Handshake datagrams are not counted: the cookie makes a client prove its address.

## Actor channels

Past the control channel, `net/connection` sends actors the way the original server does (rules and
evidence in `net/connection.h`, from `fixtures/replay_data.bin` and UE 5.6 `UChannel::SendBunch`). The
Connection owns every channel's reliable sequence: emitters hand it a **chain** and never pick a chSeq.

### Chains

`Connection::send_chain(Chain)` queues one logical bunch on an actor channel. A `Chain` is a channel, an
`open` flag, `reliable`, the `exports` (each a whole export-bunch payload: `bHasRepLayoutExport`, a count,
the objects) and the `payload` (SerializeNewActor and content blocks, or an RPC). The libaocnet replicator
produces exactly these (`Emission::exports` / `payload`). It is split into partial bunches:

- Every export bunch is a part of its own, in front of the content, and at most `kMaxSingleBunchBits`
  (7621) bits. A chain with exports is never one part, however small: ch 3 pkt#22 chSeq 954/955 is the
  PlayerController open (3545 bits of exports, then 1314 of content), and 956/957 the `ClientSetHUD` RPC
  (873 + 173).
- Content of at most 7621 bits goes out whole; more is cut into parts of exactly `kMaxPartialBunchBits`
  (7616, a whole number of bytes: the client requires it of every part but the last), the last part taking
  the rest. The GameState open, ch 6 pkt#28..#45, is 3473 bits of exports and 123,841 of content in
  16 x 7616 + 1985 (chSeq 954..971). 7621 is UE's `MAX_SINGLE_BUNCH_SIZE_BITS` on AoC's 1028-byte packets
  (derivation in `connection.h`); the capture has no chain between 7617 and 7621 bits (unproven there).
- With more than one part every part is partial: `bPartialInitial` on the first only, `bPartialFinal` on the
  last only, `bOpen` on the first only. AoC's third partial flag (`bPartialCustomExportsFinal`) is never set:
  it is 0 in all 452,536 bunches of the capture. Each part gets the channel's next chSeq (an unreliable chain
  none) and, on a reliable one, the channel name EName 102.
- A chain that cannot be sent is refused whole (`send_chain` returns 0, nothing queued): an export bunch over
  7621 bits, content over 524,288 bits (the client fails the connection on a bigger reassembled bunch), a
  reliable chain whose parts would take the channel past 256 unacknowledged bunches (UE's reliable buffer),
  or a channel in the wrong state (below). A chain of more than 256 parts is refused however empty the
  window is, so the emitter must keep its chains below it. `can_send_chain(chain)` asks the same without
  queueing.

`send_chain` is the commit point of an emission: the replicator advances its shadow before it, so a caller
emits on a copy of the shadow and keeps it only when the chain was accepted ("Atomic emission" in
`connection.h`, `libs/aocnet/README.md`).

### Open and close

The first chain on a channel has `open` set (reliable; `bOpen` on its first part); after it the channel is open
(`channel_open`) until `close_channel`. A chain on a channel that is not open, or an open chain on one that is,
is refused; channel 0 is the control channel and uses `send`.

`close_channel(channel, reason)` queues a reliable bunch of 0 bits with `bClose` and a close reason
(`CloseReason`: destroyed, dormancy, level unloaded, relevancy, tear-off; the capture's 195 closes are 179
dormancy, 15 relevancy and one destroyed). The channel is closed for sending at once, but **its index stays taken
until the packet carrying the close is acknowledged** (`channel_closing`), and an open chain on it is refused until
then. UE keeps a closing channel in its table the same way (`UChannel::Close`, `GetFreeChannelIndex`,
`ReceivedAcks`); reopening sooner is a race: if the close's packet is lost and the open arrives first, the resent
close runs `CleanUp` on the new channel and the actor never appears. A lost close is sent again with its old
chSeq. The channel's sequence goes on across a close: ch 21 opens with 954/955, closes with 956, opens again with
957.

### Ack events

The replicator needs to know which packets arrived (it exports an object's path again until the client
acknowledged it). `send_chain` returns a `ChainId`; `take_ack_events()` returns an `AckEvent` `{chain, channel,
part, export_part, delivered}` for each part when the packet that carried it is acked or lost. Parts count in
sending order: part `i` below `Chain::exports.size()` is `exports[i]`, the rest is the content. A reliable part
that was lost goes out again in a new packet (same chSeq) and its event follows when that one is acked; a lost
unreliable part is final. Take the events after every `receive()`; at most 65,536 are kept, the oldest dropped.
The caller marks the replicator's `ExportAcks` from them (`libs/aocnet/README.md`, "Caller contracts").

### Unreliable data

A full packet window (256 unacknowledged packets: a client that stopped acknowledging) holds everything
back. Reliable bunches wait, bounded by the channels' windows; unreliable ones would pile up without
bound. UE drops unreliable data it cannot send, and so does `flush()`: once it has sent what it can, at
most `kMaxQueuedUnreliable` (256) unreliable bunches stay queued and it drops the oldest first, a whole
unreliable chain at a time. Each dropped part is reported by `take_ack_events()` as lost (final, as for any
unreliable part), and reliable bunches are never dropped (`queued_unreliable()`, `unreliable_dropped()`).

### Receiving

`receive` returns the client's bunches in order, each once, with reliable partial bunches reassembled. It
walks a packet's bunches with `wire::walk_bunches_ex`, which is strict: a bunch header that reads past the end of
the packet, or data that overruns it, stops the walk. The bunches before the damage are handled, then the
connection `failed()` and the packet is not acknowledged (UE closes the connection on "Bunch header
overflowed"; the legacy `walk_bunches` still reads a cut-off header as an empty bunch and ignores how the walk
ended). At most 1024 bunches are walked per packet; a 1028-byte packet holds at most 293. The connection also
fails when the client sends more than 256 early reliable bunches over all channels, a partial bunch over 524,288
bits, or one of more than 256 parts; the endpoint then drops it.

## Native bootstrap (`bootstrap/`)

What worldd sends a joining player so that the client spawns it, possesses it and lets it walk (spec
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-native-world-state-design.md` section 4; the original server's sequence
and where each value comes from are in `docs/world-bootstrap-transcript.md`). It is a pure builder over the
libaocnet replicator: no sockets, no `Connection`, no database. The session code gives it a `PlayerSetup` and
sends what comes out.

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `player_setup.h` | `PlayerSetup`: the character (Guid words, name, race and gender as the client's enums, level, class record Guid, customization, spawn and yaw), the stats (`StatsSetup`: the Owner, OwnerProxy and EveryoneProxy item lists, the float array), the abilities, the extras of a level-1 character (weapon skill line, attunements, held items, appearance ids: the captured Bard's by default), the login identity (`?Name=` and unique id) and the session (`SessionGuids`, ServerId, world time, PlayerId). `mint_session_guids` takes the GUIDs from the per-ServerId allocator in the capture's order |
| `constants.h` | The export chains (ids, paths, flags, checksums), the four `CharacterLoadTracker` values, the `ClientSetTime` formula, the storage sizes, each with the packet it was seen in |
| `bootstrap.h` | `PlayerBootstrap`: `join_sequence()` returns the ordered `Send`s `{role, opens_channel, emission, reliable}`; every step is also a method; the replies E3 sends on request (`phase_in`, `combat_settings_echo`, `storage_payload`, `empty_group`, `default_action_bar`, `good_move_ack`) |
| `captured_*.h` | **Generated** by `python tools/capture/gen_all.py` from `fixtures/replay_data.bin`: the captured Bard's 442 stats and 57/58 abilities, the 429-byte level list, the GameState values, the captured character's appearance, the default action bar and the empty group |
| `captured_setup.h` | `captured_character_setup()`: the capture's own character (RandomChar, its Guid, GUIDs under ServerId 60) as a `PlayerSetup`, the golden tests' input; `captured_bard_level1_stats()` is the layout of the StatsComponent's arrays (the captured item ids, keys and order) that the engine's stats are put into, and the abilities stand in for the character service's until content.db has them |
| `character_setup.h` | Plan E1: `build_player_setup(svc::Character, ContentDb, LoginIdentity, SessionValues)` makes the `PlayerSetup` of a joining player from its record (below) |

The sequence (`join_sequence()`, the original's order): PlayerController open with its 7 properties and
`GlobalGMCommands`; `ClientSetHUD`; the six pkt#23 RPCs (voice, the captured level list, flush, bandwidth cap,
`ClientSetTime` from the world time); the GameState open; `CharacterLoadTracker` 1011...1; the pawn's open with
8 components and its four empty storage payloads; `ClientRestart`, camera mode and rotation; the
PlayerController's update (`Pawn`, `ControllersOriginalPawn`, `Name`, the attachment to `CollisionCylinder`,
tracker 0111...1); the pawn's second state (RemoteRole 2, Instigator, PlayerState, Controller, bHidden 1,
Alignment, Ability, Stats, the dynamic resource storage); the PlayerState open. The GameState comes in a
**mode**: `Tier1` (the begin-play flag, the game mode class, the clock), `Tier2` (also the 25 combat
settings, `GameTimeScalar`, `ServiceConnectionFlags`) or `Captured`, the original's whole open (its 244 estates,
46 nodes and 32 quests, GUID 4608), **an escape hatch for in-game debugging only**.

Using it from the session (plan E3):

```cpp
bootstrap::PlayerBootstrap pb(setup, bootstrap::GameStateMode::Tier2);
if (!pb.ok()) { /* pb.error(): which value the replicator refused */ }
auto seq = pb.join_sequence();                  // nullopt: nothing was built, shadows untouched
for (const bootstrap::Send& s : *seq) {         // channel of s.role; the first of each role has opens_channel
    chain = { .channel = ch, .open = s.opens_channel, .reliable = s.reliable(),
              .exports = s.emission.exports, .payload = s.emission.payload };
    id = connection.send_chain(chain);          // remember (id, s) for the acks
}
// each AckEvent {chain, part, export_part, delivered} of an export part: pb.mark_exported(s.emission.exported_by_bunch[part])
// a reply: auto cp = pb.checkpoint(); auto r = pb.phase_in(); ... send it; on a refused chain: pb.restore(cp)
```

Not in the bootstrap, as the spec says: the PlayerController's quest, FTUE and XP subobjects, world actors, the two unreliable load tracker values, per-connection replication
conditions (the union goes to the one client) and the GameState's clock ticks (the 120 s `ClientSetTime` refresh is
the session's since the E1 stage 3 fix: `PlayerBootstrap::set_time`). `test_worldd_bootstrap` compares the PlayerController open, `ClientSetHUD`, the pkt#23 RPCs, the
GameState's RepLayout block, the pawn's open and second state (pkt#78 and #107), the empty storage payloads,
the possession bunches, the PlayerState open, the phase-in and the move acknowledgements with the original's
bits, and decodes the whole sequence in every GameState mode.

## The player's real values (`bootstrap/character_setup.h`, `content/content_db.h`)

Plan E1 fills the `PlayerSetup` from what the platform knows: the character record (`svc::Character`, from the
character service), `content.db`, the login identity and the session's values. `build_player_setup` returns
`{setup, error, notes}`: with no setup the login is refused (`error` is one sentence for the log, the player's
text is `login_text::kLoginFailed`); `notes` are what to log on success (the default spawn used, a JSON member
not read, a rounding-ambiguous attribute, a class name that disagrees with the archetype). Nothing is guessed:
a value that cannot be proven is an error.

**content.db.** `content::ContentDb::open(path, error)` opens the SQLite file `contentc` writes
(`cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc` -> `dist\content\content.db`, about 165 MB, see `services/README.md`)
with `SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY`: a path that does not exist is an error and is never created, and nothing here writes.
It checks `meta` (schema version 1; `contentc.version` and `source.dbc.sha256` must be there and are logged) and
that the tables and columns it reads exist (`class`, `class_stat_base`, and since plan A7 contentc version 2's
`ability_runtime`, `effect_runtime`, `effect_stat_runtime` and `stamina_rule`: names from
`services/internal/content/schema.go`; a file built before that fails here and the message says to rebuild it with this
checkout's contentc). `class_for_name(name)` (and `class_for_archetype(id)`) give the class Guid and name,
`primary_attributes(class_guid, level)` the six attributes with contentc's `rounding_ambiguous` flag, and
`ability_rules()` the numbers of Sprint, Dodge and Rest (below). One
`ContentDb` may be shared by threads (SQLite's serialized mode, a statement per call). worldd links the vcpkg
`sqlite3` port (`vcpkg.json`; CMake: `find_package(unofficial-sqlite3 CONFIG REQUIRED)`, target
`unofficial::sqlite3::sqlite3`), so a first build after this change needs `scripts\build.ps1 -Configure`.

**The mapping.**

| `PlayerSetup` | From |
|---|---|
| `character_guid` | the record's id, 32 hex digits, as four big-endian words (`d8b2fe19...` is the capture's 3635609113, 2516274681, 2602449240, 1814768596) |
| `name`, `level` | the record's (level 1 to 25: the game's maximum, which the character service enforces; content.db's tables go to 50) |
| `race`, `gender` | the record's `race_id` and `gender_id` **as they are** (plan B1): they are the client's own enums, `ECharacterRace` 0 Kaelar, 1 Vaelune, 2 Vek, 3 Renkai, 4 Empyrean, 5 Pyrai, 6 Dunir, 7 Nikua and `ECharacterGender` 0 Male, 1 Female, which are also what the character-select protocol and the pawn carry; every race 0-7 joins. Tulnar (8) and any id past the enum are refused with `login_text::kRaceUnsupported` ("This race is not supported on this server yet."), a gender other than 0 or 1 with `kLoginFailed`. (Until plan B1 the character service stored the *class* as the "race" and worldd mapped the ids 2, 7 and 8 of a Bard, a Tank and a Mage: a Kaelar Tank could not join.) |
| `class_record_guid` | content.db's class row named by the record's `class_id` (`ECharacterClass`: 1 Ranger, 2 Bard, 3 Cleric, 4 Fighter, 5 Rogue, 6 Summoner, 7 Tank, 8 Mage; Bard -> 108640158570105). `class_id` 0 (a record the character service's migration could not derive a class for) is refused. A `class_name` or `archetype_id` that disagrees with the class is only a note in the log: the class id decides |
| `stats` | plan B3: the stats of the character's class at its level wearing its items, computed by the stat engine from content.db's stat program and the worn items' stat blocks ("Stats" below), laid into the StatsComponent's arrays by each stat's audience; the captured Bard's item ids, keys and order are kept for the stats it has (so the captured Bard in its kit gives pkt#107 bit for bit) and the stats a class has that the Bard does not take the next ids |
| `known_abilities`, `records_to_load` | the captured Bard's lists, whatever the class (spec section 5) |
| `customization` | `appearance_json` read by name (below) |
| `x, y, z, yaw_degrees` | the record's position (cm, degrees) or, with none saved, `kDefaultSpawn`: the captured character's spawn point and yaw |
| `items`, `held_items`, `appearance_ids` | the record's items (plan B2): each as the `FItemInstance` the wire carries (the record, quantity, quality, durability, instance guid and creation time, every other member at its default as the original server's starter items are) in its storage and slot; `HeldItems` (the 23 slots, array key 46 as the captured Bard's) and `AppearanceIDs` are what the worn items show (below). A record with no items has none of the three; a record whose items cannot be played (a slot outside its storage, two items in a slot, a guid that is not 32 hex digits ...) is refused with `kLoginFailed` and the reason in the log |
| login, session | as passed in |

**Customization.** The client's JSON (`FJsonObjectConverter` of `FCharacterCustomizationSaveData`; what the character
store keeps as `appearance`, a jsonb that reorders the keys) becomes `repl::CharacterCustomization`: lowerCamel keys
are the members' names; each of the 30 scalar members present becomes a delta (floats as float32 bits, ids from
decimal strings, `skinSet` an int32); `race` and `gender` go out as the Race and Gender bytes (the client has no
preset for `presetGuid` 0) and, as `class`, are cross-checked with the record, and a mismatch is an error; a non-zero
`presetGuid` is an error (whether the client has that preset, and so whether those two bytes are on the wire, is
not known; the character service stores a creator preset as 0, so a stored record has none); `sectionsValues` keys are the body section enum's names (None 0 ... cCount 29); a morph name is the key
with its first letter capitalised and a trailing `_N` split off as FName number N+1, which is how the capture's 83
morph names read (`Nose_Bridge_Hooked_0` is "Nose_Bridge_Hooked" and 1). `customization_to_json` is the inverse for a
wire value, used by the tests. Unproven: the spelling of the keys inside a `decalData` or `decalBlendGroups`
element (the exe's member names in lowerCamel; no JSON sample has a decal) and `presetGuid` other than 0.

## The player's session after Join (`session/`)

`NMT_Join` is the client saying its map has loaded. With `EndpointConfig::players` set (worldd's `main` does) the
Endpoint then starts a `PlayerSession` on the connection (`session/player_session.h` has the whole contract).

**Start.** The login identity is the NMT_Login's `?Name=` option and unique id (`ControlChannel::login_message()`: the
Login's second FString and, after it, the `FUniqueNetIdRepl` with flags 8 and the id string; a Login without them
falls back to the character's name, with a note in the log). `PlayerWorld::new_session` mints the GUIDs under
worldd's ServerId (`protocol/server_guid_allocator.h`, the GameState's once per run, the PlayerState, PlayerController,
pawn and its components per player), takes the world time from the Endpoint's clock (seconds since it started) and the
next PlayerId (1, 2, ...). `build_player_setup` (E1) makes the `PlayerSetup`; a character it refuses is told why in the text the refusal picks
(`login_text::kRaceUnsupported` for Tulnar or a race past the enum, `kLoginFailed` for anything else, a class content.db
lacks included) and the connection closes, the reason in the log. A
`PlayerBootstrap` (E2) builds the sequence in the `--gamestate-mode`.

**Channels.** The four actors take the lowest free actor channel indexes from 3 that are neither open nor closing on the
Connection: 3 PlayerController, 4 GameState, 5 pawn, 6 PlayerState on a fresh one (the original's were 3, 6, 19, 193).

**Sending.** The 15 chains of `join_sequence()` go through `send_chain`, in order, one at a time; a chain behind a full
channel window (256 unacknowledged reliable bunches) stays at the head of the queue and is tried again once an acknowledgement has
freed room on its channel, and nothing overtakes it. A chain refused with nothing in flight on its channel can never be sent: the join then fails and
the client is told `kLoginFailed`. Each `AckEvent` of an export part that was delivered marks the GUIDs of that export
bunch exported (`mark_exported`), so later chains name those objects without their paths.

**The client's requests** are read with `repl::read_client_bunch` on the class of the channel's actor and answered:

| Request | Reply |
|---|---|
| PlayerController `ServerAcknowledgePossession` | logged |
| `ServerRequestClientLoad` | the four storage payloads on the PlayerController (Backpack, Equipment, QuestStorage, resource, holding the character's items), `ClientUpdateGroup` (empty) and the default action bar (pkt#597-607) |
| `ServerUpdateAccountCombatSettings` | the nine `CharacterInGameSettings.CombatSettings` values of pkt#620 with the request's values, as a PlayerController update (`kCombatEchoPaths`; the other 41 values are not echoed: the class defaults are not known to worldd) |
| pawn `ServerClientIsReadyToPhaseIn` | the phase-in update (`bHidden` 0, `bIsPhasedOut` 0, `bClientIsReadyToPhaseIn` 1), once |
| `ServerRequestStorageData` | the named container's storage payload, with its items, on the pawn (pkt#773) |
| pawn `ServerItemUsed` | wears the item in a bag slot or takes off the item in an equipment slot (plan B2, below): the storage events and the gear's update in one packet, or, refused, the Backpack's and the Equipment's payloads |
| pawn `ServerActivateItem` | nothing: the retail client sends it with every `ServerItemUsed` of an item that can be worn, with the same four parameters, and the server answers the pair once |
| PlayerController `ServerRequestStorageEvent` | a stack moved or swapped inside the Backpack (plan B2, below) |
| `ServerMovePacked` | read whole and checked (plan A6, below): a plausible move gets `ClientMoveResponsePacked`, unreliable, with its TimeStamp; an implausible one gets `ClientAdjustPosition`. The old unpacked `ServerMove*` RPCs are neither checked nor acknowledged |
| AbilityComponent `ServerTriggerAbility` | Sprint, Dodge or Rest starts (plan A7, below): an update of the pawn's Stamina, active abilities, effects and cooldowns, reliable. Any other ability the character knows runs in the generic runtime (plan D2, "Abilities of every class"): its item, cost, cooldown and effects at their times. A refused one gets `ClientEndAbility(ClientInstanceId)` |
| AbilityComponent `ServerNotifyHeldAbilityCancelled` | releases a running Sprint or Rest (plan A7) |
| anything else | the RPC's name is logged once at debug, and ignored |

### Movement and saving (plan A6)

Spec section 10; the numbers and their evidence are in `session/move_checker.h`.

**Reading a move.** `libs/aocnet/protocol/repl/move_packed.h` reads the whole parameter, `FCharacterServerMovePackedBits`: the
"send" bit, `SerializeIntPacked(NumBits)` (at most 4,096, the exe's limit) and the container, which must end exactly where the
field says. The container is the new move, then (with a bit each) the pending move and the old move, then
`bDisableCombinedScopedMove`; a move is TimeStamp, Acceleration, Location, control rotation, compressed flags, movement base,
movement mode and four bits of AoC's own (the input direction). All 2,483 captured moves read and write back to the client's
own bits (`test_repl_move_packed`). Layout evidence, with the exe addresses, is in the header.

**Checking.** Each session has a `MoveChecker` with the last good position (location, yaw, movement mode, TimeStamp), which
starts at the spawn point. For each packet the old and pending moves are judged first, oldest first (those whose TimeStamp the
checker has seen are skipped: every packet repeats the last frame), then the new move, whose verdict is the reply. A move is
refused when a number is not finite, its location is outside the Verra box, or its step is further than the limits allow in the
time it claims (paced against the server's clock, below). A new move whose TimeStamp does not rise (a duplicate, a datagram
that arrived out of order; the client's clock reset is allowed: every 240 s it starts again at 0, a fall of more than 120 s) is dropped without a reply, as UE does: no
acknowledgement, no correction, not counted as refused (a debug line):

| Limit | Default | |
|---|---|---|
| `max_speed` | 2,135 cm/s | horizontal; 1.5 x the fastest step in the captures (the dodge, 1,423). `--max-move-speed` changes it |
| `dash_speed` | 3,355 cm/s | horizontal, while a Dodge is on (the Evasion and 0.3 s: `AbilityRuntime::dash_allowed`): 1.5 x 2,237, the Rogue's dash in retail capture 742 (the other 30 retail Dodges peak at 1,408-1,688). The larger of this and `max_speed` |
| `max_rise_speed`, `max_fall_speed` | 1,350, 6,000 cm/s | upward (1.5 x a jump's launch) and downward (1.5 x UE's terminal velocity) |
| `max_rise_burst` | 500 cm | the upward credit (the other directions hold `slack`): climbing onto a ledge in game lifted the character 112, 132 and 131 cm in three moves 15 ms apart, which the 50 cm credit refused as "rising too fast". Spent once, earned back from the allowance not used: a sustained climb is held to `max_rise_speed` |
| `slack` | 50 cm | a credit, not an allowance on every move: earned back from the allowance not used, so over time the limit is the speed |
| `max_time_step` | 5 s | the most time one move may claim |
| `pace_rate`, `pace_bank` | 1.25, 5 s | the time a move may claim is paced against the server's clock: a bank earns 1.25 s per second, holds at most 5 s and is spent by each accepted move's dt. Racing the TimeStamp, or 36 moves in one datagram, buys the bank once, then 1.25 x the speed limit over real time |

An accepted move becomes the last good position and gets `PlayerBootstrap::good_move_ack` (as in A5). A refused one changes
nothing and gets `PlayerBootstrap::correction`: `ClientAdjustPosition` (ACharacter field 29, unreliable) on the pawn's channel
with the refused move's TimeStamp, the last good position, zero velocity, no base and the last good movement mode (walking
when that was 0). At most one correction goes out per datagram, the latest refusal's (`end_of_datagram`; a move accepted
later in the same datagram cancels it: the client is where the server has it). The client
finds its saved move with that TimeStamp, takes the position and replays what followed, so its next move is measured from the
last good position. Nobody is kicked. A parameter that does not read gets neither reply (logged once, with the reason). The
first three refusals are logged at info (what was refused, why, the move's movement mode, the step and the allowance, where the player is put back),
the rest at debug, and a line at the 10th, 100th, 1,000th and 10,000th.

**Saving.** The session hands its last good position and yaw (the control rotation's, degrees) to the world's `PositionSink` when
it changed by more than a centimetre (or turned, at the end): every 60 s (`PlayerSession::tick(conn, now)`, from the first
tick after the join), and in `PlayerSession::finish`, which the Endpoint calls from `remove()` whatever ended the session (the
client quit, timed out or broke the protocol), when a second login of the character takes over, and from `finish_players()`
when worldd stops. `finish` compares with the record the session started from, not with the last hand-over: the saver may
have given up on that one (the service was down), so the end hands over the last good position again when it differs from the
record (or a hand-over was made before: the player may be back where it started). A session that never moved saves nothing. A
refused move is never saved. A second login of a character that is playing starts from the old session's last good position
(the record the login read is a minute old at best), and the old session's position is handed to the saver first. `CharacterSaver` is the sink worldd runs: `save()` only
locks a mutex; one worker thread calls `SaveCharacterState` (position only: the service leaves `last_region` and the level
unchanged) and, since plan B2, `SaveInventory` (below). The queue holds one entry per character (a newer position replaces the waiting one), at most 4,096 characters (the
oldest is dropped and logged beyond that); an `Unavailable` service is tried again (3 tries, 1 s and 2 s apart) and a refusal
or the third failure is logged and dropped. The save call has its own 2 s deadline (the other calls 5 s). When worldd stops,
`main` calls `finish_players`, then flushes the saver once, for at most 5 s (the log says how many positions were left); the
saver's destructor does not wait again, it abandons what is left and joins the worker, which finishes the call it is in (2 s
at most). Worst case from the signal to the exit: 0.5 s telling the clients + 5 s + 2 s = 7.5 s. The network thread only
queues, so it never waits for the service.

Not checked, and unproven: collision; a movement base and an old move (no
capture has either; read as the exe's code says); the limits for classes other than the Bard, mounts and speed buffs (the
captures are one Bard's; `--max-move-speed` raises the limit); `max_fall_speed` and the clock-reset gap come from UE's
defaults, not the exe. In game: walk, log out, log in at the same spot; with `--max-move-speed 300` walking snaps back
(not run: the client is the user's).

Replies are built when they are about to go out, after the join is on the wire: checkpoint, build, `can_send_chain`, send, or
restore the checkpoint and try again when the window has room (`PlayerBootstrap::checkpoint`/`restore`, the atomic emission contract). At
most 64 reply steps wait; a request that does not fit whole is ignored (and logged once). A bunch that does not read is logged
and ignored.

**Flags and environment** (flag first; the environment variable is the default):

| Flag | Environment | Default | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--content-db` | `AOC_CONTENT_DB` | `dist/content/content.db` | plan C1's `contentc` output (about 165 MB; `cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc`, inputs in `services/README.md` "Content"). Opened read-only at startup. Missing or invalid: worldd still starts, logs why and how to build it, and **refuses every login** with `login_text::kContentMissing` before the join ticket is redeemed |
| `--max-move-speed` | `AOC_MAX_MOVE_SPEED` | `2135` | cm/s, the fastest a player may move along the ground (plan A6, above): 1.5 x the fastest step the captures show. Lower it (say `300`) to see the corrections in game: walking snaps back |
| `--gamestate-mode` | `AOC_GAMESTATE_MODE` | `tier3` | `tier1`, `tier2`, `tier3` or `captured`, how much of the GameState a joining player gets (above). `tier2` is `tier1` plus the combat settings: in game (2026-10-01) tier 1 already starts the world, but the client logs "Failed to find effect record to display PVP opt-in protection" without them. `tier3` (Nodes 1, stage 2, the default) is `tier2` plus the node maps of retail's GameState and the node fields of OwnerInfo (see "The nodes the client is told of"). `captured` is an escape hatch for in-game debugging only |
| `--no-node-scenery` | `AOC_NO_NODE_SCENERY` | off | no town replicators (Nodes 1, stage 3a): the towns are empty holes, as before |
| `--node-origin` | `AOC_NODE_ORIGIN` | `captured` | `captured` (the open locations of the 13 nodes the retail captures show; the node record's location for the rest) or `record` (every node at its record's) |
| `--scenery-radius` | `AOC_SCENERY_RADIUS_M` | `1450` | metres: a town's replicator is sent to a player within this distance (retail opened it at 1,190 to 1,450 m) |
| `--no-node-people` | `AOC_NO_NODE_PEOPLE` | off | no town people (Nodes 1, stage 3b): the layouts' and buildings' populations are not spawned and a node's level changes none |
| `--roads-levels` | `AOC_ROADS_LEVELS` | `dist/content/roads_levels.tsv` | the roads of every node level with their owner zones (`contentc roads --levels`): used instead of `--roads` when the file exists, so that a road's tier follows its zone's node level |

The default path is relative to the working directory, like `--service-token-file`; `scripts\services.ps1 run -World worldd` runs
worldd from the repo root and passes `--content-db` explicitly (`AOC_CONTENT_DB` if set), and warns when the file is not there.

**What the log shows** after the lines of a login (below): `[session] cyb: joining as ServerId 60, PlayerId 1, GameState tier2;
channels PlayerController 3, GameState 4, pawn 5, PlayerState 6; at (...) cm`, the setup's notes (the default spawn used), one
`sent <step> on channel N (P parts)` line per chain of the join, the wait for a full window, possession acknowledged, the
phase-in, the first move acknowledged (once; later moves are not logged), each refused move (plan A6) and each save of a position
(`saving the last good position (...)`; the saver's own lines are `[saver]`) with its reason; the character's items (`items: 4 in the bag, 13 worn, 0 quest items`, or a warning when there are none), each
item request applied or refused (the first three at info, then debug) and each save of the inventory (`saving the inventory (17 items) (periodic save)`). What the client can
cause per call (a possession acknowledgement, a load, the combat settings, a reply's `sent` line) is at info the first three
times and at debug after. A client that asks faster than its budget (`PlayerSession::kLoadBurst` loads at once, one more every
`kLoadInterval`; storage payloads `kStorageBurst` and `kStorageInterval`) has the rest dropped, the first drop logged once. No join
ticket or token is logged.

Unproven until a run in game: that the client accepts the whole sequence from worldd (the builder's chains equal the original's
where the capture has them, `test_worldd_bootstrap`, and the session sends them as built, but nothing here ran against the game), that the join's burst (cyb's Tier 2 join is 29 packets, 25 KB, sent in one tick) is
not lost on the client's socket (the original sent its 64 packets, 51 KB, over 174 ms; `Connection` has no pacing yet), the
combat-settings echo's nine values, and that the client wants no more replies than these (the node and siege summaries and
the client-state notice are left out, as the spec says). The original also sent the default action bar before the pawn
(pkt#77); worldd sends it only as the reply to `ServerRequestClientLoad`.

### Abilities and stamina (plan A7)

Spec section 7 ("Settled numbers" there has the evidence for every figure here). The AbilityComponent's two client RPCs go to the
player's `AbilityRuntime` (`session/abilities.h`), pure logic on a clock the session gives it (seconds since the session's first
tick; the replicated times add the world time and the synchronized-clock offset). No new flag: every number is `content.db`'s
(`ContentDb::ability_rules()`, read once when `PlayerWorld` is made; rows this worldd cannot run, say a Sprint that is not held, make
the world not ready: logins are refused with the reason in the log, as for a missing content.db).

| Ability | What the runtime does |
|---|---|
| Sprint (held) | needs 1 Stamina; one update adds its ability item (MontageGroup "Sprint", the Sprint montage), its effect and **MoveSpeedMult 1.0 -> 1.2** (the retail server replicates it with the Sprint: the client moves at 600 cm/s times it, **720 cm/s** with no talents, against 600 running; retail's talented characters had 1.25 and 1.3); the first charge of 1.25 comes with the activation when the player moves, then 1.25 every 0.25 s while it moves (5 a second, 80 charges: a full bar lasts 20 s). It ends when Stamina falls below 1 in one update (items removed, MoveSpeedMult 1.0, the 1 s DisableRegen effect added), and `ClientEndAbility` follows 163 ms later (retail's). A cancel (`ServerNotifyHeldAbilityCancelled`) ends it without `ClientEndAbility` (the client ended its own), a Rest with one (no retail capture has a Rest: no instance may linger on the client). **A Dodge does not end it** (retail capture 745: the Sprint, MoveSpeedMult and the drain run on through a Dodge). Ending starts a 1 s regeneration pause: the DisableRegen effect item, removed when the pause is over |
| Dodge | costs 50 at once; one update with Stamina, the ability item (the montage and MeshYaw its trigger's AnimationIndex and InputVector name: 5 forward, 7 right, 8 back, 9 no input with MeshYaw 32768, 2 the Rogue's dash; any other index has no montage), the 0.65 s `Cooldowns` item (Charges 1, as retail's) and the 0.5 s Evasion effect; the Evasion ends in one update that adds the 1 s DisableRegen effect, so regeneration is off for 1.5 s; the ability and cooldown items go when the cooldown ends. Refused under 50 Stamina and in the cooldown (a Dodge at exactly 0.65 s after the last is allowed, one 1 ms sooner is not). A Dodge opens a window (the Evasion and 0.3 s) in which the A6 move checker allows the dash's speed |
| Rest (toggle, out of combat) | adds its ability item (with the montage's export); 2 s later the Rest effect and Health and Mana regeneration 0.6 -> 2.5; a cancel jumps the montage to its end section and 67 ms later removes the effect and puts the regeneration back; a second Rest replaces the first in one step |
| anything else | `ClientEndAbility(ClientInstanceId)` and nothing else; so is every refusal (Stamina, cooldown, combat) |

Stamina regenerates +5 every 1 s (the tick's phase is the session's clock), except while a Sprint is on and the player moves, the Evasion
is on, or a pause runs. The player is moving when the A6 move carries an input direction or goes faster than 30 cm/s
(`PlayerSession::kMovingSpeed`), and stops counting as moving 0.75 s after the last such move.

`ServerInstanceId` of an ability item is the server's own counter of the instances it started (0, 1, 2 ...; a refused trigger takes
none), as the retail server's is (a constant offset from the client's `ClientInstanceId` per capture, 393 of 414 captures), not the client's
id; `ClientEndAbility` and `ServerNotifyHeldAbilityCancelled` carry the client's, so the runtime knows both of every running instance.
The effect items' `StartTime` and a cooldown's start and end are on the synchronized clock of `ClientSetTime` (the retail captures confirm it: within
0.1 s in 90 captures).

Replication. `AbilityRuntime` says what changed as `bootstrap::AbilityChange` values. The session applies them to the pawn's state at once
(`PlayerBootstrap::apply_ability_change`: the Stamina and regeneration items of the three stat arrays, `CurrentEffects`,
`ReplicatedActiveAbilities`, `Cooldowns`; the key arithmetic is the original's, +1 per changed item and +2 for a removal) and sends one
reliable update when the connection has room (`ability_update`: the AbilityComponent's block, then the StatsComponent's, with the export of
a montage the client has not acknowledged), then the `ClientEndAbility` calls in order (reliable, after the update that removed the instance's
items). The update is built from the shadow, so changes that pile up behind a full window, or a stalled tick, are one chain later: the state is
the queue. Nothing goes out before the join's chains. A montage's export (id, name and package, found in the retail captures: the Sprint's, the
Dodge's five and the Rest's) rides in the AbilityComponent's block of every update that names it until the client acknowledged it. A Sprint that
runs out of Stamina sends the update first (the items, MoveSpeedMult 1.0, the DisableRegen effect: retail's one packet) and `ClientEndAbility`
163 ms later; a Sprint started with 1.25 Stamina is that whole story in one chain. A trigger takes a token of `PlayerSession::kAbilityBurst` (8) refilled every
`kAbilityInterval` (100 ms); the rest are dropped and counted. A trigger whose parameters do not read whole is answered from its first two parameters when it is not Sprint, Dodge or Rest, and ignored when it is
(since plan B2 the whole `FItemInstance` is read, so a trigger that names an item, a mount or a consumable, decodes like any other: its slot and location are in `TriggerAbility`; worldd runs no ability that needs the item). The log shows each
start and end at info (`[session] cyb: the Sprint ends: out of Stamina (Stamina 0.00)`, then `the client is told the Sprint is over ... ClientEndAbility 0`)
for the first 100 transitions of a session (`PlayerSession::kLoggedAbilityTransitions`, then debug), the first three refusals at info and the
rest at debug, and each Stamina step at debug. The ground-speed line (`ground speed while sprinting 720 cm/s ... while running 600 cm/s`) is logged
when a Sprint ends, the first three at info: it shows what the client really moves at. An update that does not build is a warning (the first three,
then debug; the next change sends it again), and when the pawn does not take a change the runtime made, abilities are off for the session
and a warning says how many ability items, effects and cooldowns the pawn still lists: the client keeps them (a Sprint's MoveSpeedMult stays until
it logs in again).

Talents (milestone A: none purchased, 1.2 while sprinting; since plan D2 the speed is the General_Sprint formula run for the ranks held: 1.3 at level 25). The retail test characters had two of the three sprint
skills (1.3, 780 cm/s) or one (1.25, 750): the content formula is 1 + 0.2 + 0.05 per purchased skill, and the Escape Velocity stacks add 0.01 each;
a later milestone. Replicated since the review of D2: the StaminaRegeneration item (Owner array, item 172: 0 while the Sprint runs and the player moves, during the Evasion and the
1 s pause, the engine's rate otherwise: `AbilityRuntime::sync_regen`, in the packet of the step that changes it, as retail's). Not replicated: the Evasion's stat mods (PhysicalEvasion and MagicalEvasion +1.0, which retail sends in the OwnerProxy array),
the Sprint's stationary effects
(`General_Sprint_NotMoving`), and everything a talent adds. A Sprint's ability item is removed when the Sprint ends; retail keeps it, updated with
the montage's end sections, until the next Sprint replaces it. Unproven until a run in game: that the client stops its Sprint on the removal and
`ClientEndAbility`, that it moves at 720 cm/s while sprinting, the 20 s by a stopwatch, the HUD, the cooldown item's use, and a client log with no
"Ending unsynced ability".

### Items and inventory (plan B2)

Spec section 3 "Items and inventory (B2)"; `items/inventory.h` has the rules and the retail evidence (about 100 equip and unequip captures).

**What the session plays.** The character's items (`GetCharacter` with `include_items`) become an `items::Inventory`: the Backpack (70 slots), the Equipment (83, the slot is the
client's `EEquipSlot`: 2 Chest, 15 Primary ...) and the Quest storage (100), one item per slot, one instance guid per item. The session owns it; the network thread changes it, the saver thread never touches it.

**The join.** In the retail login's order (`PlayerBootstrap::join_sequence`): the Backpack's `ClientReceiveStorageEvent` Adds on the PlayerController (at most 16 to a chain, one call per item, the character as the instigator), the
pawn's open, the Backpack's payload on the pawn, the Equipment's Adds and payload, the Quest storage's Adds and payload, the resource storage's payload, then the pawn's second state, which now carries `HeldItems` and `AppearanceIDs`.
`HeldItems` has 23 entries (ReplicationIDs 1-23, in the order of the keys 15-18, 26-39, 41, 75-78) whose value is the record worn in that slot, 0 for none; its array key starts at 46 as the captured Bard's. `AppearanceIDs` lists what
the worn armor, costume and cloak show, by ascending slot (1-9, 71, 79: rings, earrings, the necklace, the artisan clothes and tool belt never are), each as its item record with enchantment 0. The captured Bard's 13 starter items
give the captured `HeldItems` and `AppearanceIDs` bit for bit (the pkt#107 test is unchanged), and the login's and the Bard's storage payloads and events are rebuilt from their items to the same bits.

**Wearing and taking off** (`ServerItemUsed`: Storage 3 = the Backpack, 4 = the Equipment; Slot; ExpectedItem, the instance guid the client believes is there; RequestedEquipSlot, 0xFFFFFFFF in all 141 retail equips). The item must be where the client
says (the guid of that slot), and an equip needs a record content.db knows with a slot it can be worn in: the first of the record's `EquipSlots` that no worn item covers, else the first (a request that names a slot the record does not list is
refused). A two-handed item takes every slot of its list, and an item worn into a slot a two-hander covers, or a two-hander over anything in its slots, sends what is there to the bag. The answer is the retail server's, in one packet (the PlayerController's
chain, then the pawn's): an equip is `Remove` the item from its bag slot, for each item it displaces `Add` to the lowest free bag slot then `Remove` from its equipment slot, then `Add` to the equipment slot (two events for a plain equip, six
for a two-hander over a main hand and an off hand); an unequip is `Add` to the lowest free bag slot then `Remove` from the equipment slot. Every Add carries the character as the instigator and every Remove of an equip or an unequip the zero guid (both events of a move or a swap carry the character). When the Equipment changed,
the pawn's update marks the 23 `HeldItems` once for each change of the Equipment storage in the answer (the array key moves by 23 for a chest piece and 69 for the two-hander) and carries the `AppearanceIDs` that changed. **The stats it changes go in the same update** (plan B3: a piece of armor
changes the character's stats; "Stats" below). **There is no class rule**: the design data has none (every class's kit holds weapons of another class's line, and a retail level 20 character wears level 26 gear),
and the record's `bCanEquip` is not one either (the three starter armor pieces of every class have it 0 and are worn), so any character may wear anything content.db lists a slot for.

**Moving** (`ServerRequestStorageEvent` on the PlayerController): a `Move` of a whole stack inside the Backpack to an empty slot is `Remove`, `Add` (both with the character as the instigator); a `Swap` of two whole stacks is `Remove`, `Remove`, `Add`, `Add`. The event's item
must be the instance in its source slot, and a Swap's target guid and count must be the target slot's. Splitting, stacking, destroying (types Add, Remove and Fill), a move onto an occupied slot that is not a Swap, a partial stack, every storage but the Backpack, a container that is none of the
character's, two targets or none, and every slot outside 0-69 are refused.

**A refused request changes nothing**: the inventory, the saving flags and the pawn are as they were, and no event is sent. The client is corrected the way it was told at the join, the Backpack's and the Equipment's `ClientReceiveStoragePayload` on the pawn, so a client
that moved something the server did not follow sees the server's view. The reason is logged (the first three at info, then debug). Budgets, as A5's: a request takes a token of `PlayerSession::kItemBurst` (8) refilled every `kItemInterval` (100 ms), the rest are dropped and
counted (`requests_dropped`); corrections come under their own `kResyncBurst` (4 at once, then one every `kResyncInterval`, 2 s), so a client that is refused in a loop gets the payloads four times and then silence. A request that does not read is logged once and ignored.

**Saving.** An applied change marks the inventory dirty. The session hands the whole inventory to the `PositionSink` (the `CharacterSaver`: a mutex and a queue, never the network) at the 60 s tick when it is dirty, and in `finish` whatever ended the session, whenever it changed at all
this session (a periodic save may have been lost, and the latest save wins, so one more is harmless); a session that never changed an item saves none. A second login of a character that is playing starts from the old session's inventory and the old session's is handed to the saver first; when that inventory differs from the record the login read, the new session is marked as having changed it, so its end saves the inventory once more even if the player touches nothing (the old session's final save may have been lost, and the latest save wins).
The saver keeps one entry per character with at most one position and one inventory in it (a newer one replaces the waiting one), calls `SaveInventory` after `SaveCharacterState`, retries `Unavailable` (3 tries) for each part on its own count and gives up on a refusal (logged); the
2 s deadline and the 5 s shutdown flush cover both calls. The character service replaces the character's items in one transaction (`services/README.md`, "Items and inventory").

**content.db.** The equip facts come from `item` and `item_equip_slot` (contentc version 3; version 4 is the oldest `ContentDb::open` accepts since plan B3, "Stats" below), and `ContentDb::open` checks at startup that both tables are there. A `content.db` built before B2 has none, so it does not open: worldd still starts, but **it refuses every login**, exactly as with a missing file (`login_text::kContentMissing`; the log says `content.db ... did not open: ... does not have the tables this worldd reads`, with the command that rebuilds it). **After pulling B2, rebuild `content.db` (contentc version 3: `cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc`) and restart worldd**; the character service needs the same file (`services/README.md`, "Items and inventory"). A file that opens but does not list an item record is another case: an equip of that record is refused and the item stays where it is (`test_worldd_session`, "a content.db built before B2", pins the refusal of a file without the tables).

Not proven until a run in game: that the client accepts the Backpack's Adds before the pawn exists (retail sends them there; the order is retail's), the `AppearanceIDs` array delta (the retail answers carry it; sparse element deltas), that the UI sends `ServerItemUsed` for the starter armor
(whose `bCanEquip` is 0 in the design data), and the look changing when the chest piece comes off (the visible mesh follows `AppearanceIDs` in the retail captures).

### Stats (plan B3)

Spec section 4. The stat formulas of the game are a graph (`services/internal/statprog`): four initializer lists (CombatStats, Base_Artisan_Stats, Player_Base, Player_Base_<class>) and the `Base`, `ValueMin`, `ValueMax` and `RegenRate`
expressions of the 466 stats of a class, the shared equations and formulas, and the equipment's contribution. contentc compiles it into the stat program of `content.db`; worldd parses and verifies it and runs it.

**At start.** `PlayerWorld` reads the `stat_program` row, `stats::parse_bundle` verifies it (every opcode and operand, every jump, the stack, the evaluation order, the crc: nothing a damaged file holds can run), and every class is run bare as a check: it
must run without a fault and give the stats the ability runtime needs (Stamina, MaxStamina, the regeneration and MoveSpeedMult its effects raise and the maxima they scale from). A `content.db` built by a contentc before version 4, with no
stat program, or with a damaged or foreign one, does not make a ready world: **worldd refuses every login** (`login_text::kContentMissing`, the log says why and how to rebuild: `cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc`), as for a missing file. The
character service refuses the same files (`services/README.md`, "Stats (plan B3)"). **After pulling B3 rebuild `content.db` (contentc version 4) and restart both services.**

**At the join.** `build_player_setup` makes the character's `stats::CharacterStats` (class from the record's `class_id`, level, and the Equipment's items: record, Quality, EEquipSlot) and lays its stats into the StatsComponent's arrays by audience:
mask 1 into `StatRepInt32Owner` (the fast array's field 3), 5 `StatRepInt32OwnerProxy` (5), 7 `StatRepInt32EveryoneProxy` (6); mask 6 (what other players' clients get) and 0 are not sent to the owner; TempHealth, the one `Type_FloatArray`
stat, goes to the float array; the four fishing stats that replicate base and equipment carry both. The item ids, the arrays' keys and the order are the captured Bard's for the stats it has (the captured keys are below its highest ids: they stay as
they are); a class's own resource stat (one Owner stat each, the Cleric two and the Mage none) takes the next id after the highest. So the captured Bard in its 13 starter items gives the captured `StatsComponent` bit for bit
(`test_worldd_character_setup`: `diff_setups` against `captured_character_setup()` finds only the toast flag, 442 stats and the held items included), each class's level-1 kit gives its own stats, and with `AOC_CONTENT_DB` the C++ gear rules equal
Go's on the real file for the captured Bard, the retail Ranger and the sixteen kit cases.

**The model** (the research's, proven on the captured Bard: 442 of 442 stats equal as float32, and on a retail Ranger of level 20 with its 29 items: 376 of 442 before effects). A stat's initial value runs the four lists in order; its value is that plus its
`Base`, plus what the equipment adds (the items' stat blocks, interpolated at their Quality and rounded half up, and the sets' bonuses), raised to `ValueMin` and lowered to `ValueMax`; the stats that spawn at their maximum (Health, Mana, Stamina,
a class's resource) are their maximum. Everything the engine stores is a float32. Effects (talents, food, guild, node), consumables and professions are part 2: the functions that read them give their "none" value, and the numbers they would add are not
in the stats. The stats are therefore exact for class, level and gear, as the spec says, and no more.

**On an equip or unequip.** `apply_outcome` recomputes the stats for the Equipment as it is after the change (`CharacterStats::update`) and applies what changed to the pawn at once; the pawn's update (HeldItems, AppearanceIDs, and now the
StatsComponent's deltas) is built when it is sent, so the deltas leave in the one chain the gear update is: only the stats whose float bits changed (and base and equipment for the fishing stats), each as a delta of the array it is in. A change of
gear that moves no stat (a costume, a bag move) sends no stat. The current Health and Mana (every stat that spawns at its maximum) are the session's: a new maximum below the current value clamps it (and the clamp is a delta), above it the current
value stays (gear does not heal). Not one packet: the equip's PlayerController events and the pawn's update are two chains of one flush; with the stats they no longer fit one 1,028-byte packet (the starter chest's 2 stats make the update 4,045 bits
beside the events' 3,930), and retail's chest piece changes 46 stats, which no one packet holds either (retail 1219 and 1253: 30 Owner, 14 OwnerProxy, 2 EveryoneProxy, the same both ways).

**The ability runtime.** A7's `AbilityRuntime` holds MaxStamina, the regeneration and MoveSpeedMult that its effects raise, and the maxima those scale from. It starts at the session's first tick from the pawn's values as they are then (not the
join's), and a gear change of such a stat goes through it (`AbilityRuntime::tracks`, `gear_stat`): an effect that is raising the stat keeps its raise on top of the new base (a Rest's regeneration, a Sprint's speed) and ends on the new base, and a MaxStamina
below the Stamina clamps the Stamina (a change in the same update). Stamina itself is the runtime's own: the gear never sets it. A7's numbers and tests are unchanged (MaxStamina and the rest come from the engine now: 100 for every class at level 1).

**Against retail's equip deltas** (capture 1219, a chest piece of Quality 6 on a character with 18 other items of unknown qualities; the levels, classes and the others' qualities are not in the capture, so no value is compared): the engine
changes 22 Owner, 9 OwnerProxy and 2 EveryoneProxy stats for every class, 32 of them among retail's 46; the 33rd is Health, the current value, which the session does not send. The 14 stats retail moves and the engine does not are all Mentality and what
follows from it (MaxMana, ManaRegeneration and its rating, five magical ratings and their five percentages): an effect, part 2 (`services/internal/statprog/README.md`, "Retail equip deltas").

**Unproven until a run in game:** the half-up rounding of an item's interpolated value (the best fit on one retail character), that the client takes the stat deltas in the same update as HeldItems (retail's are in the same tick), the fast arrays' keys with a
class's own resource item added after the captured ids, and the character sheet's numbers for the classes other than the Bard (the engine is the Go engine's, proven against the capture of a Bard and a Ranger).

### Item use and leveling (plan D1, stage 1; an uncommitted draft)

The part-2 spec section 2 and its "Settled in D1"; `session/item_use.h`, `session/leveling.h` and `protocol/repl/leveling.h` have the rules and the evidence.

**Using an item.** A `ServerItemUsed` on a bag item that `content.db` lists no slot for is a use, not an equip: the item's `ItemActivationArchetype` (contentc version 5's
`item_use`) says what it does, and its `ActionResponse`s (`item_action`, `action_response`) are run in order. Stage 1 runs the two types the GM items use: **GiveItem** grants
its reward tables (`reward_table`, `reward_item`, `reward_xp`): every item reward as fresh instances into the lowest free bag slots (a quantity split into stacks of the record's
`MaxStackSize`; the record's default quality and durability), every Adventuring XP reward that contentc resolved to the level scrolls' formula as XP; **SetPrimaryArchetype**
makes the character the action's class. A use consumes one of the stack when the archetype says so (`ConsumeOnUse`: a Remove of the stack, then an Add with one less when
some is left; the GM scrolls are single items). Refused, changing nothing, and answered like a refused equip (the Backpack's and the Equipment's payloads under the resync
budget): an item that is not where the client says, an item that is neither worn nor used, an activation other than `ActivateActionResponses`, a response type stage 1 does
not run (a Callout, a quest grant ...), a reward table that does not grant always and select all, a table whose predicate (`GetPlayerLevel() < N`) the level fails (the
level-10 scroll at level 12: the scroll stays), a bag with too few free slots for the grants, one's own class scroll. A Weapon XP reward (the level scrolls carry one for the
weapon line) and an XP expression that is not the formula are notes in the log, and the rest of the use goes on. A use takes a token of the item budget (`kItemBurst`, 8,
then ten a second), as an equip does.

**The XP.** The level scrolls' `ExperienceValue` is `(curve(target) - curve(level) + GetXPDebtRatio * GetPlayerXPForCurrentLevel) / max(0.001, 1 + AdventuringXPAddMult) + 10`
(contentc checked it with aocexpr over the real curve and wrote its parameters): worldd reads `GetPlayerXPForCurrentLevel` as the XP span of the current level and the ratio
as debt / span, so the term is the debt, which the grant pays first. The XP total is the Adventuring item of the XPTracker's `ExperienceFastMap`, absolute as retail
replicates it, with the `ExperienceDebtFastMap` item; the level is the highest level of the curve (`leveling_type`, `leveling_curve`: 0 at 1, 300 at 2 ... 418,400 at 10,
14,295,400 at 25) the total reaches, held at the cap (`LevelCapOverride` 25), the total held one below level 26's threshold (16,563,399). The level-10 scroll at level 1 gives
418,410 XP; the level-25 scroll after it 13,877,010 more.

**The XPTracker** (`ULevelingComponent`, the PlayerController's stably named subobject `XPTracker`, checksum `0x037669d1`) is the join's 16th chain (its own chain, after the
PlayerState open, so that the pkt#103 golden holds; the original sent it in pkt#103): the Adventuring XP and debt items and, when the level has granted a rank, the
`SkillTreePurchasesFastMap` entries (stage 1: the Universal tree's; stage 2: every tree the character has ranks in). `SkillPointFastMap` and the certifications are not sent (never captured; the client derives its points). Every
change is a PlayerController update of the changed maps (`PlayerBootstrap::leveling_update`, the typed `LevelingState` of libaocnet keeping the keys).

**A level-up** sends, in this order and as close to one packet as the chains allow: the storage events of the use, the XPTracker update, `BroadcastPlayLevelUpEffects(Adventuring,
level)` on the pawn (unreliable, as its flags say), the pawn's update with `BaseCharacterInfo.Level`, the `TempHealth` float-array item marked (every captured level-up has it)
and the stat deltas of the new level (B3's equip-diff path: the engine runs at the new level with the gear and the granted ranks' constant stat mods, only the changed
stats go out; Health and Mana clamp to a new maximum and are not raised), with an infinite `CurrentEffects` item per granted passive (EffectId from 1,000,000, apart from
the ability runtime's counter), then `ClientUpdateSkillOnActionbar(skill, tree Universal, rank 0, bUseHint)` per granted skill, as retail's auto-grant of a weapon passive at
level 20 was sent. The granted ranks are the Universal tree's `SkillTreeNode`s with `bAutoGranted` and a `LevelRequirement` the new level reaches: Sprint
Training I (5), Endurance Training I (10), Guard Training I (15), Sprint Training II (20), Endurance Training II (30), Sprint Training III (35), Guard Training II (45) and
Endurance Training III (50) (stage 2 adds the class tree's); Endurance Training's effect has a constant `MaxStamina +10`
mod (`skill_rank_stat_mod`), which the stat engine takes beside the gear, so MaxStamina rises by 10 at level 10 (through the ability runtime, as a gear change of it does).
Sprint Training's effect has no constant mod on the sprint: its speed comes from the Sprint effect's formula reading `GetSkillPurchased`, which is plan D2's effect system;
the rank is recorded and listed, the sprint does not get faster yet. The `CalloutQueueReplication` field (the on-screen popups, undecoded) is not sent.

**A class change** (`SetPrimaryArchetype`) sets `CombatInfo.PrimaryClassId.Guid`, recomputes the stats for the new class and saves the class. In stage 1 the client's abilities, action
bar and known abilities stayed the captured Bard's whatever the class; stage 2 switches them live (below).

**Persistence.** The progress (level, class, the Adventuring XP and debt) goes to the character service at once (`SaveProgress`, through the saver between the position and
the inventory) and once more when the session ends if it changed at all. At the join the record's level and its Adventuring row make the progress (`character_experience`,
migration 0005; a record with no row, or one below its level's threshold, starts at the threshold); the ranks the level has granted and their mods are applied before the
stats are computed, so a relog keeps the level, the XP, the class and MaxStamina. A second login while playing re-reads the record (the save is immediate; the old
session's last save may still be in flight: not carried over as the inventory is).

**content.db.** contentc version 5 is the oldest file this worldd opens; `PlayerWorld` reads the Adventuring curve and the Universal tree's grants at start, and a file without
a usable curve is not ready (no login, the log says to rebuild). The character service refuses the same files.

**What the log shows:** `[session] cyb: level 1, Adventuring 0 XP (debt 0), class Bard` at the join; per use `used item 6064634615524884604: +418,410 XP; Adventuring 418410
XP (debt 0), level 1 -> 10, granted General_Stamina_Passive_Sprint_1, General_Stamina_Passive_StamBoost_1; 1 storage event(s); the Weapon experience reward ... is not
granted`, `the new level changes N stat(s)`, `saving the progress (level 10, class 2, Adventuring 418410 XP, debt 0) (a level-up)`; a refused use `ServerItemUsed (a use)
refused, nothing changed: <why>`.

**Modelled, not captured** (no GM item use and no Adventuring level-up exist in the retail captures): the formula's two functions, the debt paid first, the Remove/Add of
a shrinking stack, the TempHealth mark and the passives' effect items on an Adventuring level-up, the cap's XP clamp, the Universal list holding only auto-granted ranks,
the XPTracker as the join's own chain. Unproven until a run in game: that the client's XP bar and level follow the XPTracker and `Level`, that it places the granted skills
on `ClientUpdateSkillOnActionbar`, and that a class change without a relog does not confuse it (the report says to relog).

### Talents, the action bar, the class change and regeneration (plan D1, stage 2; an uncommitted draft)

The part-2 spec's "Settled in D1, stage 2"; `session/talents.h` and `session/action_bar.h` have the rules, the evidence and what is modelled; the retail research is in the
scratchpad's `research/stage2/evidence.md` (128 `ServerPurchaseSkillBuild` calls in 27 captures, the action bar payloads, the KnownAbilities lists).

**Talent slots.** A node of a tree that names a `SkillTreeSlot` instead of a skill (the Bard's songs and their AOE variants, its Instrument, its masteries; the picks of the Rogue, the Tank
and the weapon lines) is one `talents::Node` per option, all with the node's guid; each option's ranks are ranks of the tree (`Catalog::find`), so a request that names one is judged
like any rank (the node's level and points gates, the rank's cost). The character owns one option of a node at a time (a second is refused: "its slot already holds ..."; a refund and a
purchase in one request swaps), and a requirement that names a slot node is met by any option owned (the Bard's instrument songs need an Instrument). `test_worldd_talents` replays all 128
retail requests (411 entries, `retail_purchases.h`) against the character each one's own answer says the server had, and each is accepted. The refusal log names the entry's rank, tree
and LevelingType Guids ("entry 1 (rank Guid ..., tree Guid ..., LevelingType Guid ...) names a rank that is not in tree Bard").

**The data** (contentc version 5, still; `services/README.md`): `skill_tree`, `skill_node`, `skill_node_require`, `skill_node_exclusive`, `skill_rank`, `skill_rank_cost`,
`skill_point_type`, `leveling_point`, `item_weapon`, and `skill_rank_stat_mod` for every rank with an infinite effect. `PlayerWorld` reads them once (`ContentDb::talent_data`
into a `talents::Catalog`; a file without them does not open: rebuild with this checkout's contentc). A rank belongs to a tree by the request's tree Guid (never the Name: a
class tree id carries a plain string that differs between builds).

**The join** lists the character's trees in the XPTracker's `SkillTreePurchasesFastMap`: the class tree (the level's auto-granted ranks, then the bought ones in the order they were
bought), the five gathering trees' auto-granted ranks (Mining, Herbalism, Lumberjacking, Hunting, Fishing: nine ranks), the Universal tree, and the weapon trees with a bought rank.
Every tree is keyed by its Guid with the hardcoded name 0 (as the retail server's replies key it). **KnownAbilities** are no longer the captured Bard's: General_Unstuck, the
abilities of the listed ranks, the combo of the weapon in the Primary slot, then the static tail of 45 (the fishing abilities, Rest, the emotes, Dodge, Guard, Sprint ...); the
