records are the same with the combos of the other weapons the character carries after the combo. A level-1 Bard's lists are the captured 57 and 58 element for element (a test
holds it). The auto-granted ranks come by level (a level-0 node with the level-1 ones), then the node's place in the tree: a retail Ranger's Salvo, Hunt of the Raven, Mark Prey,
Camouflage, Thorns, Strider, Regeneration. The stats take the constant mods of every rank the character has (a weapon tree's only while its line is in hand).

**A purchase** (`ServerPurchaseSkillBuild`, on the pawn): the request is judged whole by `Book::plan`: every entry names a skill rank and a skill tree (their TypeIds), the rank is in
that tree, the tree is the character's own class tree, the Universal tree or a weapon tree, no rank twice in the call (at most 256 entries); refunds come first and must name a
bought rank (never an auto-granted one); each purchase must not be owned or auto-granted, needs the node's `LevelRequirement` (a weapon line is treated as being at the character's
level: weapon XP is not tracked), the node's `PointRequirement` in points spent in the tree, its `SkillsRequired` (any of them, or all when `bAllAreRequired`; a requirement that names
no skill is met), none of its mutually exclusive skills (either way round), the earlier rank of its skill, and the points its `SkillCost` lists (a class rank 1 Archetype, a
Universal rank 2 or 3 Stamina, a weapon rank 1 to 5 Weapon; a level gives 1 Archetype and 1 Stamina point from level 2, a weapon line 1 Weapon point from level 2, minus what the
bought ranks of the tree cost). The purchases of one request are judged as a set: a rank whose prerequisite is bought later in the same request waits for it (the client lists a
tree's nodes in the tree's order, and the Bard's instrument songs come before the Instrument they need), the tree's list keeps the request's order, and the request is refused only
when a rank is still not met once nothing more can be bought. After the refunds no rank may be left without its prerequisite or its earlier rank. A refused request changes nothing, is counted and logged, and
the tree's unchanged list is sent again (the XPTracker item marked dirty) so that the client's view is corrected, under the resync budget; retail has no refusal to copy. (What
the client's talent window shows between a click and the server's answer, such as "24/24 selected", is the client's own pending selection (no capture shows what it does on a refusal): the resend
corrects the list it holds, nothing more can be done from here. The log names the refused entry's rank, tree and LevelingType Guids.)
An applied request is answered in the retail order: the pawn's update (`KnownAbilities` and the records, a passive's `CurrentEffects` item or its removal, the stat deltas the ranks'
constant mods cause), the XPTracker's whole list of each tree touched, and `ClientUpdateSkillOnActionbar` for each bought rank whose node has `bAutoAddToHotbar` (none for a
refund). A bought ability is **appended** to the lists (the live order is not the join's); a refund removes its element (everything after it shifts and goes out from the first changed
index; a removal at the tail sends only the count). A passive's item lasts the effect record's own duration (empty: infinite, FLT_MAX): a marker effect (0.1 s of a weapon rank, 1 s of a
Universal rank) is removed by the server when it ends, and the item of a weapon rank of a line that is not in hand is not sent. A respec is the same call with every bought rank
refunded (free): the lists shrink, the effects go, the auto-granted ranks stay; a Universal or weapon tree's reply is the list alone. The ranks and the bar are saved at once
(`SaveProgress`, whole: `replace_skill_ranks`, `replace_action_bar`; migration 0006). A purchase takes a token of its own budget (`kTalentBurst` 8, then ten a second).

**A weapon swap** (`ServerSetUseRangedWeapon`): when a weapon is worn in the target slot (Ranged 17, or Primary 15), its combo leaves the old combo's place and becomes the last element of
`KnownAbilities` (and of the records), and `OwnerInfo.WeaponSkillLineId` follows. Equipping or taking off items changes the weapon line in hand but not the lists (retail changed only the
records; not done).

**The action bar** is stored per character (migration 0006) and sent once, whole, as one `ClientReceiveActionbarSlotPayload` when the client asks for its load. The client's
`ServerSetActionbarSlotUseData` is stored without a reply (a `UseType` of None clears the slot; a bank outside 0-19, a slot outside 0-15, a use type past 9, an instigator past 3, a rarity or combo button past 15 or a
bar of more than 256 slots is refused and counted; a skill the client puts elsewhere is moved, not copied). **Design choice, not retail:** a bought or granted skill with an ability is
also placed by worldd on the first empty slot of the class pages (banks 0-3, slots 0-11), so that it survives a relog; the client is told with the usual skill update, and places it
itself too (if the two differ, a relog shows the server's slot). A character with no stored bar gets the default: the weapon bank (Guard, Sprint, the weapon swap, Dodge: the
captured Bard's) and the class's auto-granted skills with the hotbar flag on the first slots of bank 0 (a level-1 Bard's is the captured five-slot bar, a test holds it).

**Bar animation probes (the D1 draft; the cause is not provable offline).** The client plays an animation after a purchase and sets its bar itself. Two things differ from retail and
are the server's to change: (1) a purchase's reply (the pawn's update, the XPTracker, the skill updates) goes out `PlayerSession::kPurchaseReplyDelay` = 84 ms after the request (retail's
median; 116 of 123 replies were one packet), not in the tick of the request; `AOC_PURCHASE_REPLY_DELAY_MS` (0-2000, read at the join; 0 = at once) changes it. (2) The bar payload lists
the weapon bank's slots 1, 2, 4, 3 three times (`Bar::wire_slots`, capture 873 line 131) so the client has nothing to normalise; the stored bar has each slot once. Every
`ServerSetActionbarSlotUseData` is logged at info with the slot the server's own placement would have used. The Sets a purchase makes the client send (one per bought active, in one
frame) have an allowance of their own (one per skill update sent, at most 64), so a build of 20-30 skills does not exhaust the bar budget (16, then 10 a second).

**A level** grants the class tree's auto-granted ranks as well as the Universal tree's: their abilities are appended to the lists, a passive gets its item (an infinite one or a
marker), a skill with the hotbar flag is placed on the bar and announced.

**A class change** (a GM class scroll) is live: the old class's abilities leave `KnownAbilities` and the records, its passives' items are removed, its skills leave the bar and its tree
leaves the XPTracker's map; the new class's tree is listed (its auto-granted ranks and anything bought earlier), the lists are the join's for the new class, its skills are placed on the
bar, and the whole bar goes out in one payload (the cleared slots as `UseType` None). The stats are the new class's and its own resource stat, which the arrays were not laid out
for, gets a fast-array item (an add on the owner, owner-proxy or everyone array by the stat's audience). Each class keeps its bought ranks under its own tree, so a Bard that
becomes a Tank and a Bard again finds its ranks. `ClientClearActionbarsForInstigators` is not used (no capture shows its shape). **What still needs a relog:** the client's own ability
book and talent UI may cache the old class until the next login (unproven: nothing of the kind is captured); a passive bought in an earlier session has no `CurrentEffects` item until it is
bought again (the stats do include it); a skill the client placed itself on another slot than the server's.

**Regeneration** (D1's basic tick; plan D2's ability pipeline has absorbed it: see "Abilities of every class", the clock is now the session's own): every 1.000 s Health and Mana each gain their regeneration stat
(`HealthRegeneration`, `ManaRegeneration`, as the pawn holds them: a Rest's boost counts), clamped at the maximum, as stat deltas; a tick makes up for at most five seconds. Stamina stays
the ability runtime's.

**What the log shows:** `ServerPurchaseSkillBuild: 1 rank(s) bought, 0 refunded; 0 effect item(s) added, 0 removed; KnownAbilities 59`; a refusal `ServerPurchaseSkillBuild (1 change(s)) refused, nothing
changed: the rank Bard_Music_Menacing costs 1 point(s) and the character has 0 left of that type`; `the client set action bar bank 0 slot 4 (use type 1): stored`; `the melee weapon set: combo ...
is the last known ability`; a class change `... the class is Tank now (its tree, abilities and bar are sent live)`.

**Modelled, not captured:** every refusal (retail has none), the gates' exact semantics (`PointRequirement` as the points spent in the tree before the rank; a weapon line at the character's
level), the weapon rank's item only for the line in hand (one capture each way), the placing of a bought skill on the bar, the clear payload of a class change, the live class change, the join
without effect items for ranks bought earlier, the regeneration tick. Unproven until a run in game: that the client's bar follows a clear payload, that its talent UI follows the lists
after a class change, and the order of the pieces when the reply is long (retail split replies of 19 or more ranks over packets in an order that is not fixed).

### Abilities of every class (plan D2, stage 1; an uncommitted draft)

The part-2 spec's section 3 and its "Settled in D2, stage 1"; `session/ability_pipeline.h` has the rules and the evidence (it is the contract), `session/ability_catalog.h` the data, `tests/test_worldd_ability_retail.cpp` the proof against retail.

**Two runtimes, one pawn.** Sprint, Dodge and Rest stay in plan A7's `AbilityRuntime` (their held and toggle mechanics, the Stamina pauses, the dash window and the Rest montage are not in any table; their numbers are data rows as before). Every other
ability the character knows runs in the `AbilityPipeline`, from `content.db`'s `ab_*` tables read once at start into an `abilities::Catalog` (`PlayerWorld::ability_catalog()`; a file without the tables, an old fixture, runs only A7's three and the log says why). The
two share the pawn's arrays (one counter for ServerInstanceIds and one for EffectIds, so no key is ever shared), Stamina (`spend_stamina`), the stats A7's effects raise (an engine-driven change of such a stat goes through `gear_stat`: Pep's +0.2 lands on top of the
Sprint's) and the session's clock. Their events are applied to the pawn in one stream.

**A trigger** (`ServerTriggerAbility` for an ability the pipeline handles) is validated, in this order: the ClientInstanceId was not seen lately (a replay is dropped with no answer: `ClientEndAbility` would end the real instance), the catalog has the ability and the
character knows it (`KnownAbilities`), it is no item's ability, no other cast is waiting for its cost (`Busy`), its shape is one stage 1 runs (a target is needed: the call must name one, which can only be the caster, and the ability must be applicable to an ally or the caster; a
held ability that is not A7's is refused), its activation requirement holds, its formulas compile, the cooldown group has a charge, and every cost is affordable (each stat cost, the effect costs: Fortissimo's five stacks of Resonance). Anything else is answered
`ClientEndAbility(ClientInstanceId)` and changes nothing; the refusal is logged (the first three at info) with its reason, and an unsupported shape or a formula that does not compile is named.

**An accepted cast** is up to four steps at their own times after the trigger (the montage's notifies; retail's numbers, `capture_ability_replies.h`):

| Step | When | What |
|---|---|---|
| item | 0.3 s (299-331 ms in the captures; none for an ability with no montage) | the ability item (ReplicatedActiveAbilities): the ability, a ServerInstanceId from the pawn's counter, the montage's static NetGUID by the call's AnimationIndex (`ab_montage.net_guid`), PlayRate 1.0, MontageGroup (DefaultGroup; Flourish EmoteGroup, the songs SongGroup), the caster as Targets when the call named one; the previous item of the pipeline goes in the same update |
| cost | 0.3 s, later for a long montage (Mesmerizing 1.011, Saga 0.906, Story_Joy 1.573, Anthem 1.904) | the Cooldowns item (`Charges` = charges left), the cost deltas (Mana is an absolute value: 3281.125 - 56.875), the effect costs, the class resource, the first overlap of the zones the ability spawns |
| hits | 35 ms after the cost for the ability's own hits (with it for Story_Joy and an ability with no montage; Serenade 307 ms), 340 ms after it for a projectile's hits | the effects of the hits that name the caster and whose requirement holds, with the record's duration, stacks and tick; a hit's stat mods on Health and Mana (a heal) at once, clamped at the maximum |

The numbers that are not data (the delays above, the montage groups, the 0.34 s of a projectile's flight) are in `ability_pipeline.cpp`'s tables with their evidence; the 11 abilities no capture shows use the defaults.

**Effects.** An instance has stacks, a start and an end. Stacking is the record's (`StackInPlace` adds up to its in-place limit: Resonance 10, Glee 1; `OverrideAlways` and `OverrideDuration` keep one instance; `StackSeperate` adds one up to the global limit) and so is the refresh (always:
the start is now; extend: the item shows the remaining time plus the full one, Glee refreshed 13.8 s in showed 28.789; never). An in-place change goes out as a change of the item (`AbilityChange::effects_changed`). Every change runs the stat engine with the effects as its effect state and sends only the stats whose
float bits changed: Mitigation x (1 - 0.03) under Glee, HealingMult x 1.05 under Note_Comedy, MoveSpeedMult + 0.2 under Pep. An effect ends at its end (its item goes; its Expire and End hits that reach the caster run) and ticks every TickTimer (its Tick hits that reach the caster: Glee heals
every 2 s; the first tick one period after the application when the record ignores the initial one).

**The class resource** (modelled: the game does it with a reaction, stored and inert here): a Bard that spends Mana gains a stack of Bard_Resonance per cast (30 s, at most 10, the stat is the stack count); the hits' own Resonance applications are left out so a cast never gains two.
**Glee:** Quick Wit (and Jest) fires a projectile whose hit heals the caster (`Bard_QuickWit_Apply`'s Health mod) and applies Glee, which ticks a heal every 2 s for 15 s; in game that is the "Glee does not heal" of the D1 test, fixed.

**Cooldowns** are a ledger per cooldown group (the shared tag, else the ability) with charges: a use takes a charge and starts one timer for the charge being recovered, which returns at its end and starts the next while any is missing; the Cooldowns item shows the charges and goes when all are back. The
pawn's array therefore always holds every ability on cooldown, a Dodge's update included: the client's predicted cooldowns are no longer dropped ("Dodge resets the cooldowns", fixed).

**Regeneration** (D1's tick, absorbed): every second of the pipeline's clock Health and Mana gain their regeneration stat as the pawn holds it (a Rest's boost counts), clamped at the maximum, at most five ticks after a stall; the stat engine gives it five times faster out of combat and a world with no NPCs is always out of combat.
**Stamina stays A7's tick** (its pauses are A7's effects: the Sprint while moving, the Evasion, the DisableRegen effect), with the rate the engine's `StaminaRegeneration` gives (5 with nothing stopping it, more under a buff) instead of content.db's constant: the evidence (Stamina gains its stat each second, 24 captures) is the same as Health's and Mana's, but moving the pauses into the engine's effect state would replace A7's tested mechanics for no change in numbers.
**The Sprint's speed** is its formula's: General_Sprint's mod is `0.2 + 0.05` for each Universal sprint rank held (`GetSkillPurchased`), run by the engine for the ranks the character has (`AbilityPipeline::sync_a7_values`, at the start and when a level, a purchase or a weapon changes them): **1.3 for a level-25 Bard** (retail's), 1.2 with none; A7 shows the value (`set_effect_value`).
The move checker has no separate sprint limit (the 2,135 cm/s limit is far above 780); the log's ground-speed line says what the client really moves at.
**Movement abilities** open a window after their trigger in which the A6 checker allows their speed (`MoveChecker::check_allowing`): Flourish's slide reached 3,659 cm/s in both captured casts (decaying to 0 in 0.6 s), so 1.5 x that for 1 s; an ability whose data has a `NetworkingMaxSpeed` (Blink 7,000, Dodge 2,500 ...) gets 1.5 x it for 1 s. The Dodge keeps A7's own window.

**Songs and the rest of stage 2.** An ability that lists an infinite ticking effect (the Bard's Music_ songs, Berserk, Whirlwind, Protect) is a loop whose effects start 2.3 s on and repeat: its item, cost and cooldown are run and nothing else. Refused and logged: an ability that needs an enemy target (Mock),
a held ability that is no toggle of A7's, an ability named with an item. Not done: hits on others, the party and enemies and their confirmations (`ClientHitConfirmationsReliable`: a self hit is never confirmed, 0 of 24), projectile flight and lingering zones that hit others (a zone's first overlap on the caster is applied with the cost, and its actor is replicated: "Lingering zones" below; the
client's LingerTick requests for the zone's ticks are honoured while the actor lives), the shield and TempHealth amounts, the hit-event heal formulas of anything but the caster's own hits, GetTarget for a target that is not the caster, channelled abilities. A Saga's six variants fire different hits at their montage's notifies (not in the data): all the self hits come at the first one, so a Saga
(needs three Notes of one theme, which stage 1 cannot gather: Story notes stay one stack each) is refused in game with its requirement false.

**What the log shows:** `the generic ability runtime is on (N abilities known, M in the tables)` at the first tick; per step `the item of Bard_Misc_Flourish`, `the cost, cooldown and class resource of ...`, `the hits of ...`, `the effect Bard_Glee ticks`, `Health and Mana regenerate` (debug); a refusal
`Bard_Misc_Flourish refused: out of charges (its cooldown is running), ClientEndAbility 12`; `the ability Bard_Music_Cheerful is a song or a toggle: ...` once.

**Modelled, not captured:** the Busy refusal, every refusal, the cost step's re-check (a cast that cannot pay when its cost comes is over: the item goes, ClientEndAbility), the shared groups' items, the timings of abilities no capture shows, the class resource rule, a song's item alone, the allowance of Blink and the like.
**Unproven until a run in game:** that the client accepts a montage export whose package id the server minted (the object ids are retail's, the package's are made from them: `bootstrap::montage_for(path, guid)`), the item/cost/effect packets' order in one update, that the client keeps its predicted cooldowns when the server's array holds them,
the slide's allowance on a real frame rate, and the heal's amount against the retail one (the retail Bard's gear and talents are not known: the amounts are the formulas' with the character's own stats).

### Hits, shields, songs and every class (plan D2, stage 2; an uncommitted draft)

The part-2 spec's "Settled in D2, stage 2"; `session/ability_pipeline.h` is the contract (the evidence is beside each rule), `tests/test_worldd_ability_all.cpp` the acceptance against 511 retail casts of every class.

**The floating numbers** ("Glee shows no number at full Health") come from `ClientHitConfirmationsReliable` (PlayerController): one `FHitInst` per hit with, per target, the stat mods `{guid, Value, EffectiveValue}` (`bootstrap::hit_confirmations`; `EffectiveValue` is the part that took effect: 0 at full Health, what fitted otherwise).
`HitComp = Instigator = Source` is the pawn for an ability's or an effect's hit, a projectile's hit has no Source; the prediction flags are (1,0) for an ability hit, (1,1) for a projectile's and an effect's tick, (0,0) for an effect's apply/expire/end and a zone's; the InstGuid counts from retail's base in steps of 65,537.
**Which hits are confirmed** is a statistical rule over the captures: a hit is confirmed iff it has stat mods, an `OnHitFXId` or `OnActivateFXId` (contentc exports it as `extra.HitFX`), or Party in its mask (also a Default_Self hit); an area hit that found nobody gets one with no target.
**Hits are request-driven.** Every `NET_ServerVerify` direct hit is a slot that is applied and confirmed 80 ms after the client's `RequestHitVerification_Server`, never before the cost step plus the hit's delay; `NET_ServerOnly` hits apply at their own time; there is no fallback deadline and the targeting guid is not checked. A request needs its own client id record; forged ones
(no such cast, a hit the ability has not, twice, after the slot is spent) are dropped and counted (`hit_budget_`: 40 per 25 ms).

**Shields.** A `TempHealth` mod on an effect is held by the effect instance; the stat's item of `StatRepFloatArrayEveryoneProxy` lists `{Guid A-D, Base, Value, EquipmentValue}` per shield; damage (`PlayerSession::take_damage`) takes from the oldest shield first, and a shield leaves with its effect. Shielding Dance's shield and its LingerTick are confirmed the way retail does.

**Songs, toggles, held abilities, ends.** A song (Single activation plus an infinite ticking effect) starts its effects 2.3 s after the press, its loop effect re-adds its siblings every tick (the heal ticks are confirmed, the amount follows the song's progress, elapsed / 15 s), a second press ends it, songs that share a tag replace each other. An ability's own infinite effects that no hit applies go on with the cast.
A held ability (`hold_animation`) ends 59 ms after the client's cancel, quietly (no `ClientEndAbility`); `ServerEndAbility(ServerInstanceId)` is answered 259 ms later with `ClientEndAbility` and the item's montage cleared. A hit's Apply-phase hits run on creation; its `EffectsToRemoveIds` and `MaxNumEffectsToRemove` remove effects before it applies (Flourish drops Pep).
Casts may overlap (16 at most; a cast that waited behind another and finds no charge left is over at its cost step); a Mend-style chain fires one projectile per cast, chosen by the charges used. An enemy-only ability with no target is cast at the air; a friendly-capable one with no target is refused (`NeedsTarget`).

**Class resources** are held `kStatResource` stats (the engine exposes limits and regeneration; `StatInputs::current` feeds the held value to `GetStat`; guids in `content::kResourceStats`: Tank_Courage, Cleric_DivinePower and its Gen, Fighter_Momentum, Ranger_Focus, Summoner_Essence): the pipeline's 1 s tick moves each by its regeneration, within its limits. Alternate cost groups are data (the ability's costs).
**The numbers that are not data** (when the item and the cost step come per ability) are `session/ability_timings.h`, generated by `tools/capture/gen_casts.py` from the captures (the middle of the range of the item and cost offsets, values over 2.5 s ignored); `set_data_timings` switches the table (the rig turns it off, the session has it on). Movement allowances are added for Disengage, Charge, Grapple, Transpose and Shadowstep (a modelled speed, 1.5 x, for 1 s).

**Acceptance.** `test_worldd_ability_all` replays each of 511 captured casts of the eight classes (the level from the Mana cost, talents from the cooldown and charges, resources from the capture) and compares the item, cost, cooldown, effects, confirmations, shields and ends with retail's reply; every difference is listed in `kKnown` with its reason and the list is exact (an unlisted difference fails). Accepted per class
(of the casts the captures show the character making; 499 of 511 after the review's fixes: the rank-typed `GetSkillPurchased` references): Bard 21/23, Cleric 133/138, Fighter 2/2, General 201/202, Mage 10/10, Ranger 56/56, Rogue 11/12, Summoner 3/3, Tank 62/65 (the 12 refused are listed with the reason: the state before the cast, mostly in capture 152, differs from retail's). Since the review the replay also holds the zone actors of the 4 dances to retail (open within 200 ms, close within 300 ms of retail's). The counts that follow come from the stage's last measure run (the final gate recounts them): 386 of the 511 match without a listed difference; the groups of the listed ones: a consumable's use goes through D1's item path (16), A7's rows (20), an enemy NPC or lingering zone (23), the state of a long mixed session (21), talent or gear modifiers (12), channelled re-triggers (12), a confirmation we send or retail did (14), a Resonance stack count (3), not resolved (13).
**Deferred to milestone E** (not built, counted by `gen_casts.py` as NPC: casts whose reply needs an enemy NPC, a lingering zone that hits others or a pet): Bard 3, Cleric 63, Fighter 32, General 1,881 (weapon combos and the like) + 341 other, Mage 25, Ranger 52, Rogue 20, Summoner 68, Tank 95.
**Not done:** Sprint, Dodge and Rest as generic data rows (A7's mechanics stay, their tests pass), `AnimExpression` montage selection, `GetEffect`, `return`, `%`, `GetActiveTarget`, `CalculateDamage` and the other NPC functions in expressions.
**Unproven until a run in game:** that the client draws the confirmations' numbers and the shield from the arrays as sent, the order of the confirmation and the ability update in one frame, the cost-step times of the abilities the captures do not show.

**The review's fixes (the in-game test of stage 2: "Sprint no longer consumes Stamina", "the dances' effects do not show", "Jest with two charges goes to 5 s instead of 8").**
- **Sprint and Stamina.** The server's drain was right in the logs (1.25 every 0.25 s while moving, no other writer); what differed from retail was the client's StaminaRegeneration item, which retail sets to 0.0 in the Sprint's first packet, at its end and at a Dodge, and back to 5.0 when the pause is over (capture 745; the same in capture 44 at a dance cast), and which stayed 5.0. A7 now keeps it: the item shows 0 while `regeneration_blocked(now)` and the engine's rate (`base_`, fed by `gear_stat` / `set_regen_rate` from the effect engine's value) otherwise; it follows the player's `set_moving` and the Sprint's charge ticks. Test: `test_d2_sprint_drain` (a moving Sprint of 5 s: Stamina falls in steps of 1.25, the item is 0, 5 when the player stands, 0 again, 0 through the pause, 5 after; a Dodge) and the A7 goldens now expect items 172 and 176 in the Sprint's and the Dodge's packets, as retail's. **Not proven in game** that this is what the bar shows: if the bar still does not move, hold a Sprint for 5 s and compare the Owner items of record 6064629575339934476 with capture 745 (108.75, 107.5, 106.25 ...).
- **Cooldown chains.** A group's Cooldowns item keeps the FIRST use's start through the chain and moves its end on by one cooldown at each returning charge: Jest's second use is start S, end S + 8, Charges 0 (as the first), the return at S + 8 is start S, end S + 16, Charges 1, and the last return deletes the item (retail captures 39 and 44; before, the return item's start moved to S + 8). `Group::chain_start` and `timers`. Test: `test_charge_chain` (Jest, Flourish) and `test_worldd_ability_retail`'s whole-capture comparison (`kChains` of `capture_ability_replies.h`: every Cooldowns item of the captures 39, 41 and 44 with its offsets from its chain's start and its deletions). **Not proven in game** that the "5 s" the user saw came from here: the first-use and second-use items already equalled retail's; a client that draws the ring from start and end would differ at the return. If the number is still 5 s, check Jest's charges on the client (`GetSkillPurchased` of the talent) and use it twice 3 s apart.
- **Lingering zones.** The dances' visual effect (the purple ring of Maddening Dance ...) is an `AAoCAbilityLingeringEffect` actor the server replicates; the animation, the cost and the effects were served, the actor was not. `ab_ability.extra.LingeringEffectsIds` (contentc) lists the zones an ability spawns with its cast (minus the ones a hit of it spawns: the Saga's); Mesmerizing's zone has no hit of its own, so this list is the only link. At the cast the pipeline makes a `Zone` that opens at retail's offset after the trigger (`zone_ms` of `ability_timings.h`: Maddening +362, Nimble +300, Mesmerizing +389, Shielding the middle of +112 and +462; else 35 ms after the item), ends at retail's dance length (`zone_close_ms` minus 0.7 s: Shielding 5,247, Maddening 5,383, Nimble 4,823, Mesmerizing 2,334 ms after the trigger; else the zone's life in the data, 30 s) and takes the infinite effects its overlap hits put on the caster with it (Shielding's Aura, which had no end: its item goes 0.7 s before the actor does, as retail's, whose Aura was deleted 702 ms before its actor's close); the actor starts its fade-out (`bFadingOut`) 60 ms after the effects go and its channel is closed with Destroyed 0.7 s after them. A cast's record takes the client's LingerTick requests while the zone lives (`max_requests`: the zone's life over its tick, plus 8).
  The session (`flush_zones`) mints the actor's GUIDs and the pawn's `CharacterMesh0` guid (one per session) from the world's allocator and, since plan E1, hands the actor to the actor system: `PlayerBootstrap::lingering_actor_of` builds it, it is added to the world's registry as an `Effect` owned by the player (never dormant; the pawn's mesh export rides with its open: `WorldActor::open_exports`), the `bFadingOut` update is a change of the registry's actor and the close is its removal (the view sends Destroyed); no other player's connection is sent it. `lingering_open` / `lingering_fade` are still the bootstrap's own (the golden test); the chain is the retail one (`LingeringSpec`: the generated `AAoCAbilityLingeringEffect` schema of `schemagen.py`, the archetype `Default__AoCAbilityLingeringEffect`, AttachParent and Instigator the pawn, AttachComponent its mesh, SourceRecordGuid the ability, LingeringEffectId the zone, two random FGuids, the subobjects LingeringEffectMovementComp {bIsActive}, AbilityComp and StatsComp). Test: `test_worldd_bootstrap` rebuilds the two retail opens of capture 44 (`capture_lingering.h`, `tools/capture/gen_lingering.py`) from the values they hold and compares export bunch and content bit for bit (2,513 + 2,044 bits and 1,441 + 2,044); `test_dance_zones`, `test_d2_dance_actor` and `test_worldd_ability_all`'s ZONE / ZCLOSE lines (`gen_casts.py`). **Not proven in game** that the client draws the effect from this actor (no capture shows the client's side); the location is the pawn's, the velocity is left out (retail sent the pawn's).
- **Ranks.** `GetSkillPurchased(tree, <SkillRank>)` (11 formulas: a talent's upgrade such as Mage_Passive_Blink_Unyielding) could never be true: the join held skill-typed references only. `Book::held_ranks()` also lists `{tree, {kRefTypeSkillRank, rank}}` for every listed rank; the Rig of the tests now builds its character through a `talents::Book` (held ranks and passives, as `build_player_setup`), so the acceptance runs the join's own rank set (`test_held_ranks`).

### The living area (plan E1, stage 1; an uncommitted draft)

The part-2 spec's "Settled in E1" (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-e1-living-area-design.md`) is the contract; this is what stage 1 built. A player now sees NPCs and resource nodes around the login spot, and a row of GM vendors in front of it. **Ground, walking and combat are stages 2 and 3**: every NPC stands where it spawned, at the height the data or the capture gave it, and does nothing; an NPC has a position, a state and an owner (`world::WorldActor`), which is the seam the next stages use.

**The area** (`spawns/area.h`): E1's was a circle around the login spot (the captured character's spawn point, `bootstrap::kDefaultSpawn`), 1 km; **since G1 stage 1 worldd's default is the whole Riverlands** (the G1 section below) and `--area-radius METRES` or `AOC_AREA_RADIUS_M` is the one place the shape is set (-1, the default: the Riverlands; N > 0: E1's circle of N metres, which every E1 test and number below still means; 0: no other actors). `PlayerWorld::load_area` reads it at start (about 100 ms: the population chain 53 ms, the actors 30) and logs what it made. On the real content.db: **914 NPCs from the data, 4 captured NPCs (8 more were the data's, seen twice), 28 resource nodes and 26 GM vendors: 972 actors**, 68 of them within 150 m of the login spot and 164 within 350 m.
- **Data** (`spawns/population.h`): content.db's chain (contentc version 7): a WorldSpawn places a PopulationSet at an origin and a yaw, the set's instances stand at offsets turned by that yaw (74,778 points in the world, 3,929 within a kilometre of the login spot); an instance draws a PopulationAsset from its AssetSets, an asset names the NPC's blueprint and the blueprint's archetype path is what the retail server names it by. A real spawner picks at random: here the pick is a hash of the point (instance, set, placement, key), so the same world is the same at every start, among the instance's assets whose archetype a retail capture opened. The level is drawn the same way from the instance's range. Of the 3,929 points **914 were spawned in stage 1; 2,867 were not, because no retail capture opened that blueprint** (the stage-2 fix below spawns them) (385 blueprints; the most points: Goblin_Gravehide 355, Bandit_Raider 145, Goblin_Seer_Gravehide 125, Bandit_Arsonist 105, Zombie_Disease 102): an archetype needs the export the client was sent (its NetGUID, path and checksum), which exists only in a capture, so those spots stay empty; 148 are inside a PopulationArea's circles (stage 2: the heights are the ground's). 0 are blacklisted.
- **Captured** (`spawns/captured_spawns.h`, `tools/capture/gen_area_spawns.py`): the opens of the town and node NPCs and the resource nodes the captures saw inside the area, whole (12 NPCs, 28 nodes; 8 NPCs were dropped for a data NPC of the same archetype within 3 m). A node is visible only: it has no interaction yet.
- **Templates**: 39 retail opens, one per archetype (the spawns add opens of their own); an `OpenTemplate` is a retail open as a value (every block read into an `ObjectState` by libaocnet's `block_reader.h`), and `instantiate` makes a `repl::Actor` from it with NetGUIDs worldd mints in the retail pattern (the actor, its AI controller object at +2, then the components in the order the open sent them) and the values that are an instance's own: position and rotation, Owner and Controller (the controller object), the timestamp, no Instigator, no attachment or based movement or root motion, no direction held, a new `CharacterGuid` (and the alignments that name the old one), the level, Health at its maximum, not dead, `PopInstanceId` and `PopAssetId`; a gatherable gets a new `GatherableGuid`. Everything else is the capture's: abilities, other stats, appearance, the interact component. `test_worldd_area` checks that an instance differs from its capture in nothing but those.

**The actor system.** `world::ActorRegistry` holds the actors (the key is the actor's NetGUID ObjectId, even), a grid of 100 m cells answers `query(x, y, radius)`, `find_by_component` finds an NPC by its InteractableComponent, and an actor carries two counters, `version` (every change) and `reliable_version` (a change that must not be lost; a change of position alone moves only `version`: the view sends it unreliably, as the retail server's 15 Hz ReplicatedMovement). `session/actor_view.h` is one connection's side, ticked with the session on every Endpoint tick and passing every 67 ms once the join and the phase-in are out (an NPC's open needs the client's world): the registry is asked for the actors within 360 m, and each lands in a ring with a 10 m hysteresis:

| ring | what the client is sent |
|---|---|
| awake, within 150 m (an actor may set its own radius: retail's large creatures 119 m) | the open with the actor's whole state on a channel of its own; then only the handles that changed, on an unreliable chain when only the position did |
| snapshot, to 350 m | the open, and the Dormancy close (reason 1) 134 ms later: the client keeps the actor as it was and is sent nothing more; it is opened again (the whole state, from an empty shadow) when the player comes within the awake ring, or as a new snapshot when its STATE changed (`reliable_version`: a death, a flag; not a move, which a walking NPC makes every 67 ms step: the E1 review's A2, which re-sent every dormant walker whole about five times a second) |
| out | nothing; an actor that was open is closed with Relevancy (3); one the client holds dormant stays (retail and UE do not destroy a dormant actor by distance) |
| removed from the registry | Destroyed (0) on its channel; a dormant one is opened and closed so that the client forgets it |

A player's mounts and effects are never dormant (awake or out). A channel is the lowest free index from 3 (the player's four actors', and none a lingering zone reserved, are skipped) and is held until its close is acknowledged (`Connection::channel_closing`: UE's GetFreeChannelIndex), so a re-open never races its own close. Every chain is built on a copy of the shadow and the shadow is kept only when the Connection accepted it (the replicator's atomic emission contract); exports are marked acknowledged from the connection's ack events, which the session forwards (`take_acks`). A pass opens at most 16 actors, nearest first (a teleport would otherwise open hundreds in one packet window); the rest follow in the next passes. `CloseReason::Disconnect = 5` is in `net/connection.h` for the stage that kicks.

**What the first in-game run of stage 1 changed.** (1) *The name.* A vendor's nameplate is `UNPCInfo.CharacterName` (every retail NPC open has it: "Siege Vendor", "Smithy Foreman"); the first draft wrote the label into `CreatureOwnerName`, a pet's owner, so every vendor kept the template's "Siege Vendor". It also kept the retail instance's `PopInstanceId` / `PopAssetId`, and the client applies the population records they name (`ANPCCharacter::ApplyPopulationRecordData`): the instance record `Node_Filler_Barracks_Vendor` has `DisplayNameOverrides` = "Siege Vendor", which would win over a replicated name. So a GM vendor now has CharacterName = its label ("GM Gear 0-9", "GM Mounts", ...) and both ids 0. The legend (label, arc and place, item count) is in worldd's log at start (`[area] GM vendor legend`): the inner arc holds the first 12 defs left to right, the outer arc the other 14. (2) *Foreign object references.* `UNPCSecondaryInfo.AppearanceComponent` named the retail server's NetGUID of the NPC's appearance component; every NPC with one now names the component this instance minted, and `AuthServerIDReplicated` is the class default 0 (the player's actors and 49 of the 51 retail NPC templates) instead of the mesh's server id. (3) *Pacing.* The world's opens were queued without limit behind and in front of the player's own unreliable chains (stat updates, move acknowledgements), which are dropped from the queue when the 256-packet window fills: the view starts nothing while more than 96 packets are unacknowledged or 128 bunches wait for a packet (`ActorView::Config`, `Stats::throttled`).

**What the second in-game run found** (E1 stage 1, fix 2). *The Stamina that stopped updating* was not lost packets (the pawn's chains are reliable; a soak with 10% loss in bursts of 20 keeps the client within a tick of the runtime) but the movement clock: the retail client starts the TimeStamp of its `ServerMovePacked` over at 0 every 240 s (captures 62, 68, 83, 85, 107, 109 have 239.98 / 240.00, then 0.02), the checker took a fall of the clock for a duplicate unless it was 2 hours (UE's 4-hour period, not AoC's), and from 4 minutes after the join every move was dropped unanswered: the client's saved moves piled up ("Hit limit of 96 saved moves", 4.2 minutes after each join in both sessions), the server never heard the player move again (the Sprint's drain, the regeneration pause, the saved position) and the HUD stood still until a relog. `MoveLimits::time_stamp_reset_gap` is now 120 s (half the period, UE's own threshold); the session logs the pawn's Stamina against the runtime's when it ends.

**Lost packets** (E1 stage 1, fix 2). A lost UNRELIABLE update leaves the client with a stale value: the shadow is what was last sent. The view enters every update chain in a libaocnet `SendLedger` (`protocol/repl/send_ledger.h`) and feeds it the Connection's `AckEvent`s (`ActorView::on_ack`): a chain with a lost part puts its handles and fast-array items back so the next update sends their CURRENT values, UE's NAK handling (a newer chain's entry, and anything acknowledged, is left alone). While an earlier reliable update is in flight, and until the actor's open is acknowledged (a client that lost the open drops an unreliable bunch for a channel it does not have), every update goes out reliably, behind it. A lost update of an actor that has since gone dormant makes the snapshot stale: the entry is opened again from the current state at the next pass. The pawn's, the PlayerController's and the PlayerState's updates are all reliable (a lost one is resent whole by the Connection), so the player's own actors need none of it. `test_worldd_actor_nak` plays the view on a real Connection and a fake client that drops chosen packets and acknowledgements (movement, stat items, reliable changes, the dormancy close, bounds, and a seeded soak of 150 runs at up to 45% loss in bursts of 10, both ways).

**The interaction gate** (E1 stage 1, fixes 2 and 3). The client sent no `ServerInteractWithInteractableComponent` for the GM vendors: it did not offer them. From the exe (`UInteractableComponent::CanInteractClient`, 0x1464bb7d0, which logs the conditions as "Can Client Interact:"; the cue built by the OnRep refresh 0x1464db870 / `FUN_1464d6250`; the server's `CanPlayerInteract`, 0x1464bc060), per input index 0..3, the client needs: the owner valid and the index to name an input with a definition (`InteractionInputData[i].PlayerInteractDefinition`, replicated, a record of the client's own design data: the gear vendor's open defines one input); `PreventsCapture` false (`InteractableType` and `CaptureProgressPercent`); the player alive; **Can Reach** (the definition's interaction point: the owner's root transform, its capsule or mesh; the radius of the owner's capsule, else the definition's distance, with `ComponentDefId`; squared distance and, for a character owner such as an NPC, the vertical gap; the definition's angle against the NPC's rotation; a line check); not on cooldown (`LastInteractionTimes[i]` plus the definition's cooldown against the client's synchronized time); **interaction permissions** (`CurrentInputStates`, "busy", the guild / party / raid / siege-team-only states, `InputEffectStatuses`); no channel time or the player standing still; `CanInputActivate[i]` true (a replicated array of bools, at +0x220 of the component; no captured NPC open sets it); and an optional gameplay tag on the player. The cue (the prompt) is built from the OnRep of `InteractionInputData` and of the two state maps for the local character, by the same conditions plus the definition's visibility predicate (design data).

*What a one-session experiment found* (fix 2; its four test vendors are gone): four vendors side by side, each opening the "GM Gear 0-9" window: A built as the GM vendors were, B on retail's Kaelar_Male_Merchant (no `UAoCNodePlotComponent`), C a verbatim copy of the captured General_Gear_Base_Vendor open (capture 758), D = A plus the per-player state below. **Only D worked** (the prompt and the window); A, B and C did not. So the node plot, the archetype and the population ids were not the cause: the client's gate needs `CanInputActivate` and the player's own CharacterGuid in the maps.

*What every GM vendor has now* (fix 3; `world/interaction_state.h`):
- **`CanInputActivate` true for the four inputs**, in the registry's actor (`spawns/area_loader.cpp`: `world::allow_inputs` on each GM vendor): the same for every player. No other NPC has it.
- **The player's CharacterGuid in `InteractVisibleStateMap` and `InteractGameplayStateMap`** (a `TMap<input index, FGuidToBoolWrapper>`, a fast array of items keyed by the index; only the inputs that have an interaction definition get an item), made **per connection**: `ActorView::Config::player_guid` is the session's CharacterGuid and the view draws every interactable actor (an NPC with an input that has a definition: the 26 vendors and 46 of the 178 other NPCs) from a copy with the guid added (`world::set_player`, deterministic, so the item ids are the same each time). The open carries it; a later update of the actor is an ordinary update of the open channel (only the changed handles, the NAK ledger, the right channel) and never repeats the entries. The registry's actor is never touched, so another connection never sees this player's guid and nothing needs taking off when the session ends: the view's entry goes with the connection (`test_worldd_area_session`: two views on one world each list only their own player; the registry lists nobody).
- **What retail shows**: the captured vendors that were used list the CharacterGuids of the players near them (capture B listed other players' guids), so the client's maps hold several. worldd sends only the connection's own: the gate looks for the local character, and a player must not learn who else is online (modelled, not retail). The other interactable NPCs list the player too, but without `CanInputActivate` they offer nothing (their state is otherwise unchanged).
- Tests: `test_worldd_area` (all 26 vendors allowed, no other NPC, the maps made on a copy, idempotent, the player removed) and `test_worldd_area_session` (the GM vendor's open read back from the packets carries `CanInputActivate` x4 and the joining player's guid alone in both maps; an update does not repeat them; `ServerInteractWithInteractableComponent` then opens the window; a creature and the other NPCs are unchanged; after the session ends the registry still lists nobody).

**The GM vendor** (`vendor/gm_vendor.h`; the retail flow from captures 716, 863, 1531 and 1536, libaocnet `vendor_rpc.h`). The window the client shows is its own: `ClientOpenVendor(MerchSetId, Vendor)` names a `VendorDef` guid, and the client draws the tabs, listings and prices from its own copy of the design data (content.db's `vendor_def` and its children are the same rows). The game ships the public test realm's vendors in that data, the 26 `PTR_ONLY_Vendor_*` defs, whose listings are the GM and PTR items and have no offer (free): so there is not one window the server composes but **one NPC for each def**, named for its kind ("GM Gear 0-9", "GM Materials", "GM Recipes Crafting ANV" ...), the template of the archetype retail uses for its vendors (`General_Gear_Base_Vendor`), standing in two arcs in front of the login spot (12 at 8 m, 14 at 12.5 m, each facing it, 1.7 m or more apart). **Stock** is what a def lists (3,376 item records, hidden listings not counted); of the 477 item records content.db names `GM%` or `%PTR%`, 447 are in some stock and **30 are in none** (they are in the data for other uses, a scroll's reward say: no vendor sells them). **Flow**: `ServerInteractWithInteractableComponent` names the NPC's component; it must be an actor the client has open (the view's awake state), within 25 m of the last good position and a GM vendor; then `ClientOpenVendor` goes out and that NPC is the player's open vendor; then the window's own `ServerGetNodeTaxTable` is answered (next paragraph). `ServerHandleVendorPurchase` is judged whole against the open vendor: the cart names its def, every item is one of its listings, quantities are 1 to 100,000, at most 70 items, and the bag has a free slot for every stack (each item becomes stacks of the record's `MaxStackSize`, as a use's reward does); an applied purchase grants the stacks in the lowest free bag slots through B2's inventory (an `Add` storage event each, in chains of 16, the character as instigator) and the inventory is saved as any change; a refused one changes nothing and the client's storages are sent again (the refused-request path of B2). Nothing is charged: worldd has no currency, so no `ClientShowCurrencyCallout` (the price the client's window shows is its own table's, and the PTR defs list none); sales (`ServerHandleVendorTransaction`) are ignored: GM vendors buy nothing. Requests take the vendor budget (a burst of 8, then ten a second).

**Cost** (E1's circle; the region's numbers are in the G1 section below). A tick of a session with 1,500 NPCs around the player (`test_worldd_area_session`, 60 simulated seconds on the fake client): 100 to 190 us on average over runs, 5 to 7 ms at most (the first pass, which scans the 1,500 and opens 16); every actor of the 350 m ring (640 awake, 860 as snapshots) sent in 7.6 s; 2,223 KB in all (1.5 KB an open on average, 37 KB/s over the minute, nearly all of it in the first seconds); 640 channels in use, peak 672; a still world afterwards costs the acks and nothing else (3 s: 1 KB).

**The tax table: why the vendor windows were empty** (fix 4). Every window opened with 0 items, the placeholders "Sales Tax: [xxx%]" and "Current Funds: 88888888" and nothing else. The client's `UVendorMenu` (exe: `InitializeVendor` 0x1469e66f0, `HandleReceiveNodeTaxDetails` 0x1469e09f0) does not fill the window when `ClientOpenVendor` arrives: `InitializeVendor` sends `ServerGetNodeTaxTable(NodeGuid)`, the node being the vendor NPC's `UAoCNodePlotComponent.NodeRecordGuid`, and waits. Only `HandleReceiveNodeTaxDetails`, which the server's `ClientSetNodeTaxTable` calls, runs `PopulateList` (the listings), sets the two tax labels and calls `OnUpdateCurrency` (the funds). worldd ignored the request, so the window stayed at its design-time text. (The window fills at once only when the vendor object is not valid or the node is 0: the client then calls the handler with an empty table itself, which is why a vendor with no node, as capture 1531's, needs no answer.) Retail answers the next packet with the node's table: 78 entries, 6,817 bytes, in about eight packets (captures 716 and 1536). `session` now answers `ServerGetNodeTaxTable` with a window open on a GM vendor within range (`vendor/node_tax.h`: the same 78 tags, TaxDetails records, flags and owning-node percents as capture 1536's default node, every tax percent 0 and the flat tax 0, the table's NodeGuid the one asked for); at most three at once and one a second, nothing without a window. The PTR defs have no tax tag (`TaxTag` None), so the window finds no entry and shows 0% whatever the table holds. The funds are the player's own currency in the client's state (worldd keeps none: 0), unrelated to the price, which stays free. Every request is logged ("the window of ... asks for the taxes of node N"). Tests: `test_repl_vendor` (the two captured tables and requests read, typed and written back bit for bit; the codec's limits and refusals), `test_worldd_area_session` (the vendor flow open, tax table, purchase against the retail shapes: ClientOpenVendor 195 bits, the table 54,537 bits with retail's tags; no table without a window; the order; the budget; junk).

**Modelled, not retail.** Which asset a spawner draws and the levels (a hash, not the game's random); every NPC stands still where the data puts it; nodes are visible only; the GM NPCs' positions and names (the retail server has none); no price and no sale; the tax table's 0% (retail's default node taxes 50%); the interaction's range (25 m) and the vendor budget; the 16 opens per pass; the 10 m hysteresis; a lingering zone is sent to its owner only.
**Unproven until a run in game:** that the client shows the NPCs of a template once the guids, level and position are the instance's, that the GM NPCs (a vendor template with a different name) can be interacted with and open the PTR windows, that the client takes a `ClientOpenVendor` for a def whose NPC is not one the data gives that vendor, the heights (the login spot's for the GM NPCs, the data's elsewhere: ground is stage 2, so an NPC may float or sink), and the look of the dormant snapshots.

### The ground and the NPCs' walking (plan E1, stage 2; an uncommitted draft)

Stage 2 of the spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-e1-living-area-design.md` §3 and its "Settled in E1, stage 2") puts the NPCs on the ground and lets them walk. The ground is extracted from the game's own map offline (`tools/mapc`, its README has the how) into `dist/content/area_nav.bin`; worldd only reads it. **Combat is stage 3.**

**`nav/`.**
- `nav_file.h`: the file: the terrain (48 landscape components of 511 x 511 samples: the client's own 16-bit heights, 25 MB) and the tiled Detour navmesh (2,488 tiles, 330,849 polygons, 60 MB), with the area's centre and radius and the hash of the geometry it came from. It is read once at start (about 0.1 s) and Detour keeps the only copy of the tiles in memory (a tile whose header does not match its size is refused: Detour trusts it).
- `nav_build.h` / `navbuild_main.cpp`: the offline half: mapc's geometry file into the navmesh with Recast (tiles of 4,000 cm built on all cores, written in tile order: the bytes do not depend on the thread count). `aoc_navbuild` is the tool `mapc` runs. The settings are the game's own where `DefaultEngine.ini` says: agent radius 35 and height 144, slope 52, watershed regions; cells 25 x 10 cm; climb 45 cm (UE's step height and the move checker's ledge; the game's navmesh says 100).
- `area_nav.h`: the queries, in world cm, on the net thread (Detour's query object is not shared): `terrain_height` (exact), `ground_height(x, y, z_hint)` (a navmesh floor nearest the height asked, the terrain when that is the surface), `nearest_point`, `on_navmesh`, `find_path` (A*, partial when the end is not connected), `raycast` / `line_of_sight` along the walkable surface, `random_point`. Detour sees coordinates relative to the area's centre (the world's x is -777,664 cm: float precision).

**`npc/`.** One `NpcWorld` holds the motion of every NPC of the registry and steps it at 15 Hz (retail's server tick) from the endpoint's clock: `Endpoint::tick` calls `PlayerWorld::tick_world`, and each session tells where its player stands (`note_player`). It is a service stage 3's AI calls: `order_move` / `release` / `order_return_home` hand an NPC's feet to the caller, `mover()` is the walking itself (`mover.h`: a path, a speed, the ground under every step, a limited turn rate).
- **Standing.** `npc/area_npcs.h` runs at start (`PlayerWorld::load_ground`: the log's `[nav]` lines): every NPC (the data's, the captured and the GM vendors) is put on the nearest ground to the height it had (within 15 m): origin = ground + the capsule's half height times the actor's scale (`npc_motion.tsv`: a wolf 88 x 1.09, a player 98). On the real area: 944 NPCs, 929 found a ground, 15 did not and stand where they were; 191 changed height by more than a metre (the data's heights were approximate, and the GM vendors floated or sank); 794 stand on the terrain exactly, 150 on a floor (a step of the ruins). 37 NPCs have no line in the table (the NPCPawn defaults) and 217 are scaled.
- **Behaviours** (a PopulationInstance's `NpcAmbientBehavior`): *Stationary* stands (retail's do: below); *Wander* and *Default* (retail's Default instances wander in the captures) idle 4.5 s (1.5 to 9: the captures' idle gaps) and walk legs of the length retail's do (a median of 250 cm, 740 at the 90th percentile, log-normal, inside `MinRadius` .. `MaxRadius` of home: a leg that would leave the radius turns back); *Patrol* walks the route's waypoints with the route's pauses, round (a looping route) or there and back; a Patrol with no route, or with fewer than two waypoints, wanders. An NPC more than twice its radius from home walks back at a run. A wander or patrol target with no path is dropped and counted.
- **Speeds** (from the retail captures: `npc/retail_motion.h`, generated by `tools/capture/gen_npc_motion.py`): the median speed of the archetype's NPCs of Wander and Default instances where the captures show one (a hawk or a raven 315 cm/s, a deer 126, a wolf 378), unless it is above 55 % of the Blueprint's MaxWalkSpeed (a chase the idle instance was caught in, not a wander); else wander = MaxWalkSpeed x 0.27 (the captures' median ratio); patrol = the archetype's retail patrol speed, or MaxWalkSpeed x the instance's SpeedMultiplierExpression x 1.05 when it is a number (0.15 walks 94 to 95 = 0.15 x 600 x 1.05, "(0.2)" is 0.2), else x 0.4; going home = MaxRunSpeed x 0.5.
- **Replication.** A step moves the registry's actor (`Change::Movement`: the position alone, so the view sends it on an unreliable chain) and writes what the retail server writes: `ReplicatedMovement` (location at 0.01 cm, velocity in whole cm/s, yaw as one byte), its timestamp and `CurrentMovementInput` (1 while walking forward). One update chain is 216 bits (retail's: about 198). The open's own location follows, so a re-open starts where the NPC is.
- **Dormant NPCs stand still.** Only NPCs within 170 m (the relevancy's awake ring and a margin) of a player the sessions noted in the last two seconds are stepped; the others stay frozen mid-state where they are, and one that leaves every ring stops at once and writes a zero velocity. (Retail's snapshot ring is the same: the client shows the actor as it was.)
- **Without a ground** (the file is missing, broken, or cut for another area) worldd starts all the same: the log says `[nav] no ground, the NPCs stand where the data puts them and do not move: ...` and every NPC stays where stage 1 put it. A missing `npc_motion.tsv` only means the NPCPawn defaults (half height 88, speed 600). `--area-nav FILE` / `AOC_AREA_NAV`, `--npc-motion FILE` / `AOC_NPC_MOTION`, and `--no-npc-movement` (stage 1's behaviour).

**Tests.** `test_worldd_nav` (a small world built in memory: the build is the same bytes with one thread or four, the file's refusals, the ground under a hill, a wall and a floating platform, a path round the wall, a point in a closed pen, rays, hostile numbers; and on the real `area_nav.bin` the acceptance test below), `test_worldd_npc_move` (a simulated clock over that world: standing, dormant and awake, wander, determinism, patrol in a loop and there and back, return home, the AI's orders, the mover, the motion table, hostile inputs and the cost of 900 NPCs), `test_worldd_area` (the real area: the NPCs stand on the ground, a player at the login spot for two minutes, the same world gives the same walks) and `test_worldd_area_session` (300 wandering NPCs on the real ground through a session and a fake client: per-tick time and bandwidth).
- **The acceptance test.** `server/worldd/tests/ground_key.h` (generated by `tools/capture/gen_ground_key.py` from 54 captures) holds 376 places where the retail captures' characters and creatures stood within 1.1 km of the login spot: 286 positions of the capturing players' walking (their ServerMovePacked), 35 of other players, 55 of NPCs. Of the 335 that are on the ground (a player in a jump's arc, MovementMode 3, is not: 41) **334 lie on the heightfield or a floor of the navmesh: 99.7 %**, a player's origin 98 cm above its ground to 1.5 cm. The 245 positions of walking players are all on the navmesh within 80 cm; a path joins all 66 pairs of walked places (5 to 80 m apart, one capture) at 1.00 times the straight line, and all 43 pairs of NPC places. The one miss: a creature 787 cm under the terrain, in a cave under a landscape hole (holes are not read). Positions beyond 1.1 km (the first, unfiltered, key had 644) are outside what was extracted.
- **Cost.** 900 NPCs awake around a player: 0.6 ms per 15 Hz step on average (all walking: 4,316 legs in a minute); the real area with a player at the login spot: 30 us per step (76 NPCs within 170 m), 3.8 ms at most (the first paths); a session with 300 wandering NPCs around the player (130 awake, 84 walking): the session's tick 250 us on average, 445 kbit/s to the client (5.3 kbit/s on the wire for each walking NPC: retail's 15 updates of ~200 bits is 3 kbit/s of content, the rest is framing), every update unreliable.

**Modelled, not retail (stage 2).** The speeds' factors where no capture shows the archetype, the 4.5 s idle, Default = Wander, the there-and-back of a route that does not loop, the 45 cm climb, one agent for every archetype (radius 35: a big creature fits where it should not), going home at half the run speed, the NPCs' starting points in their walks (a hash of the id), the 170 m awake test (the registry's view of "near a player", not each connection's ring).
**Not done:** splines, water (the rivers' beds are ground), the landscape's holes (caves), doors; patrols of routes placed by a city node or an encounter; a route's NumberOfWaypoints; groups and formations; NPCs outside the extracted ground stand; a navmesh for the player's own movement checks (the spec leaves it to a later step).

### The chunked ground: the Riverlands' navmesh in a window (G1, stage 1, Part A; an uncommitted draft)

The ground of the whole Riverlands is 950 MB of navmesh (31,195 tiles) and 161 MB of terrain; Detour holds at most 4,096 tiles (`dtNavMesh::init` needs 10 bits of salt: 1,024 polygons a tile, 4,096 tiles), and the net thread must not wait for a disk. So `area_nav.bin` is **chunked** (`nav/nav_file.h`, version 2; `tools/mapc/README.md` has the build) and `nav::AreaNav` keeps a **window** of it.

- **The file.** Header, the region's 401 rectangles, the terrain directory and samples (resident from the start: `ground_height` and `terrain_height` never wait; a lookup by 25,600 cm cell finds the grid), the chunk directory (cx, cy, tile count, size, offset, an FNV-1a hash of the chunk) and the chunks' blobs (a chunk is 8 x 8 tiles of 4,000 cm = 320 m: up to 64 tiles, 1.3 MB on average, tile (tx, ty) is in chunk (floor(tx / 8), floor(ty / 8)); the grid is E1's, so E1's tiles are the same tiles). A version 1 file (E1's flat one) still loads, whole. A damaged chunk is refused by its hash, a tile Detour cannot read by its header's counts; the window then counts a failure and the chunk stays out.
- **The window** (`set_interest`, `pump`, `ready_at`, `load_around`). The owner says where its players stand: the chunks within **2** of any player's chunk (5 x 5, 1,600 tiles at most) are requested nearest first, a chunk stays until it is more than **3** away from every player (one chunk of hysteresis: a player on a border does not make the same chunk come and go), and the mesh never holds more than its **4,096 tiles**, resident or on the way (when the players' windows do not all fit, the room is shared by distance: a chunk nearer to a player than a resident one is to its own takes that one's place, so the chunk a player stands in always wins; the fix batch's section below). No player: nothing changes. A **worker thread** reads and checks a chunk (hash, the tile header); the **net thread** takes what has arrived in `pump(budget)`, a few tiles at a time under the budget (3 ms; at least one tile), with `addTile` / `removeTile`. Detour joins a new tile to its loaded neighbours, so a path and a ray cross a border that both sides have loaded, and **an unloaded border is a wall**. `AreaNav` keeps no poly reference between calls (and `Mover` keeps world points), so a removed tile's salt change is never a stale reference: the answer is to ask again. `PlayerWorld::tick_world` drives it at the world's 20 Hz (`NpcWorld::player_points`, `pump`, `NpcWorld::chunks_resident`); at start `preload_window` loads the login spot's 5 x 5 chunks (about 0.1 s), or, for a small area (E1's 1 km circle), all of the area's chunks when they fit four fifths of the mesh.
- **NPCs.** An NPC whose chunk is not resident (`NpcWorld::add`) **stands on the terrain, dormant, and is grounded for good when its chunk arrives** (`chunks_resident`: the surface nearest the height the data gave it, a floor of the navmesh where the chunk has one); `GroundReport` counts them (`wait for their chunk`). An awake-range NPC whose chunk has left the window sleeps where it stands. NPCs the data places beyond the footprint are not built: the navmesh ends at the region's edge.
- **The edge.** `AreaNav::in_region` (the rectangles). `PlayerWorld::note_player` logs an exit (`[region] player N left the Riverlands ...`; at most once every 30 s per player, the stair-stepped edge is crossed often) and the re-entry that follows a logged exit; **there is no wall**: the move checker (`session/move_checker.cpp`) never looks at the ground (speed, rise and fall only), so a player outside is judged as inside, and every ground query (`ground_below`, the mounts' `ground`, the NPCs') answers "none" there and takes its no-ground branch (the E1 circle's edge behaved the same). `region_exits()` / `outside_region()` are for tests. Past the edge the mounts degrade quietly: there is no terrain, so a summon is refused, and a rider who logs out in the air outside is saved mid-air; the player is told nothing.
- **Measured** (`test_worldd_nav_window` on the real file, a 27 km walk across the Riverlands at a mount's 22 m/s): the window follows with 344 chunk loads and 309 unloads, **a peak of 2,240 tiles resident (2,696 counting the ones on the way)**, no failure, the longest pump 3.3 ms (budget 3), a chunk read and checked in at most 10 ms on the worker, the densest chunk's 64 tiles added in 7.7 ms in all (over two or three pumps) and removed in 3 ms, a pump 0.35 ms on average. The E1 circle through the chunked file: the answer key's 334 of 335 ground positions, 245 of 245 walking players on the navmesh, 66 of 66 paths (the same numbers as the flat file).
- **Tests.** `test_worldd_nav_window` (137 checks: a small chunked world built in memory, 8 x 8 chunks of tiles of 1,000 cm: the chunked build keeps the circle build's tiles, the file's refusals, the window, paths, rays and ground at a border, the policy and the cap, the cap shared by eight players who arrive one after another, set_interest's gate, a failed read and its retry, the NPCs, the region's edge and where a respawn is put; and on the real file E1's tiles, the walk above and the A* by the length of the leg) and `test_worldd_nav` (the answer keys: E1's circle and the Riverlands' walking players, `tests/ground_key_region.h` by `tools/capture/gen_ground_key.py --region`: **the key has positions in 6 of the 12 zones (Joeva 283, Winstead 62, Miraleth 9, Halcyon 6, Samia's ArtisanPOI 3, NewAela 1); of the 209 positions within 30 m of the heightfield 205 lie on it or on a floor, but 192 of the 209 are in the two node towns and only 17 are anywhere else (16 on the ground); 155 more (115 in Joeva's zone, 38 in Winstead's, one each in Miraleth's and Halcyon's) stand 40 to 57 m above the heightfield**, where retail's players waited: the cooked map's foliage stands on that heightfield within 15 cm in those same ZOIs, so the towns' own ground is not in the map. The test prints the key per zone and checks every zone with five or more positions near the heightfield; the user's walks are the evidence for the rest of the footprint).

### The Riverlands as the area: the population, the nodes, the GM garden and the load (G1, stage 1, Part B; an uncommitted draft)

The area is now the world of G1: the 12 zones of influence (ZOIs) whose biome is Riverlands, **49.12 km2 as 401 exact rectangles** (`spawns/region_data.h`, generated by `tools/capture/gen_region.py` from `fixtures/region/*.json`; the lookup is `spawns/region_index.h`, a grid of 8,192 cm squares over the rectangles). `spawns::AreaConfig::riverlands()` is worldd's default (`--area-radius -1`); `AreaConfig{}` is still E1's 1 km circle (`--area-radius 1000`), with no zones, and gives E1's numbers unchanged (1,803 NPCs from 3,781 + 817 points, 28 nodes).
- **The zone of every point.** `AreaConfig::zoi_at(x, y)` is the guid of the ZOI record that owns the point's 512 cm cell (the 12 zones tile the footprint exactly; each zone's cell count equals its record's; the login spot is in Ruins of Aela). The spawn filter takes it: a `PopulationFilter` that names zones of influence (11 overrides name Aela, Winstead or NewAela; 2 assets name one in their own filter) passes when the point's zone is one of them, and in a circle (zone 0) never does, as in E1. `DataSpawn::zoi` and `PopulationStats::by_zoi` carry it; the start log lists the NPCs by zone. `--no-zone-filter` (AOC_NO_ZONE_FILTER) is the kill switch (the Riverlands with E1's rule). Modelled: the ArtisanPOI cells (`..._ArtisanPOI`, 0.45 km2 together) are their own zones, not their parent's (the research could not tell how retail's filters treat them). The biome is still "none" (a filter that names a biome never passes), as in E1.
- **The population.** The region's whole data population goes through E1's spawn filter, kill switches and generic-NPC rules unchanged: **22,122 points (18,120 placement points + 4,002 random points of 412 area sets) -> 10,554 NPCs**; 10,524 points are dropped by the filter (zones off; 10,485 with them on) and 1,044 have no NPC to make (1,083 with the zones on: the 39 points the zone rule lets through the filter are Blueprints no NPC can be made from, so the NPC count is the same). (The spec's "about 10,850 NPCs and 22,421 points" were the 55 km2 chunk upper bound, not the exact footprint.) Besides: 4 captured NPCs (8 were the data's twins), 439 resource nodes and 26 GM vendors: **11,023 actors** (10 more with the garden). **Golden counts:** `tools/capture/pop_oracle.py` is an independent Python port of the selection (it began as the research's `pop.py`, which on this content.db and the exact footprint gives 10,554 as well, and E1's 1,803 for the circle), reading the footprint and the zones from the JSON rather than from the header; `gen_pop_golden.py` writes `tests/pop_golden_region.h` (the stage counts, the points dropped by reason, the NPCs by Blueprint and by zone, and a digest of every NPC's place, level and Blueprint) for zones off and on, and `test_worldd_region` compares worldd's own run to it. A selection takes 30 ms; `load_area` for the region 0.5 s.
- **The nodes.** Retail's captures are the only source of node positions: `tools/capture/find_region_nodes.py` scanned all 1,562 captures (about 4 minutes on 3 processes) for gatherable opens inside the footprint: 1,158 sightings of 516 nodes (a node keeps its GatherableGuid across sessions); one sighting each, a fresh one before a gathered one; **70 nodes were only ever seen gathered and are left out** (a felled node: stage 2's state machine can add them), 7 were the same place under another guid; **439 nodes** (`fixtures/region/node_picks.json`), each built the way E1's were (`gen_area_spawns.py`: the open whole, its exports completed from the other picked captures, every block decoding to its last bit). 28 are inside E1's circle (the same 28). Visible only; stage 2 gives them their interaction. **`area_spawns.h` stays a header: 396 KB** (the move to a data file was for more than about 2 MB).
- **The GM garden** (`--gm-garden` or `AOC_GM_GARDEN=1`; **off by default**; `spawns/gm_garden.h`). One node for each gathering profession and certification tier, made from a captured gatherable open with a REAL `GatherableActorDef` of the design data (`spawns/garden_defs.h`, `tools/capture/gen_gm_garden.py` from content.db: the def's Guid in the actor, its PlayerInteractionDef in the first input of the InteractableComponent), 30 to 60 m from the login spot (a sector for each profession, the tiers outwards: 32 m for Novice to 56 m for GrandMaster), on the ground (a navmesh floor within 1.5 m of the terrain, else the terrain; the login spot's height without a ground file), at least 6 m from any other actor and 8 m from another garden node; placed by `load_ground` (or by `main` when there is no ground). **The data has actor nodes for 10 of the 15 slots:** Novice, Apprentice and Journeyman for Mining (Granite, Slate, Limestone), Lumberjacking (Oak, Weeping Willow, Braidwood) and Herbalism (Daffodil, Moonbell, Spindlevine), and GrandMaster for Herbalism (`TestSpiritBloom`, a designers' test def). **There is no GatherableActorDef at Master for any profession, nor at GrandMaster for Mining and Lumberjacking** (their Master and GrandMaster interactions belong to painted foliage, stage 3). worldd logs the five gaps and places nothing for them. A def's tier is the highest certification of the interactions of its foliage states (the resource type's own requirement disagrees for `Plant_BloodboiledSpiritBloom_Medium_1`).
- **Cost** (`test_worldd_region_load`: the real content.db, npc_motion.tsv and the 950 MB chunked area_nav.bin; run it alone. The sessions are ActorViews on real Connections with a fake client; the world tick is `PlayerWorld::tick_world`):
  - *Start* (`PlayerWorld`, Blueprint table, `load_area`, `load_ground`): 0.48 + 0.02 + 0.53 + 0.38 = **1.4 s**; 6 MB before, **265 MB after the area, 461 MB after the ground (peak 573 MB)**: about 23 KB an actor in the registry, with the ability catalog, the terrain (161 MB) and the login spot's window besides. Of 10,584 NPCs, 1,617 found their ground and 8,967 wait for their chunk (grounded when a player's window reaches them).
  - *One player at the login spot* (the densest start): the world tick **0.06 ms on average** (budget 1 ms), p99 0.4 ms, 1.2 ms at most in the steady state and **7.9 ms at most in the first minute** (the first step of the NPC world, which sets 10,584 timestamps once; budget 10 ms); the client **8.5 KB/s** (budget 100), 175 channels in use, peak 203 (budget 700); 175 actors awake and 293 dormant.
  - *Six players crossing* (seeded tours through the footprint's rectangles at a mount's 22 m/s, 15 simulated minutes, 18,000 world ticks): the world tick **0.26 ms on average**, p99 3.2 ms, **4.1 ms at most, none over 5 ms**; each client 5.7 KB/s on average (worst 10.0 KB/s; the busiest 10 s 54.9 KB/s), peak 205 channels; **the ground window peaked at 4,096 tiles** (the cap: six windows of 25 chunks do not fit, so the room is shared by distance, review A I1: 1,453 chunk loads and 1,408 unloads, none failed, and **in none of 5,220 player-seconds did a player stand in a chunk that was not resident**; the first version, which kept the first players' outer rings, made 898 loads and 854 unloads and never counted the coverage); a pump of the window costs the world tick **4.1 ms at most** (a pump stops at its 3 ms, one tile may overrun it: a tile's removal takes 3.2 ms at most, its addition 1.1 ms; these are what the 3 ms p99 of the crossing is made of), standing the NPCs of an arrived chunk on their ground 0.8 ms at most. A dense chunk's 64 tiles take up to 23 ms of addTile in all, over several pumps (so "a chunk load <= 5 ms on the net thread" holds per pump, not per chunk); the worker reads a chunk in 40 ms at most (a busy disk). At the end 1,626 of the 10,584 NPCs still wait for their chunk; memory 524 MB.
  - *A client that starts 3 km from the default spawn* (the investigation of 2026-10-10: nothing proved that the NPCs which are waiting for their chunk at the start reach a client): 44 NPCs stand within 100 m of the start, all 44 waiting for a chunk that is not loaded; 45 s later none waits, all 44 are opened to the client (it holds 105 awake and 171 dormant actors) and each stands at the height the NPC world grounded it at.
  - *The searches that found no path*, by why (the crossing's 15,365 legs): of 15,066 wander asks 812 found no path (370 ran out of the 2,500 + 5 a metre expansions, 346 had no way, 89 were longer than 3 times the straight line, 7 started off the mesh) and 290 found no point to walk to; of 384 patrol asks **113 (29 %) found no path: 74 no way at all, 31 an end waypoint off the loaded mesh, 8 out of expansions**, none too long. So the cap costs 2 % of the asks; why a fifth of the patrol legs have no way (waypoints on navmesh islands, a lake, a border that is not loaded) is not investigated (docs/backlog.md).
  - What the load test changed: the NPC world's step reached **11.8 ms** with up to 17 paths asked in a step: a walk that cannot be found made Detour's A* explore up to its 16,384 nodes. `AreaNav::find_path` takes `max_iterations` (Detour's sliced find-path) and an NPC's `Mover::move_to` gives a search up to 2,500 node expansions (its legs are a few hundred metres) and then no path (the NPC decides again later; deterministic, unlike a clock budget, which broke `test_worldd_area`'s same-world check). The longest step is now **2.0 ms** with the same 17 paths. The counters are `PlayerWorld::window_cost()`, `NpcWorld::Stats::max_step_ms` and `AreaNav::WindowStats::max_tile_add_ms`.
- **Tests.** `test_worldd_region` (the index: every cell of the bounding box against the data, the zones' cell counts, edges, hostile numbers; the golden counts in both modes; `load_area` on the region: the oracle's NPC count, every actor inside the footprint, two loads the same world, time and memory; the garden: real def records in content.db, 30 to 60 m, clear, grounded, deterministic, with no ground or no donor) and `test_worldd_region_load` (above; heavy: `scripts/run_tests.ps1 -Heavy` runs it, the default gate does not; it skips without the chunked ground). `test_worldd_spawn_filter` and `test_worldd_area` (E1's circle) are unchanged and pass.

### G1 stage 1: the review's fix batch and the Nodes 1 test's findings (2026-10-10; an uncommitted draft)

A code review and the Nodes 1 in-game test found these; each has a test.
- **The window shares the tile cap by distance** (review A I1). The first version let a resident chunk that a player WANTED never give way, so three players parked in three places filled the mesh with their outer rings and a fourth player (or a relog, or a respawn elsewhere) got no chunk at all, not even the one it stood in. Now `plan_window` ranks every chunk by its squared distance (in chunks) to the nearest player and a newcomer takes the place of a resident chunk only when that chunk is STRICTLY farther from its own player than the newcomer is from its own: every player keeps the chunk it stands in (the mesh holds about 70 chunks, so about 70 players are covered), the outer rings give way together, and two players at the same distance do not take each other's chunks in turn. `test_worldd_nav_window` ("the tile cap, shared by distance": eight players arrive one after another on a mesh of 15 chunks; each gets its own chunk).
- **The window is planned when something changed** (review A I2). `PlayerWorld::tick_world` is called after every datagram the endpoint reads; `drive_window` now runs at the world's 20 Hz (ticks closer than 45 ms are skipped), and `AreaNav::set_interest` returns at once when the players' chunks are the same as at the last plan and no wanted chunk waits for room or for a retry (`WindowStats::plans` / `plans_skipped`).
