A player that is not a number is no player. `PlayerWorld::drive_window` logs every 30 s at info: the chunks and tiles resident, the players' chunks (`ready` or `LOADING`), the NPCs still waiting for their chunk, loads, drops, failures, retries, plans.
- **One failed read no longer fails the rest** (review A I3, the investigation's D2). The worker tested `if (!in || ...)` before every read; `read_nav_chunk` clears the stream, but the guard came first, so after one short read (the file rewritten under a running worldd, an antivirus lock) every later chunk failed and was marked failed for good. The worker clears (and reopens) the stream before each read, a failed chunk is asked for again after 30 s (up to 4 reads; `set_retry_delay`), and every failure is named in the log (`[nav] ground: chunk (cx, cy) ...`). `read_nav_chunk` also checks that a chunk's tiles lie in that chunk and are sorted (given the file's `chunk_tiles`), the directory is sorted without repeats and no row holds more tiles than a chunk can, and a tile with more polygons than the mesh's `maxPolys` is refused (review A M6, B M3).
- **A respawn or a summon over a chunk that is not here** (review A I4). `PlayerWorld::spot_height`: a spot whose chunk is not resident keeps the data's height (the terrain alone would put a shrine on a bridge under the bridge); the summon's ground check trusts the player's own height over a chunk that is not here but has terrain.
- **The A* cap** (review A I5). A search gives up after 2,500 expansions plus 5 a metre of the straight line (`Mover::search_iterations`), and one step of the NPC world spends at most 60,000 expansions on all its searches (`NpcWorldConfig::max_search_iterations_per_step`; the rest decide on the next step). Of the data's 9,783 patrol legs the median is 15 m and the 99th percentile 101 m (5 are over 300 m), so the cap serves them; an A* over polygons of about 3.4 m is quadratic in the leg (a 500 m leg needs over 10,000 expansions), and the few long legs, and the ones a river sends the long way round, are given up and tried again later (`test_worldd_nav_window` measures the bands on the real navmesh; the load test counts the failures of a live run).
- **Smaller:** a waiting NPC that the registry removes leaves `pending_ground()` (review A M1); the region-exit log is at most once every 30 s per player (M2); a pending NPC grounded more than 30 cm from where it stood is a reliable change of its actor, so a view that holds it as a Snapshot or Dormant actor sends it again at its grounded height (the investigation's D1); a ground file that is a flat file under the Riverlands as the area is an error in the log (review B I6); the GM garden refuses a donor with fewer than 7 interaction values (M6). Past the region's edge the mounts degrade quietly (no terrain, so no summon, and a rider who logs out in the air outside is saved mid-air): there is no message to the player (review A M3).
- **After the player dies, the client's movement clock starts again** (the Nodes 1 test: 66 s of frozen movement, melee "out of range", then a snap back to the shrine). The death's `ClientRestart` restarts the client's pawn and with it the TimeStamp of its moves near 0; `MoveChecker::teleport` kept the old TimeStamp, so every move below it was dropped as TimeStampNotRising (debug line, the first three only) until the new clock passed the old one. `on_respawn_choice` calls `MoveChecker::restart_clock()` (the position, credits and pace bank stay; a possession's `rebase` already starts everything over). A run of 30 or more moves dropped that way is logged once at info. `test_e1_player_death`: the moves of the restarted clock after the respawn are acknowledged. A line at info every 30 s per session gives the player's position, the ground chunk (`ready` or `NOT LOADED YET`), the moves acknowledged, refused and dropped, and what its view holds.
- **A flyer in movement mode 3** (the Nodes 1 test: the gryphon's moves of 69 cm in 0.069 s and 96 cm in 0.078 s were refused by 1 and 19 cm and the rider put back mid-flight). Mode 3 (falling: the take-off, a stall) was judged along the ground at the mount's ground limit; on a mount that can fly it now gets the cruise times the margin as its horizontal limit, as the flying mode has (the rising and falling rules, and a mount that cannot fly, are unchanged). `test_worldd_mounts_fly`.
- **The loot-chance knob** (the Nodes 1 test: drops too rare). `--loot-chance-mult N` / `AOC_LOOT_CHANCE_MULT` (default 1; the play launchers set 3) multiplies the chance of every `RollEachByPercent` draw (`RollContext::chance_mult`, each chance capped at 1) and nothing else: never a quantity, a weight or a predicate. A table picks its sub tables by percent and a sub table's items by percent again, so x3 gives up to about 9 times the item stacks (a Bandit Raider of level 10: 66 stacks in 3,000 kills, 250 at x3); the Glint certificates are a predicate on `Glint_Chance`, not a percent draw, and do not change. The death line now names the creature (else its Blueprint), the highest looter's level and the level gap with the data's `Loot_Falloff` (1 for a gap of 0 to 9, 0 from 10), the loot chance multiplier and each stack by item name and quantity. `test_worldd_rewards`.
- **The default gate** no longer runs `test_worldd_region_load` (review B I1): `scripts/run_tests.ps1 -Heavy` does, and the baseline does not list it; its wall-clock checks are three times their design budget; a test that skipped part of its checks prints a NOTE after the run's summary. mapc: E1's circle needs its own `--out-dir` and has no `--check`; a finished batch part is sealed (length and FNV of its three files, flushed to the disk, the marker written last) and the signature names the paks' and the usmap's write times (review B I5, I7); `region_foliage.tsv` is version 2: the owner actor is a column, a def a component does not serialize is its archetype's (review B I2).

### The missing NPC types and the NPCs that stood still (plan E1, stage 2 fix; an uncommitted draft)

The in-game run of stage 2 showed wolves wandering, **guards and the "Bone" goblins standing still, and no goblins, hawks or grems at all**. The root causes, with their evidence:

1. **3,262 of the 4,598 points had no NPC because no capture exported their Blueprint** (385 Blueprints). The data names every Blueprint's path (`PopulationAsset.AssetRef`); only the export (path, package, NetworkChecksum) was missing. **The client does not compare a zero checksum**, read from the exe's code (`AOCClient-Win64-Shipping.exe`, not from a log line): `UPackageMapClient::InternalLoadObject` (0x1442740f0) reads the export's checksum when the flags have bit 2 and `cmp dword ptr [rbp-0x31], 0 / je` at 0x1442748b8 skips the whole check on 0 (the "Default object package network checksum mismatch" log, 0x1442749b2, is past it); the guid cache's own check (the code at 0x144270347, "GetObjectFromNetGUID: Network checksum mismatch", the connection-closing `NetChecksumMismatch`) starts with `cmp dword ptr [rdi+0x20], 0 / je 0x144270567` on the stored checksum; both are also skipped by `net.IgnoreNetworkChecksumMismatch`. So computing UE's class network checksum is not needed.
2. **Patrols walked nowhere, and their failed path-finds starved every wander.** The log's own counters: 42,040 failed patrol targets in two minutes, and of 189,682 wander asks 189,560 deferred. `npc_route.waypoints` are the route component's transform *already applied* to its `points` (911 of the 915 routes that carry both say so; the captured patrollers walk them as they are, to 30 to 63 cm); the draft applied the transform again, so every waypoint lay far off the navmesh, no patrol could start, and each failed attempt spent the 24 path-finds a step all the NPCs share, in id order, so that the NPCs after the first few never got a turn. Fixed: the waypoints are used as they are; the budget goes round (the NPC the step starts with changes every step); a Patrol with no route wanders. With it, 117 of the 117 NPCs with a life of their own within 150 m of the login spot walked in two minutes (46 of 243 moved at all before).
3. **Stationary instances stand in retail.** The retail captures hold 139 observed epochs of NPCs of Stationary instances and none moved (an earlier, looser count found 15 of 215 moving: 14 scripted encounter handlers and a guard captain). Guard_Wizard NPCs of Patrol instances walk (9 of 9) and its Stationary ones stand (0 of 16); the captured Goblin_Bonebinders of the area (11 epochs) stand or walk the short legs of a Default instance. 58 of the area's 86 Guard_Soldier and 74 of its 155 Goblin_Bonebinder NPCs stand now (Stationary by the data); the guards and goblins that stood in the run were these and the patrols and wanderers the starved path-finding kept still. The Wander and Default ones walk now. Where the data says Stationary and retail's instance does too, the NPC stands.
4. **No NPC flies.** Hawks and ravens have `MOVE_NavWalking` Blueprints (capsule 140), the captures send them in the same movement mode 10 as every creature, with a zero vertical velocity, and a hawk's height follows the terrain's slope smoothly (200 cm over 12 m in capture 771, where its origin is 6 cm from the ground the walkers beside it imply: the one comparison the captures allow, there being no hawk in the area's captures). They walk the navmesh: the fliers count is 0, and the answer key (`tests/test_worldd_area.cpp`) holds it.

**What a generic NPC is** (`spawns/generic_npc.h`): the open of a plain creature the captures hold (the Wolf's: ANPCCharacter and the six standing subobjects) with the Blueprint's path from the data and checksum 0 as its archetype export, NetGUIDs of worldd's own (a hash of the path with the static bit set: retail's are hashes too), the Blueprint's `StartingAlignments` and `DefaultScale` (from `npc_motion.tsv`: `mapc` reads every pawn Blueprint under `/Game/DesignAssets`, 2,169 of them, along the parents of its class default object), and the instance's `PopAssetId`, `PopInstanceId` and level range, from which the client reads the nameplate, the faction and the rest. A Blueprint with no line in `npc_motion.tsv` is not a pawn (a banner, a tent, a quest sparkle: 655 points) and gets no NPC; a missing table, `--no-generic-npcs`, and `--npc-exclude Goblin_Gravehide,Hawk_C` (names or whole paths, case apart; `AOC_NO_GENERIC_NPCS`, `AOC_NPC_EXCLUDE`) are the kill switches, each type that is left out is counted in the log. **Modelled, not retail:** the donor's stats, abilities and associated node (stage 3 reads the NPC stat lists), no appearance component (92 points name only assets that have an NPCAppearance record: 79 Guard_Soldier, made from the captured template when there is one, and 13 quest NPCs, which show the Blueprint's default); the NPC types are unproven in game until a run: that the client opens an NPC from an export it has no checksum for, and shows each Blueprint's look.

**The PopulationArea sets**: a set with a PopulationArea places no instance at an offset: the area says how many NPCs the world keeps (`MinNumSpawns` to `MaxNumSpawns`, drawn by the point hash) inside its circles (`Center` and `Extent` relative to the placement's origin; a circle is picked by its weight times its area, the point is uniform in it), the set's instances in turn; the height is the terrain's (`ground_by_terrain`). 101 area sets keep 817 points in the area (the stage-1 draft counted the 148 offsets of their members, which are not where the NPCs are).

**On the real area now** (the log's `[area]` and `[nav]` lines): **3,973 NPCs: 3,943 from the data (2,771 from a generic Blueprint, 817 of them from area circles; 655 points are props), 4 captured, 26 GM vendors** (stage 1: 944). All 3,973 found a ground. **Standing 1,007 (Stationary by the data, the GM vendors, the captured NPCs), wandering 2,868, patrolling 98, flying 0**; 17 Patrol instances have no route and wander. `[nav] by type (standing/wandering/patrolling)` lists the counts for each of the 257 types (Goblin_Gravehide 444: 78 / 358 / 8; Wolf 231: 80 / 144 / 7; Goblin_Bonebinder 155: 74 / 68 / 13; Raven 134: 0 / 133 / 1; Guard_Soldier 86: 58 / 25 / 3 ...). A step takes 130 to 370 us on average with the area's crowd; a session with 300 NPCs around the player: 213 us a tick on average, 321 kbit/s to the client with 46 NPCs walking on average, 7.0 kbit/s on the wire for each (the shorter legs stop and start more often: each stop is an update).

**The answer key** (`tools/capture/gen_npc_motion.py` -> `npc/retail_motion.h`, from 39 captures): per archetype the NPC epochs the retail captures hold, how many moved, the median wander and patrol speed and the leg lengths, and the epochs of Stationary instances (139: none moved). `test_worldd_area` holds the area to it: every archetype that moved in most of its epochs (55 of them; 23 are in the area) has NPCs that walk here (the four that do not, RockElemental_Rockling, Cultist, Guard_Wizard and Node_Villager_M, have only Stationary instances in the area, and their Stationary instances stand in retail too), 90 % of the NPCs near the player that wander or patrol walk within two minutes, from where it stands 90 % of the NPCs can walk to a wander target, the per-type counts add up to the stand / wander / patrol / fly counts, and no NPC flies.

**Not done:** routes placed by a city node or an encounter (none lies in the area; an encounter route's waypoints are relative to the placement's transform: 46 cm from the captured walkers; a city node's to the node's, which is a runtime thing), a patrol's `NumberOfWaypoints`, NPC appearance components, the stat lists (stage 3), flying (none in retail).

### The spawn filter and the fights (plan E1, stage 3, part 1; an uncommitted draft)

Part 1 of stage 3 (spec §4 and its "Settled in E1, stage 3 part 1") makes the NPCs fight: a spawn filter first (the area holds only what retail spawns for a character here now), then the combat core. Loot, XP, respawn, the NPCs' death and the player's death are part 2: the seams are listed at the end.

**The spawn filter (`spawns/spawn_filter.h`).** The user's run showed spawns retail does not have (invisible villagers, invisible "NPC Quest Master"s, a T-posed "Wail"). The filter is the data's own conditions evaluated against what this world is (`SpawnContext`, set by `PlayerWorld::set_spawn_context`, before `load_area`), and the retail captures are the answer key. A point spawns when no record on its way is `bDevOnly`, its WorldSpawn is the world map's (`Verra_World_Master`: the rest are quest, event and phase spawns, a node's layout and the designers' test maps; on the 1,105 captures 12.3 % of the world map's covered points have an NPC of their archetype within 20 m, the empty-map ones 0.4 %, every test map 0 %), no gate (quest objective, event, story phase, building level, node layout, action response) is the only way its set spawns, and each candidate asset passes its PopulationFilter (the instance's FilteringOverride, else the set's, else the WorldSpawn's: the node level, culture, zones, biome, season, weather and the hour). A **humanoid Blueprint whose body needs an NPCAppearanceComponent** the open does not carry is left out until an appearance generator exists (`--spawn-humanoids` lets them through, invisible). On the real area **3,943 points became 1,803 NPCs**; the 2,469 points dropped: not the world map 1,478, hour 441, humanoid needing an appearance 402, a prop tag 121, gated 19, node or zone 7, node level 1 (the log's `[area]` line counts them). `--no-spawn-filter` is stage 2's world; `--spawn-hour H` sets the hour the time-of-day conditions are judged at (default: the machine's local hour).

**The stat engine, format version 3.** An NPC's stats are the stat engine run for the kind of NPC at its level: `statprog` compiles two NPC profile classes (`kNpcClassBase` = 32 "NPC", 33 "NPC_Guard"; their lists are CombatStats, NPC_Base and the elite scalar, no common lists) and `combat/npc_stats.h` runs them with the Blueprint's `StatDistribution` (eight weights) and `EliteLevelRanks` as the NPC state. Version 3 adds `OpActor`: an expression that reads another actor (`GetTarget().Health`, `GetInstigator().Level`) is bracketed by it, so the same program runs as the caster or as the target (`Evaluator::SetActor`; an unset role reads as the caster), and the seven native damage functions (host functions 20 to 26: `CalculateDamage`, `...Multiplier`, `...AdditiveMultiplier`, `...Mitigation`, `...Critical`, `...Penetration`, `...Dampening`; `stats/damage.h`) over a `DamageProfile` read from `DefaultCombat.ini` (content.db's `combat_profile` and `combat_element`): float32 reads and products in the order the exe runs them, a Go twin (`services/internal/statprog/damage.go`) and the shared corpus (`stats/testdata/corpus.txt`: the NPC, role and `damage` parts) hold both to the same bits. A content.db built by an older contentc (version 8 or less: no combat tables, a version 2 stat program) is refused: rebuild it (`cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc`).

**`combat/`.**
- `combat_data.h` resolves the design data once: the damage profile, the guids of the stats the rolls read (found in the bundle by name), `combat_config` (the in-combat timer 6 s, the global multipliers, the out-of-range distance 3,500 cm) and who fights whom (`alignment_relation` over `relationship.permissions`: 1 hostile, 2 friendly, 3 neutral; the player's alignment is 6064629444695359488).
- `hit_context.h` is what an expression reads of the hit it runs for (tags, flags, the source, the targets, the relation) and `hit_resolver.h` works out a hit: the rolls, then the value of each stat mod through the stat engine as the instigator with the target in the role of `GetTarget()`. **The rolls are modelled, no capture shows a rate**: miss = the target's evasion less the instigator's accuracy of the hit's aspect; block = BlockChance; crit = CriticalChance less CriticalAvoidance (a hit's own CritRate, BlockRate or MissRate expression replaces them; `bForceCant*` and `bForceRoll*` decide what can happen at all); the aspect and element come from the hit's EventTags (`ability.PhysicalScalingOverride` and `MagicalScalingOverride` win). A heal rolls only the crit. A hit that misses has no stat mods. The result is applied by the world to an NPC and by the session to its player (shields first).
- `npc_replicator.h` writes an NPC's combat state into its replicated actor as retail does: the StatsComponent's EveryoneProxy items (Health, MaxHealth, Mana, MaxMana, MoveSpeedMult, PhysicalPenetration, MagicalPenetration, StealthLevel, CharacterType: the engine's replication mask 7; an item the donor open lacked is added) and CombatInfo (`bInCombat` and `ActiveTargets` flip together, `bIsDead`, `EliteRank`).
- `combat_world.h` is the fights of the area, one per worldd (`PlayerWorld::combat()`, started when the ground is there unless `--no-combat` / `AOC_NO_COMBAT`): an NPC becomes a combatant the first time it matters (a player targets or hits it, or comes within the scan radius of an NPC hostile to players); the thousands of others stay plain registry actors. It runs each combatant's AI, its attacks (the kit of its Blueprint from `npc_kits.h`, written by `tools/capture/gen_npc_kits.py` from the research's picks, else a level-scaled basic attack, `NPC_Bandit_Melee_Slash_ABL_1`, cast through D2's ability runtime with the NPC as the caster) and what a player's hit does to it; players join as `PlayerPort`s. `hit_port.h` is the interface the player's ability runtime asks (`WorldHitPort`: is the target there, alive, hostile, in range and in sight; its stats; the hit rules; where to put what the hit did).
- **`npc/ai/`** (`npc_ai.h`, `threat.h`): idle (stage 2's wandering and patrolling) -> aggro (a hostile living combatant in the aggro radius and in line of sight by Detour, or anyone that hurt it) -> chase (a path to the top target, repathed as it moves) -> attack (the kit's rotation, in range, a little farther once started) -> leash (beyond the leash radius from home, or an empty table) -> reset (walk home at a run, threat cleared, healed to full). Threat is the hit's `threat_expr`, else |damage| x ThreatGeneration; healing an enemy adds some and coming into the radius a little; a challenger needs 110 % of the top to take the target. Numbers: the asset's aggro radius, else 800 cm (`--npc-aggro-cm`); the leash max(3,000, 3 x aggro) (`--npc-leash-cm`); a think every 0.2 s. **Modelled: no capture shows an aggro by proximity, a leash or a reset.**
- **The session** (`session/player_session.cpp`, `CombatSide`): a `ServerTriggerAbility` whose `Targets` names an actor of the world is checked (alive, hostile where the ability needs it, in range, in sight: `Refusal::BadTarget`, `OutOfRange`, `NoLineOfSight`, answered with `ClientEndAbility`) and its ability item carries that actor's collision cylinder as `Targets`; a `RequestHitVerification_Server` component is resolved the same way, the hit is resolved by the NPC-aware pipeline and confirmed with one `FHitInst` per target (the target's component, flags, the formula's value and the part that took effect). An NPC's hit on the player is queued and applied by the session's tick (Health through the shields, the confirmation naming the NPC as the instigator, Lethal at 0). The player's in-combat flag follows the first exchange and clears 6 s after the last: the stats' `in_combat` flag (the out-of-combat regeneration is five times faster, so it ends) and the pawn's CombatInfo `bInCombat` (`PlayerBootstrap::set_in_combat`).

**Unproven until a run (the in-game test is the first):** the client's target object ids (an NPC's collision cylinder: since E1 stage 3 fix 3 the actor id + 4, retail's slot inside the actor's block, exported with the bunch that first names it; the draft's + 24 was another object's GUID and disconnected the client); what the client shows of an NPC's attack on the player (the confirmation has no animation: retail NPC abilities have no montage replication in the captures); and every modelled number above. **The MaxHealth gap is closed (the E1 review, 2026-10-07):** an NPC's MaxHealth was 5 to 19 % too high for rank 0 at levels 5 to 20, 25 to 40 % too low for elites and several times too low for guards because both stat engines ran an initializer entry's `GetStatInitRunningTotal()` over the earlier entries alone and added the stat's Base afterwards, so the elite scalar's `total * f` scaled only the small NPC_Base part of MaxHealth and left Constitution's part unscaled; the running total is the stat's value so far with the Base folded in (`services/internal/statprog/README.md` "The model"). Now every one of the 29 goldens and the 26 captured opens is within 1 % (MaxHealth and MaxMana), and MoveSpeedMult is retail's 1.05 + 0.1 x rank (every NPC had 1.0: 5 to 35 % slower than retail's); the elites' and guards' ratings (power, mitigation, accuracy) follow, so an elite's hits are 14 to 74 % harder than before and a guard's ten times. `test_worldd_combat` asserts the 1 % for all of them (it tolerated 20 % for rank 0 alone and printed the rest).

**The seams of part 2:** `CombatWorld::set_death_hook` (an NPC whose Health reaches 0 calls it once with the killer, where it died, what it was and who fought it; its AI stops, Health 0 and `bIsDead` are written in the same step and the corpse stays in the registry), `ai_state(id)`, `health_of(id)`, and the motion table's `rewards` column (the reward tables each Blueprint names).

**Not done (part 2):** loot, XP, respawn, the NPCs' corpses closing, the player's death, NPC attack animations, humanoid NPCs (they need an appearance generator).

### Loot, experience, death and respawn (plan E1, stage 3, part 2; an uncommitted draft)

Part 2 of stage 3 (spec §4, "Settled in E1, stage 3, part 2") finishes the fights: an NPC's death, what it hands out, the corpse's life, its respawn, and the player's own death. The research is `research/combat` (retail captures 552, 758 and others, decoded to the bit); where no capture shows a number it is modelled and listed below.

**What the data gives (contentc version 10).** The kill's experience and loot are not a research table: the design database's reward tables decide them. `contentc` exports all 8,197 `RewardTable` records as JSON (`loot_table(guid, name, data)`: the table's own predicate, its algorithm and its entries; an entry is a reward with experience, items and currency, or a sub-table, each with a weight, a percentage or a weight expression) and the decay of the level difference (`xp_rule('adventuring_decay')`). The expressions compile into the stat program (1,531 of the 1,845 expressions the abilities and rewards hold; every one the kill rewards use compiles), with four host functions (`GetPlayerLootMaxLevel` 30, `GetPlayerLootAvgLuck` 31, `DoesNPCContainTag` 160, `GetPlayerLevel` 161), the compiler's `Int`, a zero-argument `GetNPCLevel()` and the `$F:Name$` formula references. A Blueprint's tables are the motion table's `rewards` column. **Kill experience** = `Base_Kill_XP(the creature's level) x NPC_Base_Reward_Multiplier x` the decay of the level difference (a level-8 Rabid gave 434 to a level-8 player; retail's ~19 at the cap is the 0.05 decay: the formula's numbers match), and **loot falls off** with `Loot_Falloff` (0 from ten levels apart: a level-25 player gets no Glint from a level-10 Wolf).

**`combat/rewards.h`** is the evaluator: `RewardBook::roll(roots, RollContext)` runs the tables the Blueprint names for the NPC's stats (its tags, its level, the looting player's level) and returns the items to loot, the direct rewards, the experience by type (Adventuring, Weapon, NodeActivity) and the currency. The selection algorithms are All, None, PureRandom, PerElementWeightedRandom, PerElementWeightedExpressionRandom and RollEachByPercent [modelled: the data names them, no capture shows a table's draw]; a quest- or biome-gated entry whose expression does not compile reads as "no". The measured Glint drop rate of a Wolf is 0.381 against the data's 0.376, the quantity 3 to 8.

**`combat/aftermath.h`** (`PlayerWorld::aftermath()`, started with the combat world) is what follows a death, from `CombatWorld`'s death hook, in the order the captures show:
- **experience**: each player that fought it (the killer and every attacker) is given `KillReward` through `PlayerPort::receive_kill`, at its own level's decay; the session applies it through D1's leveling (below);
- **loot**: rolled at the death; with items the corpse gets its three loot components (below) and its rights are the fighters' pawns; a kill with nothing to give gets none (retail: the first Lancer of capture 552);
- **the corpse closes** (`registry.remove`: every connection that holds it closes the channel, Destroyed) 15.0 s after the death with nothing to loot (retail: 7 of 7), 4.0 s after its loot was emptied (retail: 11 of 11, 3.97 to 4.08 s), and 120 s after the death when nobody takes its loot [modelled: no capture shows one];
- **the respawn**: the NPC's spawn point makes it again `respawn_time` (the instance's) after the death, with a new actor id, while the corpse may still stand (retail: 178.7 to 181.0 s for 180 s, 1.07 and 1.47 s for 1.0 +- 0.2); the variance is uniform +- the instance's [modelled shape], a data NPC whose instance says 0 takes 60 s [modelled], `NoRespawn` never respawns, `AllDead` is treated as `Refill` [modelled]. `PlayerWorld::respawn_npc` rebuilds it from the area loader's `SpawnRecipe`: the recipe's data (level, name, tables, home, respawn, spawn point) over a fresh `instantiate`, whose controller, InteractableComponent and CollisionCylinder are the new block's own (the E1 review's A1: the first generation's cylinder stayed in the respawn, so the registry refused it while the corpse still held that GUID and the spawn point never refilled, and once the corpse was gone the first hit exported it under the new actor: the fix-3 disconnect); a respawn the registry refuses is logged.

**`world/corpse.h`** adds, in the update that follows the death, the three dynamic subobjects retail sends on the corpse's channel: a `ULootComponent` (OwnerGuid, `CosmeticData.TopRarity` = (quality - 1) / 20 of the best item, 7 once emptied), a `UItemStorageComponent` (the loot container the window's events name) and a `UInteractableComponent` (`ICD_DefaultLoot`; its state 2 to the players that may loot, 0 once emptied), and points the NPC's `UNPCSecondaryInfo` at them. The blocks are the retail ones (capture 552, packet 2799, channel 127: `spawns/corpse_exports.h`, generated by `tools/capture/gen_corpse_exports.py --check`) with the players' guids and the new objects' ids put in; a dynamic subobject cannot be taken off a client, so a corpse keeps them until its channel closes. `add_pile` makes the same for a dead player's loot pile (`APlayerLootPile`, capture 758).

**The loot window (libaocnet, `protocol/repl/storage_rpc.h`).** `ServerInteractWithInteractableComponent` on the corpse's component is answered with `ClientReceiveLootStoragePayload(LootActor = the NPC's actor, NewStorageData, LootAllocationMethods)`: one slot per item and quality, `bClientRetrieveOnly` 1, each slot's `bCheckItemLootPermission` 1 with the looting player's CharacterGuid, allocation {Default 1, the rest 0}; the client takes with `ServerRequestLootStorageEvents` (type-3 events: the container's slot as the source, the bag as the target) and is answered, as retail does, with `ClientReceiveStorageEvent` removing the stack from the container, then the add to the bag, and `ClientRemoveListenerFromStorage` once nothing is left. `test_repl_loot` decodes every loot RPC of capture 552 and checks the builders reproduce them bit for bit (48 checks). **Retail's Add events carry an optional struct the codec does not decode (the research found it in 9 delete and chest events): the adds here are plain ones, unproven in game.**

**The session (`session/player_session.cpp`).**
- `apply_kill` applies a kill's Adventuring experience through D1 (the experience pays the death debt first: D1's rule; a level-up runs the whole level-up path), updates the XPTracker and logs it; the Weapon and NodeActivity experience is rolled and not applied.
- `open_loot` / `on_loot_request`: a corpse is opened only by a player with rights (a pile by anyone), within `kInteractRangeCm` and with the actor open on its connection; a take is checked event by event (the container is the corpse's, the slot holds a stack not taken, the item and instance guid are that stack's, no slot twice, at most `kMaxLootTakes` = 16 events); the bag takes each stack or leaves it in the corpse (a full bag changes nothing and the window stays); loot requests have their own budget (`kLootBurst` 8, then one per 100 ms) and are refused without a window, for a corpse that is gone or too far.
- **The player's death** (`check_death` after every ability step): `bIsDead`, Health and Mana 0, `ClientSetLastDeathLocation` (the pawn), `ClientRestart` and `ClientSetCameraMode` twice (the PlayerController: the retail death frame), the experience debt (D1's rule: 2 % of the level's span, `leveling::with_death_debt`) with an XPTracker update, and the PvE loss: about half (rounded up) of every stack of an `Item.Resource*` tagged item of the bag goes into a loot pile where the player fell (public loot, 600 s, 4 s once emptied), the stacks cut by `ClientReceiveStorageEvent`s. A dead player's triggers, hit requests and interactions are ignored (`Refusal::Dead`), nothing regenerates, and the combat world drops it as a target. `RequestServerRespawnChoices` is answered with `SendClientRespawnChoices` (one choice: the respawn record the research found, 6064632337807835941, the same as the home); `SendServerRespawnChoice` with that record brings the player back at `PlayerWorld::respawn_spot` (the nearest attunable respawn shrine in the area, the data's `AttuneableCharacterRespawn` placements, else the login spot) with full Health and Mana, the move checker teleported with it; any other record is answered with the choices again, marked invalid, and the player stays dead. Choices of a living player are ignored.
- Switches: `--no-loot` (`AOC_NO_LOOT`: no experience, no loot, no respawn), `--no-respawn`, `--no-death-loss`, `--corpse-seconds`, `--loot-corpse-seconds`, `--loot-chance-mult` (each with an `AOC_` variable; the last multiplies the chance of every percent loot draw, never a quantity: the fix batch's section above).

**Modelled, not retail** (no capture shows the number or the rule): the selection algorithms' draws; an unlooted corpse's 120 s and a pile's 600 s; the 60 s default respawn, the variance's shape and `AllDead`; the death debt's 2 %; the loss of `Item.Resource*` stacks only (the Glint certificates carry `item.resource.cert`, so half of them is lost too), rounded up; the loot's luck is 0; the respawn choice offers one record; the Weapon and NodeActivity experience is not applied; `ClientUpdateItemDurability` on death is skipped.

**Unproven until a run:** the loot window as the client draws it (the plain Add events, the interaction through the corpse's new component), the death frame's effect on the client (the camera, the respawn dialog), the pile's open, and what the client does with the respawn teleport.

**What else changed.** An ability's activation requirement for a cast aimed at another actor is evaluated with the target (`AbilityPipeline::eval_requirement`: the distance between the two, the relation, `IsApplicableTarget(GetTarget(), ...)` which answers for the target when there is one); gameplay tags are matched without regard to case in the stat engine, the Go reference and the hit rules (they are FNames: Summoner_Amplify's `"pet.summon"` is the pet effect's `Pet.Summon`; the shared corpus was regenerated).

**Tests.** `test_worldd_rewards` (32: the tables, the algorithms, the drop rate, the decay), `test_worldd_aftermath` (44: kill, loot, corpse timing, respawn, hostile calls), `test_repl_loot` (48: the loot RPCs against retail's bits), and in `test_worldd_ability_session`: `test_e1_whole_fight` (aggro, a hit, the bite, 14 kills, the experience, the loot window, hostile requests with and without the window, a spam of forty, the take, the events' order, the corpse's 4 s close, hits on a dead target, a far one, a forged one and the player's own cylinder), `test_e1_loot_with_full_bag`, `test_e1_player_death` (the frame, the debt, the loss and the pile, the choices and an invalid one, the respawn, taking the pile back) and `test_e1_death_without_loss`. **`test_worldd_ability_npc`** is the acceptance test of the class abilities that need another actor: `capture_npc_casts.h` (`tools/capture/gen_npc_casts.py --check`) holds the 358 NPC-stage class casts of the retail captures (the research counted 361; the generator's rules put 358 here, and the 1,881 weapon swings at a target are counted per weapon in `kCombos`), each replayed in order on a character of the capture's class and level against a fresh creature 3 m away: **347 are accepted, 358 match the captures up to a listed difference, 120 match exactly**; the 11 refusals are listed (charges or cooldown a talent changed, a target's effect, a vessel); the amounts are a distribution (ours over retail's: median 0.13 over 188 hits, the same stat mods in every one; the caster has no gear and no talents the captures do not name, and a mace alone is a factor of 2.4); what is left is listed per cast in `ability_npc_known.inc` with its reason (effects on a creature are not run, reactions are not followed, area hits at a ground point, allies, shields that follow damage, talents). The creature of a cast whose captured target is another actor of unknown level is a level-1 Wolf (34.9 Health since the E1 review's stat fix, a fifth less than before), and most Mage casts kill it: three Firebolt casts whose later hits (the second projectile hit, the Burning) retail confirmed on a target that survived are listed with that reason (`R_KILL`); `-v` prints the creature's Health after each cast.

**Not done:** pets and summons (the summon abilities run and cost, no pet appears; the pet-requiring ones run with the pet's effect given to the caster), effects on NPCs other than shields (damage over time, snares, debuffs: part of the listed differences), reactions (Vengeance, Reflect), Weapon and NodeActivity experience, group loot, the NPCs' own loot flags (`bPublicLoot` of a Blueprint), humanoid NPCs.

### The player's attacks, the weapon swap and the client's clock (plan E1, stage 3 fix; an uncommitted draft)

The user's in-game run of 2026-10-06 (`logs/run_20261006_162218`): the NPCs fought well, but no ability and no weapon swing hurt one, Mock healed the caster, the weapon swap did nothing, and every cooldown ended about 3 s early (Jest showed 5 s of its 8). Four roots, each with a test that reproduces it (`test_worldd_ability_session`: `test_e1_inline_target_session`, `test_e1_clock_resync`; `test_worldd_items_session`: the swap test; `test_repl_ability_rpc`: the inline targets); the spec's "Settled in E1, stage 3 fix" has the evidence.

**A target named in place.** The client has no NetGUID for a component the server never exported, so it names an NPC's CollisionCylinder *in place* (ObjectId 1, the export flags, the NPC's GUID as the outer, the name "CollisionCylinder") in `ServerTriggerAbility`'s Targets and in `RequestHitVerification_Server`'s HitComponents, the form it uses for its own cylinder in 8 of the e2e capture's 16 Scepter combos (`libs/aocnet` `InlineObject`). worldd resolved only the GUID form: the run's log says "a hit request for ... is found nobody" and "the hit ... (the client asked): it found nobody" for every cast at a Wolf. `parse_trigger_ability` now keeps the inline targets (`TriggerAbility::inline_targets`), `PlayerSession::actors_of` resolves them by their outer (the actor; `CombatWorld::actor_of_object` also takes a component id), and an inline reference to the caster's own pawn or cylinder counts as its own in a hit request. The confirmation's HitComp stays the cylinder's id (the actor id + 24, retail's pattern).

**Mock's heal.** Mock and Jest list the same two projectiles (key 1 Mockery: damage and Demoralized; key 2 Quick Wit: the heal and Glee), both aimed at the target. A projectile's hits land on the actor it reaches, and on the caster only when the cast aimed at nobody else; with the target unresolved, the pipeline fired both at the caster and Bard_QuickWit_Apply (Self | Ally) healed it. Now (`AbilityPipeline::step_hits`) a projectile aimed at other actors does not reach the caster, and a projectile reaches the caster at all only when the ability's ApplicableTargets names Self, Ally, Party or Raid (`Cast::self_reachable`): an enemy-only ability whose target the world does not have is cast at the air [R: capture 40 line 295 confirms Bard_Mockery_Apply alone for a Mock at another actor]. No hit of a projectile names both the caster and enemies (the 7 that do are direct or zone hits). `test_worldd_ability_npc`: 358 of 358 match, 121 exact (120 before); the two Mock "extra Bard_QuickWit_Apply / Bard_Glee_Tick" lines left `ability_npc_known.inc`.

**The weapon swap.** Retail answers `ServerSetUseRangedWeapon(bUseRanged)` with one chain on the pawn's channel (capture 403, all 8 swaps, 4 to 6 packets later): an 18-bit `UPlayerCombatInfo` block with `bIsUsingRangedWeapon` = the request, the AbilityComponent's K/R update, the StatsComponent's update (the weapon ranks' effects and stats) and OwnerInfo's `WeaponSkillLineId`. worldd sent all but the first; the client's weapon in hand follows that flag (`PlayerBootstrap::set_using_ranged_weapon`, set in `on_set_ranged` before the pawn's update; with nothing worn in the slot the flag still follows and the combo stays).

**The client's clock.** In the client (UAoCGameInstance; exe `0x146aa2ba0` = ClientSetTime's handler → `0x145efcf70`, the core-ticker delegate `0x145efe650` registered in `UAoCGameInstance::Init`, `0x145ef0fc0` = GetSynchronizedTime): `ClientSetTime` stores NewSynchronizedTime and keeps the value before it; every frame adds `FApp::DeltaTime` to the stored value; GetSynchronizedTime is the larger of the two. The join's `ClientSetTime` is processed in the first frame after the client's LoadMap (3.26 s in the run: the client log's frame 531 loads from 26.15 to 29.41, frame 532 takes the join), and the core ticker then adds that frame's whole delta after the set, so the client's clock ran ~3.3 s ahead of every cooldown and effect stamp for the whole session: Jest's 8 s showed as 5, Get Off The Stage was pressed 3 s before its end and refused three times ("out of charges"). Retail repeats `ClientSetTime` every 120.0 s (pkt#23, 5128, 8807 ...), which corrects it within two minutes; worldd sent it once. Now `PlayerBootstrap::set_time(world_time)` goes out 1 s and 5 s after the phase-in (the loading frames are over) and every 120 s (`PlayerSession::kTimeSyncInterval`), with the server's world time at the send. A correction downward is a short freeze of the client's clock (the max of the two values), upward is immediate. **Unproven until a run:** the frames after the phase-in are short (an over-count of one frame's delta remains), and the freeze passes while the player is still phasing in.

### The tooltip as the truth (plan E1, stage 3 fix, Part C; an uncommitted draft)

The user's rule: each ability's in-game tooltip is the truth for its numbers and behaviour. The client builds it in `USkillToolTipWidget::BuildFinalTooltip`
(exe `0x1469519e0`; the description's tokens in `0x146995490`) from the ability record's `AbilityDescription` template and the record's expressions evaluated
for the player: the header lines are the charges (`CooldownCharges`, printed when over 1), each enabled cost group's stat costs (about `ceil`), the range
(`CustomTooltipRange`, else `AreaTargetRange` for a clicked AoE, else the targeting record's `ValidDistance`, else `ValidDistanceExp`; `floor(cm / 100)` m)
and the cast time (the montage's end-notify time over the cast speed: a client-side value; `DynamicCastDuration` for two abilities). The skill tooltip prints
no cooldown line: the cooldown is the data's `Cooldown`. **A hit's number is a percent:** `$hit1$` prints the stat mod's `BaseValue` term x 100 with a percent
sign (the formatter's suffix carries `%nostat`), "healing for 150% Healing", "Hit 1 - 53% Slash"; the amount is the stat mod's formula over the caster's stats.

`tools/capture/gen_tooltips.py` (the research's generator, moved here; `--check`) evaluates that for every ability of every class, the 14 weapon combos, the
general, consumable and profession abilities (255) at level 25 with no gear, no skill and no effects, into `tests/tooltip_key.h`; `test_worldd_tooltips` holds the
runtime to it on a character of each class: `AbilityPipeline::inspect()` runs the very formulas a trigger runs (`resolve_formulas`: the cooldown, the charges,
the enabled costs, the cast time) with nothing cast, `AbilityPipeline::evaluate()` an effect's duration, and the catalog gives the target mask, the hits' terms
(`Catalog::hit_named`) and the effects the ability reaches. **Every cooldown, charge count, evaluable cost, target mask and held behaviour of the 255 is the
tooltip's**, and so is every hit percent the texts state: 109 constant terms equal, 98 formula terms (the weapon combos' `0.533 x EvalFormula(the weapon tree)`,
the songs') are the same text the runtime compiles. The differences are listed in `tests/tooltip_known.inc` with their reason, exact (an unlisted one fails):
the range (the client enforces the tooltip's per ability; the server's one bound, 5,500 cm, covers every tooltip range, the largest being Rogue_DeathsMark's
5,000), the cast time (the montage's), eight costs and two hit numbers only the stat engine or a cast in progress gives (the key states no value; the runtime's
is printed), the hits and effects the texts promise through a REACTION or another ability's hit (the Rogue poisons' statuses, the Mage's Elemental
Empowerment, the pets'), the crowd-control durations whose formula calls `GetEffect` (not in the stat program: the runtime does not apply an effect whose
duration does not run), the three Hunt marks' bare `GetTerm`, Misdirection's dummy hit, and four activation requirements the stat program cannot run
(Ranger_Dash, Sic, the two fishing casts). The backlog has what is left to do.

### Hits that look like hits, the book, and one clock for the fights (plan E1, stage 3 fix 2; an uncommitted draft)

The user's run of 2026-10-06 20:20 (`logs/run_20261006_201738`): the NPCs took damage, but the projectiles did not visibly hit them, no damage number
showed above them, they had no death animation and "instantly respawned in the same spot", and the ranged book did no damage (its swings put the player
in combat and nothing else). Three roots, with the retail answer key of `research/hitfx` (1,562 captures' exports, 4,437 confirmations, 117 deaths, every
weapon swing) and a test for each in `test_worldd_ability_session` (`test_e1_npc_cylinder_export`, `test_e1_one_clock`, `test_e1_ranged_book_at_wolf`):

- **The client could not resolve the target of a hit.** The confirmation's `HitComp` and the ability item's `Targets` named the NPC's collision cylinder by
  a GUID (the actor's + 24) the server had never exported, and the client resolves such a GUID to nothing: its homing projectile had nothing to fly at, the
  confirmation drew no impact, hit reaction or floating number (there is no other wire for them: no impact multicast, no hit-react montage, no hit counter in
  retail; the client draws them from the confirmation and the replicated Health). Retail names the **actor** as the HitComp (7,450 of 7,450 targets), names the
  cylinder in the item's Targets, and exports the cylinder (flags 7, checksum 0xb876932f, the NPC as the outer; it is never in the actor open) in the bunch that
  first names it, until acknowledged. worldd now does the same: `HitTargetOut::component` is the actor, `cylinder` is exported with the confirmation
  (`client_hit_confirmations`'s `extra_exports`) and `ActiveAbilityItem::target_actors` with the AbilityComponent's update (`PlayerBootstrap::npc_component_export`,
  kept per item like the montages), and the client then names the cylinder by its GUID in its requests (`CombatWorld::actor_of_object` takes it). The
  confirmation's `ImpactLocation` is the target's position (retail: median 0 cm off) and `ImpactNormal` a unit vector (modelled: from the target to the caster).
  A stat mod whose value is 0 is left out of the confirmation (retail: the book's FX hit carries none).
- **Two clocks.** The ability runtime counts seconds from the session's join; the combat world and the aftermath are ticked with the server's world time
  (`Endpoint::tick` → `PlayerWorld::tick_world(seconds since worldd started)`), and a player's hit reached `CombatWorld::apply_hit_to_npc` and so
  `CombatWorld::kill` with the runtime's time: the death was stamped 102 s in the past (worldd had run 102 s when the user joined), the corpse's 15 s and the
  spawn point's 40-60 s were over at the next world tick, so the corpse was destroyed at once (no death animation to see) and the respawn appeared in the same
  instant. `WorldHitPort` now translates (the join's world time is the runtime's zero): deaths, shields and the player's in-combat window are on the world's
  clock. The death frame itself is retail's (Health 0 and `bIsDead` in one update, no RPC, no montage: 116 of 117 retail deaths), and the respawn rule holds
  (death + `respawn_time` + the variance, the same spot, a new actor): the data's Wolves here respawn in 20-60 s, the Rabid and Raven in 20-150.
- **The book's damage is not where the draft looked.** `Weapon_Book_Hit_n`'s stat mod is the data's `0` ("here for the primary FX and to be used for tooltips,
  not to do damage"); the damage is `Book_Projectile_Split_Hit_n`, a direct hit no projectile carries, that fires the split projectile when it lands
  (`ab_hit_projectile`, now `HitDef::fires`). Retail resolves a ranged weapon combo's projectile on the server (1,272 book, 77 longbow, 28 shortbow swings:
  not one `RequestHitVerification`) and confirms the step's two records in one confirmation 0.5-1.3 s after the trigger: the FX hit with no StatMods, predicted on
  both sides, and the Split hit with the damage, predicted on neither. The draft fired every projectile row of the combo (ten FX hits, 0 damage) and left the Split
  hits for a request the client never sends. Now `AbilityPipeline::step_hits` fires the projectile of the swing's **step**, the montage the client's AnimationIndex
  names, matched to the projectile hits by the step in their names (`WeaponCombo_RNG_BOOK_03_Proc` → `Weapon_Book_Hit_3+`; `WeaponCombo_Longbow_04+_Montage` →
  `WeaponCombo_Longbow_04+_Proc`; the wand's beam montages fire nothing: the client asks for the beam hits), and with it the step's direct hits that no projectile
  carries but that fire one (the Split hits), as retail's second record. The weapon combos' hits stay in the client's request slots too (a melee swing is the
  client's request, as before).

**Modelled, not retail:** the impact normal's direction; the step matching by name (the shortbow's finisher montages carry no step number: they fall back to
the first swing's projectile) and the book's second-projectile variants (`_4_2`: retail fires it with `_4`; here too, by the shared step). **Not done:**
`StopAnimMontageOnNpc` (retail sends it two packets after the first hit on an NPC that plays an ambient montage), `ServerSetActiveTarget` (the client's
target selection, on the pawn: not answered; retail replies with the player's `CombatInfo.ActiveTargets`), and the client's `ServerUpdateLevelVisibility`
(the three unreadable 550-bit PlayerController bunches of every run: the streaming levels; nothing of combat).

### The cylinder's GUID and the split projectile's origin (plan E1, stage 3 fix 3; an uncommitted draft)

The user's run of 2026-10-06 22:15 (`logs/run_20261006_221422`): the abilities did damage with numbers, the deaths animated, the NPCs aggroed and leashed;
but "casting Maddening Dance disconnected the client", and the book's auto attack still drew no projectile. Two roots, both in the run's own client log,
with the retail confirmations of `research/hitfx` as the answer key; the tests are `test_e1_cylinder_in_own_block`, `test_e1_zone_abilities_export_rule`
and the Source checks of `test_e1_ranged_book_at_wolf` in `test_worldd_ability_session`:

- **The cylinder's GUID was outside the actor's block.** The client logged `NetGuidMismatch: Outer mismatch. Path: CollisionCylinder, Outer: 16790986,
  Expected: 16791004, NetGUID: 16791010` and tore the world down, 12 ms after the server's kill confirmation of a Wolf (22:18:53.169 / .181). An NPC's block is
  nine GUIDs (`spawns::instantiate`: the actor, its AI controller at + 2, a slot at + 4, six components at + 6 .. + 16); the draft named its cylinder as the
  actor's **+ 24**, which is whatever was minted next. A Wolf had respawned (16790986 .. 16791002), the Lament's zone actor was minted right after it (16791004;
  its LingeringEffectMovementComp at + 6 = 16791010 = the Wolf's + 24) and opened to the client, and the first hit on that Wolf exported 16791010 as the Wolf's
  "CollisionCylinder": UE's `FNetGUIDCache::RegisterNetGUIDFromPath_Client` refuses a GUID it holds under another path or outer. For the area's NPCs the same
  GUID is the next NPC's AlignmentComponent; the earlier hits survived because the NPCs' mint-order neighbours were not open on the client (luck). Retail mints
  the cylinder at the AlignmentComponent's - 2 = the actor's + 4 (211 of 211 captured exports): so does worldd now (the + 4 slot is the cylinder,
  `NpcInfo::collision_cylinder`; `ActorRegistry` indexes it like the interactable, `CombatWorld::hit_component_of` and `actor_of_object` read the registry and do
  no arithmetic on ids). Maddening Dance itself was a coincidence: the client evaluated its expression 19 ms before the failure, and its trigger never reached
  the server. The test mints two Wolves back to back, opens both, hits the first with the acks held, and reads every export bunch the way the client does (the
  GUID, the flags, the outer's entry, the path, the checksum): no GUID with a path may come twice under another path or outer. The all-classes test casts every
  dance and lingering-zone ability of every class (24; the Bard's four dances open their zone actor, as do four others; the rest need a target or a resource)
  with a Wolf hit while the zone stands, and checks the same rule over every chain sent.
- **The split projectile had no origin.** 13 ms after every book confirmation the client logged `UCombatHelper::GetTransform(): Failed to find origin location.
  EAbilitySystemOrigin: EAbilitySystemOrigin::Source, Owner PlayerPawn, Instigator PlayerPawn, Source , Target CollisionCylinder, ImpactLocation ...` and
  `UAoCAbilityComponent::SpawnProjectile(): Failed to find spawn transform for projectile` / `FireProjectileAtTarget(): Invalid projectile`: the Split hit
  (`Book_Projectile_Split_Hit_n`) fires the split projectile from the hit's **Source**, which worldd left empty (and it named the split projectile as the hit's
  record). Retail's Split confirmation names the target actor the book's projectile landed on as the Source and the projectile that landed
  (`Weapon_Book_Projectile_n`) as the SourceRecordGuid, one target each [R: capture 45 packet 148; 1,289 of 1,289 split confirmations name the target; the
  2,177 confirmations of hits a projectile carries (the book's FX hit, the longbow's, the wand's, Quick Wit's) have Source 0]. Now `HitSource::source_is_target`
  makes one confirmation per target with that target as the Source (`HitConfirm::source`), and the paired hit's record is the projectile that landed. The
  Target was resolved already (the cylinder export of fix 2 works): only the origin was missing.

**Unproven until a run:** whether the book's main projectile (the client's own, spawned by its montage and predicted on both sides) is drawn: the client logged
nothing about it. **Not done:** the book's confirmation timing (a fixed 340 ms after the cost step; retail's 0.5-1.3 s after the trigger is the montage's launch
plus the flight).

### Mounts: the data, the formats and the items (the mounts milestone, stage 1; an uncommitted draft)

The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-mounts-design.md`; stage 1 is what the next stages stand on (summoning and desummoning, riding, flying and swimming come after it). Nothing a player sees changes yet except the GM mounts vendor's stock.

- **content.db version 11** (`content/content_db.h`: `kMinContentcVersion` 11, `ContentDb::mount_data()` reads the four tables whole: `MountData` with `find(item)`, `stat_at(owner level, stat)`, `ability_with_role("summon" | "dash" | "dismount_elevated" | "dismount_grounded")`, `road(tier)`, `dash()`): the 101 mount items with their Blueprint, activation, speed percentages and `health_factor()`, the `Mount_Base` stats at an owner's level (MaxHealth 4875 at 25), the 34 abilities a rider may use or that dismount him, and the road and dash effects (`services/README.md`, "Mounts"). The tables are read when asked for, not at open: a file without them still opens and `mount_data` says to rebuild it.
- **The GM mounts vendor sells all 101 mount items** (`vendor/gm_vendor.h`: `kMountsVendorName`, `GmVendors::mounts_added()`): the `PTR_ONLY_Vendor_Mounts` def lists 20 ground mounts; `GmVendors::load` adds the other 81 to its stock, so a purchase cart naming any mount item is accepted and granted free (the stock of the "GM Mounts" NPC shows 101). **What the client can choose is its own window**: it draws the vendor from its own copy of the def, so it offers the 20 (the player can buy a horse for the first test), and the 81 others are reachable only by a cart the client's window does not build. The Eagle Gryphon (`Mount_Flying_PTR_ONLY`) is in the client's own `PTR_ONLY_Vendor_Siege` def, so "GM Siege" sells it; of the 101 mounts 44 are listed by some vendor def of the client's data (`PTR_ONLY_Vendor_Mounts` 20, `PTR_ONLY_Vendor_Siege` 1, `Node_Mayor_Vendor` 23, `June21SpotTestMounts_Vendor` 3, with overlaps) and none of the other 57 (the Gryphon and Dawnbreaker flyers, the aquatic mounts ...) is in a window the game would show. The log says it at start (`[area] the GM mounts vendor sells all 101 mount items`).
- **A mount item keeps its state** (`svc::InventoryItem`: `persisted_health`, `persisted_mana`, `lock_expiration`; `items::to_instance` / `item_of`): retail's login shows two persisted stats (Health 108986352686712, Mana 109183576135288) and a LockExpiration (Unix seconds) on its 24 mount items, and the character service stores them (`services/README.md`, "What a mount item persists", migration 0007, adds columns only). `to_instance` writes them as the FItemInstance's stats in that order and its lock, `item_of` reads them back (a stat that is neither is ignored, a negative or NaN one refuses the item); a persisted Health of 0 is a Health (a dead mount), not an absent one. Nothing sets them yet: stage 2 summons and stage 3 saves.
- **The formats** (`libs/aocnet/README.md`): `UMountComponent` (generated), `FLocalizedMessage` (the callout codec), the mount donors of captures 911, 898 and 871 (`spawns/mount_donors.h`, from `tools/capture/gen_mount.py`: one open per kind of mount, in the tables `spawns/captured_spawns.h` reads for `area_spawns.h`, for stage 2) and `mapc mount-speeds` (`dist/content/mount_speeds.tsv`: **the speeds the move checker needs come from the Blueprints**: a ground mount's `MaxRunSpeed`, a flyer's `MaxFlySpeed`, an aquatic mount's `MaxSwimSpeed`; worldd reads the file at start, `--mount-speeds`: stage 2 uses the run speed for the desummon's run, stage 3 the rest).

### Mounts: summoning and desummoning (the mounts milestone, stage 2; an uncommitted draft)

A mount item from the bag now summons a mount that stands in front of the player, and the item (or a desummon) sends it away; **riding is stage 3** (the mount cannot be mounted yet: its interaction does nothing). The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-mounts-design.md` section 3.

- **The actor** (`spawns/mount_spawn.h`, `world::ActorKind::Mount`): `MountBook` (the data's mount tables and the three captured donors, one per kind: ground 911, aquatic 898, flying 871; `PlayerWorld::mounts()`) makes the actor from the donor of the item's kind. NetGUIDs follow the 93 captured exports: the actor A, its CollisionCylinder A+2, the subobjects A+4 .. A+14 (Alignment, InteractInfo, BaseCharacterInfo, CombatInfo, AbilityComponent, StatsComponent), A+18 the mount component, A+20 the interactable component (A+16 is never exported). The values that are the instance's own are the owner's (its CharacterGuid in the alignments, the creature-owner guid, the interaction state's maps and input state; its name; its pawn as the Instigator), the mount's own CharacterGuid, the item's guid and `bIsSummoning` 1 with a DissolveTimer, its place and yaw, Health and MaxHealth (`Mount_Base_HP` at the owner's level times the item's factor: 3,572 at level 20, 24,375 for a level-25 flyer) and the owner's level as StealthLevel; everything else is the capture's. A Blueprint a capture exported (the three donors') keeps its retail export; any other mount, the GM Eagle Gryphon among them, gets the class default object by its path with checksum 0 and a static NetGUID worldd mints (the same path always the same one), as E1's generic NPCs do. The actor has `never_dormant` and an `owner`: only its owner's connection is sent it.
- **The controller** (`session/mounts.h`, one per session): retail's timeline. Summon (`ServerItemUsed` + `ServerTriggerAbility(Summon_Mount, the item)`, one packet): +80 ms the Summon_Mount ability item (with the montage) and the bank-18 bar (`ClientClearActionbarsForInstigators([Vehicle, Mount], 18)` + the dash on the Mount instigator: slot 0 `MountAbility_Sprint`, an aquatic mount's slot 1 `MountAbility_Sprint_AllowSwimming`), +104 ms the open 575 cm in front of the player on its yaw (on the ground when the world has one), `bIsSummoning` off 0.47 s after the open, +1.54 s the item again without its montage. The item is not consumed, and the item's use alone answers nothing (no storage event, no resync). Refusal (no ground within 250 cm of the player's feet at the spot, a lock on the item, a dead player): no actor, the item at +105 ms with a callout on the PlayerController ("Cannot summon mount here.", `CalloutQueueReplication`, type 1, point size 32), the item's end at +1.6 s, the callout taken off 3 s after it was shown. Desummon (`ServerItemUsed` + the PlayerController's `ServerDesummonMount`): `bIsDesummoning`, the DissolveTimer, invulnerable and untargetable at +0.1 s, the mount runs forward along its yaw at its Blueprint's run speed (`dist/content/mount_speeds.tsv`, `--mount-speeds`, else 600 cm/s times the item's ground percentage; `ReplicatedMovement` every 67 ms) from +0.2 s, `bIsDesummoning` and the movement input off at +3.1 s, the actor leaves the registry at +3.2 s (every view closes its channel, Destroyed). **One mount per player**: a summon while one is out (or being summoned) desummons it and summons nothing (retail: another mount item used while one is out is a desummon of the old one only); a summon while it dissolves, or on top of a cast, is ignored (and the client's instance ended). A damaging hit on the player BEFORE the open cancels the cast (`MountSummon_Cancel`: the item ends at once, nothing spawns); once the mount has appeared a hit never desummons it (the user's rule, stage 4). The mount leaves with its owner's session.
- **The session** (`session/player_session.cpp`): a mount item's `ServerItemUsed` is noted and answered with nothing; `ServerTriggerAbility` of Summon_Mount goes to the controller before the generic pipeline (which refuses an ability that names an item): the item is the bag's item at the call's slot (a trigger naming a sword, an empty slot, the equipment or no item gets `ClientEndAbility` and nothing else); `ServerDesummonMount` takes the item budget; the controller's effects (the pawn's ability item, the bar, the callouts) are applied and sent in retail's order (the pawn's update first, then the PlayerController). `bootstrap::PlayerBootstrap` gained `mount_bar`, `callout_add` and `callout_remove`.
- **Tests**: `test_worldd_mounts` (the open of each donor rebuilt from its capture's own values is the capture's open but for the road effect, the ground object, the capture owner's guild and the effects of its place; the GUID layout; archetypes; the owner's guid nowhere left of the capture's; all 101 items make an actor that opens; the controller's whole timeline, refusals, one mount per player, spam, the cancels, the desummon and its run, a mount that left the world, the session's end) and `test_worldd_mounts_session` (the same on a connection with a fake client: the packets the client receives, nothing consumed, the budget and the hostile requests, and the golden replays: **captures 911, 896, 898 and 871 (the summon), 874, 893 and 898 (the desummon), 876 and 870 (the refusal)**, `tests/capture_mount_session.h` from `tools/capture/gen_mount_session.py`: the item worldd shows is retail's but for the instance, the animation counter and the instigator, the bar is retail's two calls, the flags and the run carry retail's handles, the callout is retail's but for its key and creation time, and each time lies within retail's range).
- **Modelled, not retail**: the height rule of the refusal and the lock rule (the exe names `MountSummonInvalidLocation` and `MaxMountSummonHeightDifference`; no capture has a value); the cancel by damage (no capture shows a hit during a summon); the run's speed before a speed table (the Blueprint's run speed is the client's); the capture owner's guild guid is cleared from the inherited alignments; the open has no `ReplicatedBasedMovement` (retail names the ground object, which worldd has no export for); no road effect and no inherited zone effect (retail's 898 and 871 carried `Lawless_Buff` and `Node_Factory_Default_Character` of their owners' place); a mount always starts at full Health (stage 3 saves it on the item).
- **Stage 3's seams**: `MountController::mount()` / `phase()` / `owner()`; the actor's `owner` and `never_dormant`; `ActorView::channel_of`; the interactable component and `world::allow_inputs`; `PlayerBootstrap::mount_bar` (also what mount-up sends); `ServerDismountInteraction` and the mounted desummon (two `ClientRestart` in one packet, capture 893) are not handled (nothing can be mounted yet); the actor view still sends the owner a mount's `ReplicatedMovement`, which retail does not once it is possessed.

### Mounts: riding, dismounting, the dash and mounted combat (the mounts milestone, stage 3; an uncommitted draft)

A mount can be ridden (`session/riding.h`, `session/mounts.h`, `bootstrap/mount_ride.cpp`). The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-mounts-design.md` section 4.

- **The controlled actor**: a session controls one actor, the pawn or a mount, with that actor's NetGUIDs, channel and schema, and its own `MoveChecker` reset at each possession (the client's TimeStamp restarts near 0). Bunches on the controlled actor's channel are routed, not dropped; the owner is sent no `ReplicatedMovement` of a possessed mount; position saving and relevancy follow the controlled actor.
- **Mount up** (`ServerInteractWithInteractableComponent` on the mount, within 250 cm): retail's three packets (capture 910): `ClientRestart(mount)`, camera mode 204, the mount's RemoteRole/Owner/Controller/PlayerState, the rider attached at `MountSocket` (`MountPtr`/`RiderPtr`), then `ServerAcknowledgePossession`. **Dismount** (`ServerDismountInteraction`, 912, 914): `ClientRestart(own pawn)`, the detachment beside the mount at saddle height, `MountPtr` cleared; the mount stays. A mounted desummon sends two `ClientRestart` in one packet (893).
- **Movement**: limits from the Blueprint's run speed (`mount_speeds.tsv`) times MoveSpeedMult (959); the dash (`MountAbility_Sprint`: Cooldowns 30 s, 5 s effect, x1.35, the hit confirmation; 906).
- **Roads were OFF in stage 3 (stage 4 turned them on, below)**: `world/roads.h` (`dist/content/roads.tsv`, read at start) adds and removes `Road_T0..T4_Speed_Mount` as the controlled mount enters and leaves a road, and is tested with synthetic roads, but mapc skips spline meshes and no extractor exists, so there is no roads file and a mount rides at its own speed everywhere.
- **Combat while mounted**: the ability filter (`bUsableWhileMounted` works, `bAutoDismount` dismounts first, the rest is refused); hits aimed at a mounted rider land on the mount's Health; the mount's death dismounts the rider, closes the mount and saves the item's Health and LockExpiration (`Inventory::update_mount`); a resummon starts from the saved Health. The player's death dismounts and desummons; a relog while mounted comes back dismounted at the mount's last position.
- **Tests**: `test_worldd_mounts_ride` (goldens from `tools/capture/gen_mount_ride.py` -> `tests/capture_mount_ride.h`: 910, 912, 914, 959, 906, 893; hostile requests; the move checker per possession; mounted combat; relog).
- **Not modelled**: mule saddle bags. (Roads, flying, diving and swimming: stage 4, below.)
- **The item's ability (stage 3 fix 1, after the first in-game test)**: a mount item used on the client runs through the AbilityComponent's `TriggerAbility`, which sends `ServerTriggerAbility` only when the ability is *loaded* there; otherwise it shows "Ability not loaded" (`EAbilityFailureType::Unloaded`) and sends nothing, and worldd saw `ServerItemUsed` alone. The component loads what `ReplicatedRecordsToLoad` lists, and retail lists the ability of every item carried (capture 1198: `Summon_Mount` among 92 records, in no `KnownAbilities`; capture 821: a bought item's record is appended in the packet after the storage event). So `bootstrap::carried_item_abilities` puts a mount item's `Summon_Mount` into the join's records (before the static tail), `PlayerSession::sync_item_abilities` appends it (one pawn update) when a mount item reaches the bag or the body during the session, and the class and weapon rebuild keeps it. Only mounts are modelled; consumables' abilities are not (their uses work from `ServerItemUsed` alone). `test_worldd_mounts_session` models the client's gate (no trigger without the record).
- **Stage 3 fix 2 (the re-test of 2026-10-08: ground mounts work now; four things were left)**:
  - **Flyers never summoned: the client says nothing and sends nothing.** `ActivateItem` runs `ValidateItemUse` before it sends `ServerItemUsed`, and the item's ACTIVATION ARCHETYPE record carries a requirement. The 44 flying items that activate by `CastAbility` have the archetype `Mount_Mayor_<node>` with the requirement expression `(Node_IsPlayerCitizenOfNode(GetPlayer(), <the node>) && Node_IsPlayerMayor(GetPlayer())) || EvalFormula(CheckMayor_OfTestNode) > 0`: they are mayor rewards. For anyone else the expression is false, the failure reason is empty, and the client returns without a word (the exe: `0x6c57c00` evaluates `OptionalExpression`, then `OptionalFailureReason`; the user's worldd log holds no use of a flyer at all). The two `Mount_Flying_PTR_ONLY` items (the Eagle Gryphon, Blueprint `Mount_Flying_Gryphon_5_Armored`) activate by `SummonMount` with the plain `Mount` archetype and no requirement: they summon like a horse. The GM starter set now grants those two as its flyers (and no mayor flyer), and `GrantMissingStarterMounts` is a top-up, so a character that had the old set gets them at the next start (`services/README.md`). Faking a node, a citizenship and a mayoralty (the client reads them from replicated node data, `APlayerCharacter`'s citizen node and the game state's node table) is not done.
  - **The rider stood on its mount: the open never exported the rider's AnimBP.** The mount's `InteractableComponent` names the AnimBP the rider's mesh is put on (`InteractionInputData.AttachmentAnimation`, a class of `/Game/Characters/Animations/Mounts_Rider/<family>/`), and every retail open exports that class (911 `ABP_PC_MNT_Swiftclaw_C`, 898 `ABP_PC_MNT_Tidesnapper_C`, 871 `ABP_PC_MNT_Dawnbreaker_C`, each with its package, flags 5, checksum 0). The donors kept the NetGUID but worldd never sent the export, so the client could not resolve the class, the rider kept its on-foot AnimBP, and `ABaseCharacter::InitAttachedRiderAnimProxy` (it needs the mesh's anim instance to be an `AoCRiderAnimInstance`) logged `Unexpected invalid anim instance when initializing rider anim proxy` at every mount-up (`game_client.txt`, `LogAoC_Mounts`). Now `tools/mapc mount-speeds` also writes `dist/content/mount_riders.tsv` (each Blueprint's interaction point, `blueprint rider_anim`, along its parents: the terror bird -> Swiftclaw, the Shoalskipper -> Tidesnapper, the Dawnbreaker -> Dawnbreaker, as the captures), `MountBook::load_riders` reads it (`--mount-riders`, `AOC_MOUNT_RIDERS`), and `make()` names the class in the interaction data and puts its export (the class and its package) into `WorldActor::open_exports`. Without the file a mount takes its kind's donor AnimBP. The log line of a summon names the AnimBP.
  - **Sprint carried into the mount and drained its Stamina.** The Sprint is the pawn's ability and the moves of a rider were counted as the pawn's moving. `AbilityRuntime::set_mounted`: getting on ends a running Sprint as a Sprint ends (its items removed, MoveSpeedMult 1.0, the StaminaRegeneration item, `ClientEndAbility` at once) without the 1 s exertion pause, and a Rest; while mounted no Sprint, Dodge or Rest starts (`Refusal::Mounted`, answered with `ClientEndAbility`), the pawn counts as standing and Stamina regenerates; getting off clears the flag (the Sprint does not come back by itself). The session calls it in `mount_up` and `dismount`. The retail captures show nothing on the pawn's stats at mount-up or dismount (910, 912, 914: the capturer stood still), so the end is the Sprint's own end (capture 745).
  - **Every mount carried the PvP flag of its donor's owner.** A retail mount's `InheritedAlignments` are its owner's alignments (911, 871, 872 and 917: Player, NonCombatant, the owner's Instigator, PVPSuppressed_Perceiver, plus a NodeCitizen and a GuildMember where the owner had them). The donors' sets belong to the players who were captured: 898's owner stood in a Lawless zone and was a `Combatant` (the PvP flag alignment; its effects hold `Lawless_Buff`), and the old code only zeroed the guids of the other instances, so an aquatic mount of ours carried a Combatant and every mount a node and a guild its owner had not. `make()` now takes `MountParams::owner_alignments` (the session passes the pawn's `Alignments` as they are at the summon; unset: the unflagged player's four) as the `InheritedAlignments` and writes the mount's own `Alignments` as the one Instigator group of the mount and its owner. For the ground donor (911) the only differences from the owner's set were the node and guild instances; no other PvP state (`bPvPForceFlag`, `bInPVPCombat`, `InPvpSuppressionArea`, a flagged effect) is in any donor.
  - `test_worldd_abilities` (`test_mounted`), `test_worldd_mounts` (`test_alignments_are_the_owners`, `test_rider_anim`), `test_worldd_mounts_session` (the AnimBP export on the wire, the mount's alignments are the pawn's) and `test_worldd_mounts_ride` (`test_sprint_does_not_ride`).
- **Stage 3 fix 3 (the re-test of 2026-10-08 ~21:00: the dash could not be used; worldd's log held no dash trigger at all, so the client sent none)**: the cause is the pawn's `InteractInfo.MountablePet`. A bank-18 slot whose Instigator is Mount is resolved by the client through the player character's `GetMountablePet()` (`AAoCPlayerController::UseActionbarSlot` 0x6b12700 and `CanEverUseActionbarSlot` 0x6a91a10 take `GetActionbarInstigator` 0x6aba180: Player = the character, Pet, Mount = virtual slot 0xe28 = the Blueprint-callable `GetMountablePet`, Vehicle = `GetMount`); with no pet the instigator is null, the gate is closed and the press does nothing, with no message. Retail sets the replicated RepNotify property `UCharacterSecondaryInfo.MountablePet` of the pawn's InteractInfo to the summoned mount in the packet of the `Summon_Mount` ability item and the bank-18 bar (871 packet 210, 898 packet 158; the login capture 1198 packet 1706 shows it on another player) and to 0 in the packet of the mount's close (898 packet 601); the older captures (906, 910, 911: 3 February) predate the property, which is why the dash worked in them without it, and worldd never set it. `MountController::Effects` now carries `pet_changed`/`pet` (the mount's actor guid when it opens, an invalid guid when it closes, dies or leaves the world), `PlayerBootstrap::pawn_mountable_pet` sets the property (the InteractInfo is added at the first summon instead of the first ride: `ensure_interact_info`) and the session sends the pawn's update. The mount's `KnownAbilities` (at the mount-up) are the second half of the gate (`UAoCAbilityComponent::KnowsAbility` 0x6bb3540). Test: `test_worldd_mounts_ride` (`test_dash_gate`: a model of the client's gate on what was delivered). Also checked: the Scorpix rider AnimBP (`ABP_PC_MNT_VerdantVoyager`) is what the Blueprint names (`Mount_Grounded_Scorpix_0`'s own `InteractableComponent` template; the Scorpion, Spider, DrillSpider and Croc Blueprints share it; the Lizard import in the package is an unused leftover), not a mapping slip.
### Mounts: flying, swimming, mounts that remain, the roads (the mounts milestone, stage 4; an uncommitted draft)

The design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-mounts-design.md` section 5 and its "Settled in stage 4".

- **A mount that has appeared stays through a fight (the user's rule).** `MountController::on_damage` cancels a summon only BEFORE the mount opens (the first ~104 ms: nothing spawns, the ability item ends at once); once the actor is out, no hit desummons it and the cast runs on to its end (the stage-2 rule desummoned it 3 s after the open: with mobs on the player every summon died). A rider's hits land on the mount's Health as before.
- **The flight model is in the move checker** (`session/move_checker.h`, `MoveLimits::air`, filled by `Ride::limits`). Movement modes 4 (swimming) and 5 (flying) of a possessed mount are judged by the 3D distance against a speed cap, not by the horizontal distance and the two vertical speeds: mode 5 is refused outright (`ModeNotAllowed`) for a mount that cannot fly, the pawn's own checker (no `air`) is unchanged.
  - Cruise: 3D speed = the Blueprint's `MaxFlySpeed` x `MoveSpeedMult` (1,290, 1,741.5 with the dash: 4,500 of 4,757 samples of capture 871 are exactly that); swimming = `MaxSwimSpeed` x `MoveSpeedMult` (920 for the Shoalskipper, 3D: capture 899's vertical share is inside it).
  - The dive: while the view is pitched down the allowance climbs (retail 1,580-1,620 cm/s^2) towards a ceiling that is LINEAR in the pitch, `MaxFlySpeed` at 0 degrees to `Mount_Flying_Base`'s `MaxFlyFallSpeed` (7,500, `bUseFlyFallSpeed`) at 90 degrees down, times `MoveSpeedMult`: 3,671 at 34.5 degrees (871), 2,188 at 13, 6,004 at 68.3 (883, the drake); the dash scales the whole ceiling (all samples at 1.350). Released, it brakes (`BrakingDecelerationFlying` 8,192; 8,500 measured). The checker keeps the allowance and carries it a moment into a fall or a landing.
  - The numbers: margin 1.10, climb 2,400 cm/s^2, brake 4,000 cm/s^2 (the data passes at 1.0 / 1,500 / 500). The session gives the checker `MoveSpeedMult` (with the last second's larger value after a fall of it) before every move.
  - `RemoteViewPitch16` of the mount follows the accepted move's pitch (retail: 456 of 457 values on 871 are the move's own; every mount, flying or not), sent a change at a time.
  - **Stage 4 fix 1: the flight is corrected for gross violations only** (the user's test 2: "when I reach high speed the server relocates me"; that run still had the stage-3 `worldd.exe`, which judged mode 5 along the ground at 1,743 cm/s, and the stage-4 law was as strict: 10 % over the pitch's ceiling was a correction). The client's own flight numbers (`Mount_Flying_Base`'s CharMoveComp; the gryphon inherits all of them): `MaxFlySpeed` 800, `MaxFlyFallSpeed` 7,500 (`bUseFlyFallSpeed`), `MaxFlyStrafeSpeed` 750, `MaxFlyBwdSpeed` 400, `BrakingDecelerationFlying` 8,192, `GravityScale` 1.3, `AirControl` 0.125; `MaxAcceleration` is not set (UE's 2,048; retail climbs at 1,600). The checker now has two judges: the PHYSICS (the speed the client carried, falling at a quarter of its braking, plus its climb: twice `MaxAcceleration` plus gravity in proportion to how steeply the move descends; at least 1.5 x the cruise, at most `MaxFlyFallSpeed` x `MoveSpeedMult` x 1.10) corrects a move at once; the plain pitch law (above) only FLAGS a move that is beyond it (`MoveVerdict::borderline`; the session logs `would be refused by the flight law (...): accepted, not corrected` with the cm, the dt, the law's number, the multiplier and the view pitch), and corrects only after 2.5 s beyond 1.3 x it (`TooFastInAirForLong`). Mode 5 is never judged along the ground (a checker without `air`, the pawn's, judges it in 3D at the plain speed). Retail's 871 has 0 moves beyond the plain law.
