Not changed: swimming (mode 4) keeps its 3D cap x 1.10; a view pitch the player fakes buys the climb of the speed (4,096 cm/s^2 plus gravity), not a switch, and the sustain window bounds a long hack.
- **The GM Eagle Gryphon**: its Blueprint (a plain child of `Mount_Flying_Base`) says `MaxFlySpeed` 800 and `MaxRunSpeed` 600, its text 215 % (1,290). The user's refused moves (82 cm in 0.052 s, 131 in 0.084, 41 in 0.026: about 1,570 cm/s) are a dive: the old limit judged every move of mode 5 at 800 x 1.5 = 1,200, and the Blueprint's own law gives 800 + 6,700 x pitch / 90 = 1,570 at 10.3 degrees down (the flight pitch the captures show is 10 to 13). Its checker cruise is the larger of the Blueprint's and the text's (`Ride::fill_speeds`: 1,290) so that a cruise at the text's speed is not refused; if a run shows the gryphon cruising at 800 the floor is one line.
- **Modelled, no capture** (`test_worldd_mounts_ride`): landing is no event (mode 5 -> 3 -> 1 moves are accepted, the allowance carried); a dismount in the air puts the rider at the detachment point (197 cm above the mount) with its moves in falling mode, and the mount STAYS where it was (a flyer hovers in mode 5, an aquatic mount floats in mode 4: ReplicatedMovementMode; only the rider falls or swims); a relog while flying saves the character on the ground below its last good position (98 cm above it); a mount left farther than `Ride::kDesummonDistanceCm` (50,000 cm, `--`: a session setter for tests) from its owner is desummoned (the exe's `bEnableMountDistanceDesummon` and `MountDesummonDistanceCentimeters`; the Blueprints turn it on, no data has the number), which is also what a server-side move of the player (a respawn) does; the player's death already dismounts and desummons.
- **Flyers' Health** is `Mount_Base_HP` x the item text: 500 % for 43 flyers, 1,000 % for the Drake of the Canopy and the two Thundering Bolt Beasts (the item-text decision), asserted for all 46.
- **Roads** (`world/roads.h`): the file is `dist/content/roads.tsv`, `mapc roads` (the BP_RoadActor_C splines of the 122 MainGrid cells around the area, `roads_raw.tsv`: 49 roads, 18.3 km) and `contentc roads` (the preset's appearance at the node level, its Mount buff's `Road_Tn_Speed_Mount`, the width; half width max(RoadWidth, 1000) / 2) write it; at Wilderness it holds 42 roads (11 Artery T1, 31 Lions Hold T2) and drops 7 that give a mount nothing there. The header names the node level; worldd warns when it is not `--spawn-node-level`. The effect is added when the mount's position overlaps a road and removed when it does not, on the accepted move (the client does not predict: 959).
- **Tests**: `test_worldd_mounts_fly` (the goldens 871, 883, 915, 899 from `tools/capture/gen_mount_fly.py` -> `tests/capture_mount_fly.h` through the mount's checker; the flight law; the gryphon; the exploits; Health), `test_worldd_mounts_ride` (flight, swimming, air dismount, relog, mounts remain, distance, the real roads), `test_worldd_mounts` and `test_worldd_mounts_session` (a hit after the open), Go `roads_test.go`.
- **Residual risk** (changed by the review's fix batch, below): the dive ceiling used to trust the declared view pitch (a faked -90 degrees on a level path sustained 5.8 x the cruise); it now counts the view's pitch only up to `AirLimits::pitch_trust` (25 degrees) beyond the path's own steepness. A faked pitch still buys about 2 x the cruise, sustained (backlog).
- **The review's fix batch**: (1) the 3D branch of the move checker no longer refills the vertical credits on every accepted move (a client alternating a mode-5 or mode-4 move with a 500 cm climb on the ground rule climbed 500 cm a pair, at any speed; the pawn's checker too); (2) a mount-up and a dismount take `MoveChecker::rebase` instead of a new checker, so the pace bank, the credits and their server-clock state are the session's (a fresh checker refilled 5 s of claimed time and the first-move second at every possession: a hop of 74 m for a ground mount, about 400 m for a flyer, several times a second); (3) the dash's 30 s are the rider's (`PlayerSession::dash_ready_at_` seeds each new `Ride`, which shows the cooldown item on the new mount's bar); (4) no road bonus in movement mode 5 (retail's road is a ground overlap volume); (5) a relog ends the old session before the new one copies its inventory (the mount's Health persisted at the end is the new session's); (6) a mount's death hands the inventory to the saver at once; (7) `load_speeds` checks the header and fails on a bad row. The exploits, each with the test that failed first, are in `test_worldd_move_checker` (alternation, rebase), `test_worldd_mounts_fly` (alternation, the faked pitch) and `test_worldd_mounts_ride` (the dash across a resummon, the pace bank across a dismount, the road in the air).


### City nodes: the registry, the zone lookup, the node reads and the biome-gated loot (Nodes 1, stage 1; an uncommitted draft)

"Node" is the settlement (`CityNodeRecord`), not a resource node or a talent node. The design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-nodes-1-design.md`; the live state is the `citynode` Go service (`services/README.md`, "City nodes"); the typed data is contentc version 12 ("City node data").

- **The registry** (`world/city_nodes.h`, `CityNodes`): the fixed data from `content.db` (`city_node`, `city_zoi`: the 81 nodes by **rank**, their type, place and zone, and the 78 zones with their biome) plus the nodes' live levels. `node_at(x, y)` gives the zone of influence at a point and its node and biome; `level_of(guid)` / `level_of_rank(rank)` the node's level; `by_name` resolves a node the way the service does (`Verra_RVR_NewAela`, `RVR_NewAela`, `New Aela`, case and spaces do not count). A registry starts with every node at Wilderness.
- **The whole-world zone lookup** (`world/zoi_surface.h`): `zoi_at(x, y)` is the ZOI guid at a point, from `world/zoi_surface_table.h`, which `tools/capture/gen_zoi_surface.py` writes from the ZOI chunk grid of the export (30,558 files; the encoding is the G1 research's, validated on the 78 records' extents and 153,359 retail moves). A ZOI is a prism, so the table is 2D, one owner per 512 cm cell at the surface height (the 3D grid has no chunk above z 163,840): 4,635 chunk columns, 58 owners, 277,560 bytes of runs; the Riverlands' five node zones come out at 10.571, 9.951, 9.506, 9.046 and 6.278 km2 as the G1 research found. G1 stage 1 (merged) has a Riverlands-only region table from the same grid, `spawns/region_data.h` (what spawning uses). **The two ZOI tables stay side by side for now, and a later step unifies them** (this one is the whole-world side, named `zoi_surface` so the unification is a rename). Until then `test_worldd_city_nodes` ("the two ZOI tables agree on the Riverlands") checks them cell for cell: every cell of each of G1's 12 zones is owned by that zone's guid in this table, and inside the Riverlands' box this table gives those 12 guids exactly G1's cells and no others.
- **Where the levels come from** (`svc/city_node_feed.h`): at start worldd loads `deploy/citynode_seed.json` (`--citynode-seed`, `AOC_CITYNODE_SEED`; Joeva 4, Halcyon 3) at once and starts a thread that calls the service's `WatchNodes` (`--citynode`, `AOC_CITYNODE_GRPC_ADDR`, default `127.0.0.1:50055`; `--no-citynode` keeps the seed's levels). The snapshot replaces every level as one change (made first and stored node by node: the people, the scenery and the roads read the levels without a lock, and a reader never sees a node at Wilderness that the snapshot leaves where it was), each change after it moves one; a number that is not the next (a gap) drops the stream and calls again, an `Aborted` or `Unavailable` end is called again. **Login never waits for any of it.** If the service cannot be reached ONE warning is logged for the outage and the thread retries (0.5 s growing to 5 s); a later outage warns once again. The calls carry the service token.
- **The node reads of an expression** (`world::node_host_fn`, the C++ twin of `statprog.NodeWorld`; `stats/stat_program.h` kHostNodeBiome .. kHostPlayerMayor, 170 to 177): `GetNodeBiome`, `GetCurrentNode`, `GetNodeRecord`, `Node_GetNodeType`, `Node_GetLevel`, `Node_InLevelRangeInclusive` (the level compared with the bounds, in the program), and `Node_IsPlayerCitizen`, `Node_IsPlayerCitizenOfNode`, `Node_IsPlayerMayor`, which answer false until the citizenship milestone. A node is its rank (-1 none), an enum answer its ordinal. The shared corpus has `nodehost` blocks the Go side writes and `test_worldd_city_nodes` checks this host against.
- **Biome-gated loot.** `combat/aftermath` rolls a death in the zone of its position (`node_at(position)`): `RollContext::biome` and `::node` answer the reward tables' `GetNodeBiome` and the other node reads (`rewards.cpp` host). 43 reward tables (`BaseLoot_Melee_RVR`, `_TUR`, `_SAN`, `_JUN`, `_ANV`, `_NONE`, the Random_Mining stones, the Corrupted armors ...) were read as "no" before and now roll: in the Riverlands (biome 14) the `_RVR` gear drops, in the Tropics the `_TUR`, and where the zone has the Default biome the `_NONE` gear (`test_worldd_rewards`, "the biome-gated gear"). Where a zone is not a Riverlands one, nothing else changes. Bandit and Minotaur melee kills near the login spot (Ruins of Aela, Lionhold, Riverlands) now give in-band gear, recipes and reagents.
- **Not in this stage:** the per-node population (`--spawn-node-level` stays the one global level) and the town scenery (stage 3); the GameState's node maps and the summaries are stage 2 (below). The citizenship reads are false.
- Tests: `test_worldd_city_nodes` (the zone lookup, the registry on the real `content.db`, the live state and the seed, the node reads against the corpus, the feed against an in-process fake of the service: token, snapshot, changes in order, a gap, `Aborted`, an unreachable service, `stop()`), `test_worldd_rewards` (the biome golden), `test_worldd_aftermath` (300 Bandits killed at the login spot give the `_RVR` gear and none of the `_TUR`, 300 at Windansea the reverse: the whole path from the position of the death), `test_worldd_content_db` (version 12).

### The nodes the client is told of (Nodes 1, stage 2; an uncommitted draft)

`--gamestate-mode tier3` (the default) is tier 2 plus what the client's node code reads; without it the client logs a `Record not found: CityNodeRecord` at every zone banner (980 in one run), finds no node for its audio and prints `Requesting Node Data with invalid Node ID`. Retail's order (capture 1198) and the formats: `libs/aocnet/protocol/repl/node_rpc.h`; the data: `world/node_wire.h` (`NodeWire`, made from `content.db` and the registry's live levels).

- **The GameState's maps** (in its open, and re-built for every join, which is what a retail server hop does: capture 623 lines 3911 and 15639 re-open the GameState with the same four maps and base key 0): `NodeIDToClientNodeData` for retail's **46 nodes** (the `Verra_*` records with a map; level, culture Kaelar, no mayor, the lord, the bounty commodity, the `SiegeInactive` tag, the vehicle deploy radius of the level's modifications), the **progress map** (the nodes at level >= 1, keyed by **rank**, the percent of the current level from the XP; 0 at Town) and the **citizens map** (level >= 3, counts 0), and `EstateIDToOwnerGuid` (the 244 estates: the 250 records minus the six test estates, owner 0). A level change from the citynode feed bumps the registry's `version()`; each session sees it in `tick` (`run_nodes`) and sends the difference as a fast-array delta on the GameState's channel (`PlayerBootstrap::node_maps_update`, one change for one level), so every client's node window and banner follow a `nodectl set-level` at once. The update is built after a checkpoint like every reply step and, when the connection does not take it, built again from that checkpoint (`node_maps_update` keeps the arrays as they were before it for `restore`; `checkpoint()` itself copies no node array, so the other reply steps of a 98-reply login burst do not). A node-maps update the connection REFUSES outright (not a full window, which waits) is dropped with an error line and the session's version is not wound back: the client keeps the old level until the next change (`docs/backlog.md`, "Nodes 1: what the review's fix batch left").
- **The login's requests**: the client sends `ServerRequestCurrentSiegeSummary` and `ServerRequestNodeSummary` for 49 nodes in the possession bunch. Each is queued by node (`kMaxNodeRequests` 256; a node already waiting is not queued twice) and answered as the connection has room, **in retail's order: every siege summary, then every node summary**, each in the order asked. The siege summary is the idle default (`WarRecordID` 0, phase 255, dates the maximum, the target the node: retail's exact bits for all 46 nodes of a map; the two dungeons and the jungle get none, as in retail). The node summary lists the layout's plots at the node's level (a Town 31): the pre-built plots (the civic core and, as the spike decided, the NodeXP projects) Operational with their building, the mayor's service plots Empty with the plot's empty building; the essence and relic slots of the level's modifications (70 and 3 from Village); no mayor, so the message can be edited from time 60; a record that is no node of a map gets retail's 429-bit default (culture 9, no layout). `NodeWire::plot_rows` is the one source of the plots, for the summary and the town's scenery. `ServerRequestAllNodeSiegeCooldowns` is answered with the empty array (one bit).
- **OwnerInfo** (tier 3): `CharactersNodeData`, one entry per character of the account (the character service's `ListCharacters`, asked with `GetCharacter`; the joining character alone when that fails), the 32-digit guid in upper case, no `CharacterId`, citizen node 0; `CitizenNodeGuid`, `MayorNode` and `NodeToPlayerReputation` are the defaults (nothing is sent). The PlayerController's `CaravanLaunchNode` was already sent as 0.
- **Not answered: `ServerSetEssentialCitizenshipWindowData`.** Its answer, `ClientPopulateCitizenshipData(FEssentialCitizenshipWindowData)`, is a NetSerialize struct with no known layout (33 to 219 kbit in the captures), UE always sends a struct parameter, and a guessed payload could drop the connection; the request is logged once and counted. The citizenship panel of the node window stays empty until the citizenship milestone decodes the struct.
- **The client's GM console** (`session/player_session.cpp` `on_gm_node_rpc`, `world/node_control.h`, `svc/city_node_control.h`): `ServerSetNodeLevel`, `ServerAddNodeExperience`, `ServerGMGetNodeData`, `ServerGMWhatTimeIsIt` and `ServerGMFastForwardRealTime` are forwarded to the citynode service (`SetNodeLevel`, `AddNodeExperience`, `GetNode`, `GetClock`, `AdvanceClock`) through a worker thread, behind `--gm-commands` / `AOC_GM_COMMANDS=1`; the answer is a `[gm]` line in the log (the node's data; "refused: ..." with the service's reason; "the node service is unavailable"). Every other `ServerGM*` call is logged as not implemented. Each one received is logged whatever the switch says: the log settles whether the shipping client's console can send them at all.
- **`scripts/audit_node_log.ps1`** counts the node lines of a client log (the record errors, the NodeAudio errors, the invalid-node requests): the check of the goal "0 and 0".
- Tests: `test_repl_nodes` (libs: the four maps of capture 1198's login and of both of 623's hops read to the last bit, typed, rebuilt and written back to the exact bits, a level change as one small delta; all 49 summaries read and the 30 without skill points or relics rebuilt to the exact bits; all 46 siege summaries rebuilt from the node's guid and name to the exact bits; the requests and the GM parameters), `test_worldd_node_state` (the registry at retail's levels against retail's maps and summaries: the radii, the bounty, the ranks, the 244 estates, the layouts, plot counts and essence of all 49; the seed's maps; a tier-3 join, the 49 + 49 requests in retail's order, a repeated request, an unknown node, the cooldowns, the live delta, a late join; the GM console with the switch off and on; `GrpcNodeControl` against an in-process fake of the node service). The captured side is `libs/aocnet/tests/capture_nodes.h`, written by `tools/capture/gen_node_state.py`.

### The towns' scenery (Nodes 1, stage 3a; an uncommitted draft)

What the client draws of a town (its buildings, walls and props) is the `LayoutAssetSetGuids` of one `ANodeLayoutReplicator` per node; the client loads the meshes itself from its design DB by the asset set's guid. The payload, decoded from 92 retail payloads to their last bit by the spike (`research/spike_replicator/decode.md`), is in `libs/aocnet/protocol/repl/node_layout.h`; the items are `world/node_layout_data.h`; the actors are `world/node_scenery.h`.

- **The payload** (`repl/node_layout.h`): a fast array whose own `NetDeltaSerialize` writes a header between the leading `bSupportsFastArrayDelta` bit and the stock counters: the level, culture and season (8 bits each), the tag container (empty bit, 6-bit count, per tag a not-hardcoded bit, the FString and the FName number: retail's is always `War.Siege.States.SiegeInactive`) and `PlotChangeVersion` (8 bits). It rides on the generic machinery: `FastArrayLayout::prefix_size` says the layout has a header and how to measure it, `FastArrayState` holds the header's bits (`set_prefix`), `write_fast_array_delta` writes it after the leading bit, `read_fast_array_delta` keeps it in `FastArrayDelta::prefix`. `sync_node_layout` brings a replicator's array to a header and an item list. An item is 768 bits after its ReplicationID (the owner, the secondary id, the array index, the asset set, then the rotation's X, Y, Z, the translation and the scale as plain doubles), so a payload is `481 + 800 x items` bits plus the tags (Joeva at Town 73 items = 58,881 bits). The schema generator (`tools/ghidra/schemagen.py`) has the class: 25 RepLayout commands, 15 net fields, `LayoutAssetSetGuids` the fast array of field 13 (handle 19) and `NodeGuid` handle 25.
- **The items** (`NodeLayoutData`, content.db only): for each prop of the node's layout (419 props in 70 layouts) whose asset set is valid for the node, OwnerGuid the node, OwnerArrayIndex the prop's index; for each pre-built plot of the layout at the node's level (the civic core and the NodeXP projects; the mayor's service plots send nothing while empty), one item per asset set of its building in the plot's state. An asset set is valid when some definition it holds fits the level window, the culture (Kaelar, every retail payload), the node's type, the season (Spring: one fixed season; retail flips Winter and Spring across re-opens) and the biome (the node's zone of influence's, which reproduces the spike's per-node trial: Riverlands for Joeva, Halcyon and Winstead, Highlands for Aithanahr, Desert for Azmaran ...). `ValidGameplayTags` is empty on all 634 definitions. The five small and medium NodeXP projects start Operational (retail shows them finished on all six live nodes at level 3 and 4; the design data starts them under construction) and the Large one as the data starts it (retail's summaries of Joeva, Winstead and Halcyon show it under construction; a Town filters its scaffold out): `NodeWire::plot_rows` carries the states, so the node summary and the scenery agree. What a fresh server cannot know (service buildings a mayor built, expansions bought) is left out. Joeva at Town is 73 items (55 layout + 18 plot), Halcyon at Village 61 (45 + 16).
- **The actor** (`NodeScenery`, `ActorKind::NodeLayout`): one per node that has items at its level, made by `PlayerWorld::start_scenery` after the area (at the seed's levels 36 of the 44 roster nodes that have a layout; the other 8 have nothing to draw at Wilderness), in the registry, so every connection's `ActorView` opens, updates and closes it. Retail's open: the archetype `Default__NodeLayoutReplicator` (`bootstrap::node_layout_archetype_export`), the persistent level, the node's place and yaw, no velocity or scale; then `AuthServerIDReplicated`, `ReplicatedMovement` (the place), `RemoteRole` 1, `Role` 4, `NodeGuid` (the client waits for it to be non-zero) and the fast array. It is sent to a player within **1.45 km** (`--scenery-radius`, metres; retail opened it at 1.19 to 1.45 km), kept while the player is near (10 m of hysteresis) and closed with a Relevancy close beyond; it is never dormant. A 1.45 km awake radius is outside the registry's grid query, so the registry keeps such "wide" actors apart (`ActorRegistry::kWideCm`, `query_wide`) and the view asks for them beside the grid. A wide actor does NOT raise the view's grid-query radius (`ActorView::query_radius_cm()` stays at the snapshot ring, 360 m, while only a town's replicator is around; an actor of up to 400 m still raises it): the first version let the replicator raise it to 1.46 km for the rest of the session, so every view's pass walked about 900 cells and sorted every actor within 1.46 km (Nodes 1 review A, I1; `test_worldd_actor_view`).
- **Where it stands.** The layout's translations are offsets from the ACTOR, and retail's open location is 6 to 40 m from the node record's location with no constant offset in the record's frame (the layout origin is an actor of the map, not the record's place). `world/node_origins.h` (generated by `tools/capture/gen_node_layout.py`) has the open location and yaw of the 13 nodes the captures show, Joeva, Halcyon and Winstead among them; those are used (`--node-origin captured`, the default), and every other node stands at its record's location. `--node-origin record` puts all of them at the records' (to judge the difference in game). **The first in-game round settles whether the record's location is enough for the other 33 nodes.**
- **A level change** (the citynode feed, `nodectl set-level`, the client's GM console): `tick_world` compares `CityNodes::version()` with the last it saw and rebuilds the nodes whose level changed: **a new full payload** (every old item removed, the new list added with new ReplicationIDs, the header's level new) and `PlotChangeVersion` + 1 (it skips 0 when it wraps), as one reliable change of the actor, which every connection that holds the replicator is sent as one update on the same channel. A level at which a node has nothing to draw removes the replicator (the clients close it); a level with something makes a new one. No capture shows a level change inside a channel, so this is the spike's decision, to be judged in game: the client's `OnRep_LayoutAssetSetGuids` re-filters every item against the new level, and a different `PlotChangeVersion` alone re-runs its asset load.
- **The log** (`[scenery]`): at start one line per node (level, items as layout + plots, `PlotChangeVersion`, payload bits, where it stands and whether that is retail's open location) and a summary; each open, update and close per player (node, level, items, bits, channel); each rebuild (`level 4 -> 3: a new full payload of 61 items (was 73), PlotChangeVersion 2`). `--no-node-scenery` (`AOC_NO_NODE_SCENERY=1`) makes none: the towns are empty holes again.
- **Not in this stage:** the plots (`UAoCNodePlotComponent`; the spike's replay showed the scenery with the replicator alone), the town's people and the roads (stage 3b).
- Tests: `test_repl_node_layout` (libs: the 18 retail payloads read to their last bit and written again from the typed payload to the exact bits, the header's refusals and cuts, the server's side: the first sync is IDs 1..N, a level change is one delta a client applies, a header-only change), `test_worldd_node_layout` (the items built from the real `content.db` against the same 18 payloads: the layout items equal to the last bit, the pre-built plots' items equal, what retail has beyond them is the built service buildings and expansions, our items and retail's other ones written with retail's header are the exact bits; the default start; the actors, their place, a level change, the 260-change wrap; an `ActorView` opening at 1.4 km, keeping, closing beyond 1.45 km, sending one update for a level change to two players; the world's `start_scenery` and `tick_world`), `services/internal/content` `TestNodeTransformColumnsKeepNegativeZero`. The captured side is `libs/aocnet/tests/capture_node_layout.h` (`tools/capture/gen_node_layout.py`).
- **contentc and negative zeros.** Retail's payloads carry `-0.0` in many transforms (29 of Joeva's 85 items) and the town's items are sent bit for bit. A `REAL` column of SQLite stores a whole-number float as an integer and reads it back as `+0.0`, so the three transform tables (`node_layout_prop`, `node_layout_plot`, `node_asset_set_member`) now declare their ten transform columns with no type (`internal/content/nodes.go`); the file must be rebuilt with this contentc (`cd services && go run ./cmd/contentc`) for the signs to be kept. The version stays 12: a reader sees the same numbers, except that the sign of a zero now survives. (So a version-12 file built before 2026-10-10 15:53, with the first stage 3a build, also reports 12 and holds `+0.0`: only `test_worldd_node_layout`, "all 18 cases reproduce to the exact bits", notices. No such file exists today; the version was not bumped for it.)

### The towns' people and the roads by node level (Nodes 1, stage 3b; an uncommitted draft)

A town is its people as well as its scenery: the guards, villagers, merchants, scholars, clerks and vendors of the layout and of the buildings standing on its plots. They are the 201 layout population sets (`node_layout_population`) and the 332 building-state sets (`building_pop_spawn`, `building_level_spawn_pop`) of content.db; the selection is `spawns/node_population.h`, the live manager `spawns/node_populations.h`.

- **Where they stand** (fitted to retail, not assumed): a layout set is placed at the node RECORD's location with the record's yaw, a building's set at the plot's transform (`NodeWire::plot_rows`, the same plots the summary and the scenery list) in that frame, the plot's yaw added. Of the 506 town NPCs capture 721 shows at Joeva (a Town), 407 stand within 3 m of a layout point so placed (the open location of the town's replicator, 6 to 40 m away, fits 18), and of the 268 whose Blueprint a building names 81 stand within 3 m of a building point (the other readings of the plot's yaw: 7 and 25). `test_worldd_node_pop` holds nine of them as anchors: a Guard_Wizard, a Guard_Soldier, a villager, animals, the warehouse clerk (0 cm) and a gear vendor (4 cm) of our selection stand within 1 m of where retail's did.
- **What each node is judged as** (`node_spawn_context`): its id, type, level, biome (its zone's) and zone of influence (the zone refusal is dropped: a filter that names the node's zone passes; the area's circle keeps E1's refusal), Kaelar, Summer, the SiegeInactive tag, and the spawn sources `Layout` and `BuildingLevelSpawn` running (the sets those sources spawn are gated off in the open world). **The siege tags a filter requires are any-of** (`SpawnFilter::filter`: the 45 filters that require Declared, Inactive and Prep pass in a world that carries SiegeInactive, the 5 that require Active do not; every other required tag stays all-of): E1's all-of could never pass. The world-map golden (`pop_golden_region.h`) does not change by it.
- **Who is shown.** A humanoid whose body is merged from an `NPCAppearanceComponent` (the villagers, merchants, clerks) was filtered out of the open world as invisible: a Blueprint made from the data alone carries none. The open a retail capture sent for a Blueprint carries the component, so every Blueprint a capture opened (`CapturedData::npc_leaves()`) is shown for a node, and all of its NPCs look like the captured one. E1's templates covered the Blueprints of a one-kilometre circle (39); `tools/capture/find_node_npcs.py` (a one-off scan of 73 captures, `fixtures/region/node_npc_picks.json`) found an open for 30 more that the towns name (`gen_area_spawns.py` adds them to its templates: 69 now): Node_Merchant_M/F, Node_Scholar_M/F, Node_Mercenary_M/F, Node_Priest_M/F, Node_Villager_M_Hammer/Shovel/Carrying, the town hall's managers (guilds, citizenship, buy orders, construction, town crier, mayor, black market, guild commodities, academic type), the guild storage clerk, the caravan master and the crate salesman, the fishing and cookhouse vendors, Kaelar citizens and merchants, Guard_Captain. The world-map golden does not change by them. Still without a look, so still dropped (`Appearance`): the quest NPCs (SQ_*, QuestNPC_*: 10) and the artisan outposts' vendors (ArtisanshipPOI_*_Vendor: Aven's End and Briarmoor Workshops), and the props the node tables name (crafting stations, mailboxes, banners, tents: `NotPawn`, no schema).
- **The numbers** (`tools/capture/node_pop_oracle.py`, `gen_node_pop_golden.py` -> `tests/node_pop_golden.h`, an independent Python port; the 10 city nodes inside the Riverlands at each of the 7 levels). Joeva: Wilderness 169, Crossroads 196, Encampment 207, Village 327, Town 585, City 172, Metropolis 172 (the Town-only sets are gone from City). At Town 563 NPCs come from the layout's 68 sets (53 Guard_Soldier, 45 Guard_Wizard, the villagers, merchants, scholars, mercenaries and priests, 40 wolves ...) and 22 from the buildings' sets (the warehouse's clerks, the gear vendors, the caravan master ...); the points lost are 1,194 of other nodes' ids, 398 of other levels, 48 with no template, 6 props. At the seed's levels the world holds 1,539 town NPCs: Joeva 585, Halcyon (Village) 346, Winstead 198, New Aela 169, Miraleth 190, Breezewater Bay Harbor 13, Aven's End 19, Briarmoor Workshops 19; Lionhold and Samia's Hope 0 (their one building is a mailbox, a prop).
- **The live manager** (`NodePopulations`, made by `PlayerWorld::start_node_people` after the area and the ground, `--no-node-people` turns it off): `start` makes the people of every node inside the area (map `Verra_World_Master`, a layout, the footprint holds its location) at its level; `tick` (in `tick_world`, after the ground window and before the NPC world's step) follows `CityNodes::version()`: a node whose level changed is selected again and the registry made to match. A point has an identity (its set, its placement, its key): an NPC stays across the change when the new selection has the same point with the same Blueprint in the same place (its actor keeps its id and reference); a point that is gone, or that draws another Blueprint, loses its NPC (the registry's removal closes every connection's channel); new points get new NPCs. Joeva Town -> Village keeps 170 of its 585 NPCs, removes 415 and brings 157 (logged: `[people] Joeva -> Village: 327 NPCs now (170 stay, 415 leave, 157 arrive ...)`).
- **No races.** The world is one thread; the citynode feed only bumps `CityNodes::version()`. The order in `tick_world` is the window (the chunks that arrived ground the NPCs waiting for them), THEN the people, then the NPC world, the fights and the corpses. A new NPC goes into the NPC world at once: grounded if its chunk is resident, else pending (it stands on the terrain, dormant, and is grounded when `chunks_resident` reports its chunk: the G1 rules, exactly as the area's NPCs); a removed NPC leaves the NPC world through the registry's removal listener (also while it is pending: the count follows) and the fights forget it (`CombatWorld::forget`). A corpse that is still lying there is removed with the rest; the aftermath's closing of a corpse that is gone is harmless. `test_worldd_node_pop` checks it on the chunked fixture of G1.
- **Respawns.** A node NPC carries a spawn reference in the node range (`kNodeRefBase` 0x80000000 + a slot); `PlayerWorld::respawn_npc` hands it to `NodePopulations::respawn`, which makes the NPC again from the node's CURRENT selection (the same point, so the same Blueprint unless the level changed it) or does nothing when the point is gone. A point replaced at a level change closes its old slot, so a respawn timer that was running for the dead one cannot bring a second NPC beside the new one.
- **Roads by node level.** A road's worth is its preset's `RoadAppearancesIds[node level]` for the node level of the zone that owns it. `contentc roads --levels` writes `dist/content/roads_levels.tsv` (`roadn`, the owner zone, the tier and the half width at each of the seven levels, the points: 145 roads, 548 KB; `--check` is in `generators.ps1`); worldd loads it instead of `roads.tsv` when it exists (`--roads-levels`; **when it is missing worldd warns `[content] no ...roads_levels.tsv` and falls back to `roads.tsv`**, so the roads stop following the node levels: copy the file with `content.db` when a build is moved), `RoadMap::apply_levels` sets every road to the level of its zone's node (a zone with no node, a starting area, takes `--spawn-node-level`) at the start and on every node level change (`tick_world`, logged `[roads]`), and a mount on a road rides at the new tier at once: 13 of Joeva's roads change tier between Village and Town. The client redraws the roads itself when the node's level changes in the GameState's maps (stage 2). At Wilderness the file and `roads.tsv` give the same tier along every road.
- **Not done:** a building changing state (Disarray, Damaged, Demolition, a mayor's new building: later node milestones); the world-map points whose filter names a node id (Miraleth's goblin camps, New Aela's cultists: 584 and 360 points, spawned only when the node is at a level; they are not node populations and never pass in the open world's context); a town's vendors answering a click (only the GM vendors do: `has nothing to offer yet`); the town's crafting stations, mailboxes and banners (props with no schema); a patrol route placed by a city node (the NPC stands); the Blueprints no retail capture opened (the quest NPCs, the artisan outposts' vendors).
- Tests: `test_worldd_node_pop` (the filter's any-of rule and zone; the frames and nine of capture 721's NPCs; the 70 golden cases against the Python port; the manager on the real nodes: start, a level change in both directions, the hooks, the respawns, two nodes at once; a made-up point that draws another Blueprint at another level; the pending-ground rules on the chunked fixture; `PlayerWorld` on a circle around Joeva with the roads; the by-level road map), `contentc` `TestResolveRoadsByLevel` and `TestRealRoadsLevelsFile`, and the unchanged `test_worldd_spawn_filter`, `test_worldd_area` and `test_worldd_region` (the move of the point rules into `spawn_point` changed nothing).

### The five city nodes' bowls: the town's people at their height and the town floor (Nodes 1, test 2 fix; an uncommitted draft)

The user's second test of Nodes 1 stood in Joeva, saw the land and the town, and saw 0 NPCs. The cause: the baked terrain (`dist/content/area_nav.bin`, the client's landscape) lies about 50.6 m BELOW the floor of Winstead, Joeva, New Aela, Halcyon and Miraleth (the landscape under a town is a pit; the town's level instance stands over it at the height of the land around). The town's NPCs had the right z (the layout's, retail's to 1-168 cm), but `NpcWorld` put a waiting NPC on the terrain and, when its chunk came, found no surface within 15 m of its height and fell back to the terrain: 558 of Joeva's 585 NPCs stood 50 m underground.

- **The people keep their height** (`NpcInfo::ground_by_data`, set by `NodePopulations::make` for every point that is not a PopulationArea's random one): `NpcWorld::add`'s pending branch does not move them to the terrain and `ground_npc` has no terrain fallback for them. A surface within 15 m of the data height still grounds them (the artisan outposts are +1 m over the baked ground; a floor of a building). The open world's NPCs are unchanged (the flag is false). No navmesh stands on a town floor, so a bowl's people stand (`Mode::Stand`) and do not wander or patrol.
- **The town floor for the players** (`world/town_floor.h`, `PlayerWorld::town_floor()`): from the heights the node people stand at over the baked terrain under them: the town's lift is the median of those more than 15 m up (50.6 m at Joeva: 49.9 to 51.0 m at the five at their Town and Village levels); the people within 3 m of it are the floor; a point is in the bowl when one of them stands within 75 m and the baked ground under the point is within 10 m of the ground under that person (so the wall of the pit and the land beyond the rim are not: on the real ground none of the 10 to 35 NPCs per town that stand on open land is taken for the bowl). The floor there is the baked ground plus the lift (retail: z = 0.986 terrain + 5,247 cm, r 0.99). Against retail's 9 anchors at Joeva it is within 2 m for 7 and 4 and 12 m for the two on a tower or a terrace. It is rebuilt (lazily, on the first question after the people changed) from the registry's node NPCs: nothing else is loaded.
- **Where it is used.** (1) `PlayerWorld::ground_at` (a GM teleport to a point with no z: `worldctl tp <name> <x> <y>`; a named place: `place_height`; the client's `ServerTeleport`) answers the town floor plus `kBowlSlackCm` (2 m: the player falls the last stretch) in a bowl, the baked ground elsewhere; a z that is given is believed. (2) `PlayerSession::join` puts a character saved 15 m or more under a town's floor (`PlayerWorld::lift_out_of_bowl`) on the floor and logs `[session] <name>: was saved at (x, y, z) cm, N m under the floor of a town in a bowl (<node>): joins on it, at z ...`; worldd changes only its copy of the position (the first move saves the new one). (3) `PlayerSession::ground_below`, which `last_position()` uses for a rider in the air, answers the town floor (plus the slack) when the flyer is above it.
- **Not done** (`docs/backlog.md`): the correction in every other ground query (a mount's summon should be refused inside a bowl, by reading the code, not run: its ground check finds no surface within 4 m of the rider; a mount's landing; a respawn at a shrine), and a navmesh on the town floor so the people can walk. `test_worldd_node_pop` holds the cases (the fixture's bowl, the pure class, `PlayerWorld` on the fixture) and, opt-in with `AOC_REAL_GROUND=1`, the five bowls on the real terrain.

### Professions and the GM artisan items (Gathering stage 2, part "Professions and GM items"; an uncommitted draft)

Every character holds the 22 CharacterProfessions from its first login (Novice, level 1, no XP; the migration 0010 backfills existing characters), the client is told, and the GM artisan scrolls work.

- **State** (`session/professions.*`): `professions::Book` (the 22 professions with their 50-level curves, 400 x level^2 XP, and the five certification levels, read once by `PlayerWorld::professions()` from content.db's `profession`, `certification_level` and `action_certification`; a file without the tables runs without professions and the log says so) and `professions::Professions` (what a character holds: tier, level, XP per profession; `state`, `level`, `tier`, `xp`, `can_use(profession, tier, level)`, `add_xp`, `promote`, `demote`, `raise_xp_to`, `to_svc`, `load`). The level is the highest level of the curve the XP reaches, held at the tier's MaxLevel (Novice 10, Apprentice 20, Journeyman 30, Master 40, GrandMaster 50); the XP is held just below the threshold of the level past the cap (the Adventuring cap rule) until a Promote. Modelled, not captured: a Promote is one tier up and takes effect only at the tier's cap (the XP before it in the scrolls brings it there; below the cap it is skipped and said so); a Demote is one tier down with the level and XP held at the lower cap (no item uses one; the client's `ServerDemoteCertification` is not handled: the session's generic unhandled-RPC path ignores it, a debug line); GM items ignore a certification's MaximumAllowable (the plan's notes say so when a tier holds more than its limit).
- **The wire** (`PlayerBootstrap::set_profession`, `ProgressSetup::professions`): the join's XPTracker lists the LevelingCertificationsFastMap with all 22 professions (key = the profession's leveling type with the hardcoded name 0, value = the CertificationLevel guid) in the order of the CharacterProfessions guids, and the ExperienceFastMap only of the professions with XP, as the retail join does (golden: 1198 pkt 1510's two maps, `capture_leveling.h` `kJoinCertifications` and `kJoinExperience`, made by `tools/capture/gen_leveling.py`, which `test_worldd_items_session` compares to a character in the same state). A later change is an XPTracker update of the absolute totals plus `BroadcastPlayLevelUpEffects(the profession's leveling type, level)` for a level gained.
- **GM items** (`use::plan_use`): the actions run IN ORDER on a copy of the character's professions: `GiveItem` (items; a Profession XP line runs its compiled expression, `reward_xp.expr`, `xp - cur + 1`, through `professions::XpRunner` with the live profession levels, `GetPlayerProfessionLevel` = `CharacterStats::set_profession_levels`), `SetCertificationLevel` (Promote or Demote of the profession `action_certification` names; the reward table every such action names, Alchemy's XP, is ignored). The 66 single-profession scrolls follow the data (Level 9: level 9; Level 15: XP, Promote, XP = level 15 Apprentice; Level 25: level 25 Journeyman, and the T3 tool of Fishing, Herbalism, Hunting, Lumberjacking and Mining). `GM_Scroll_MakeMeLevel25Artisanship_All` has one XP line and ONE Promote per profession (Tailoring two), which ends at level 10: it is modelled as its description says (level 25 in all 22, Journeyman), the plan's note says "modelled". Only a profession that moved a level or a tier is changed (a scroll's +1 XP past its level is not kept); a scroll that moves nothing and gives no item is refused and stays ("nothing would change: Mining is level 25 (Journeyman) already").
- **Saving**: `hand_over_progress` gives the character service all 22 rows with every hand-over (`SaveProgress.professions`, each replaces its profession's row); the join reads them back (`Professions::load` corrects a tier outside 0-4, a level outside the tier's range, XP below the level's threshold).
- **For Harvesting**: `PlayerSession::professions()` (level, tier, `can_use`), `set_professions(after)`, `profession_wire(changes)` and `queue_profession_level_ups(conn, changes)` do the session side of an XP gain; a tool is `inventory().at(Equipment, items::equip::Pickaxe | Axe | Sickle | FishingRod | Bow)` (26, 29, 33, 35, 38) and `gather_tool` (content.db) says its profession, slot and certification tier. The GM vendors (`PTR_ONLY_Vendor_Artisan` and the Novice/Apprentice/Journeyman ones) list all 67 scrolls and the Starter and T1 tools.
- **Tests**: `test_worldd_professions` (the rules on a small book; the real file's professions, the 67 scrolls from a fresh character against a model of the data's action order, the refusals, the expression runner, the T3 tools' slots and the vendors' lists; skips without `dist/content/content.db` or `AOC_CONTENT_DB`), and in `test_worldd_items_session` the join's maps against the retail golden, the scrolls through the session (XPTracker, level-ups, storage events, the tool equipping, saves, a relog, the saver).

### Harvesting the resource nodes (Gathering stage 2, part "Harvesting"; an uncommitted draft)

The 439 captured resource nodes of the Riverlands and the GM garden's 10 can be harvested: Mining, Lumberjacking and Herbalism actor nodes (trees, plants, stones, gems and metals that are `GatherableActorDef`s). Foliage (the painted trees and plants), Hunting corpses and Fishing are Gathering stage 3 and later. The retail wire is from capture 1427 (the whole Daffodil harvest), 1454 and 1455 (a gathered node's open), 1460/1461/1466/1469/1470/1471 (the others); `tools/capture/gen_gather.py` writes them into `libs/aocnet/tests/capture_gather.h`.

- **Data** (`gather/gathering.*`, `ContentDb::gather_data`): content.db's `gather_def` (339 defs: the profession, the tool slot, the certification tier, the base duration, the channel and wear formulas, the reward table, the range, the respawn), `gather_tool` (87 tool items with their profession, tier, slot and requirement) and `item_variation` (the Quality formulas). The nodes' reward tables are their own `RewardBook` (the interaction's line, the experience lines, the bonus tables of `Random_Mining_*`); the rolls are the data's equations run on the stat engine with the harvesting player's own stat run (`Gathering::channel_seconds`, `Gathering::harvest`). The ratings (`Artisan_*_Rating`, 194 stats in the bundle, 112 read by the equations) are the stat engine's settled values, equipment included: a T1 tool carries a little, a T3 tool a lot (the T3 sickle's experience rating gave 152 XP for the Apprentice tier's 90); `Gathering::set_ratings` overrides them for a test. Per harvest: each item's variation formula rolls its quality first (1, 6, 16, 31, 51, 71, 101 = Common to Artifact), the quantity formula (`5 + Quantity`) reads it (`GetRolledItemQuality()`: the index of the rarity band by default, the literal quality with `--gather-quality-literal`; both give the captured Oak's 5 + 1), the experience lines read the FIRST item's rarity (`GetItemRarity()`, `GetSourceCertificationTier()`): Novice 80, Apprentice 90, Journeyman 100 times the rarity (1.0, 1.5, 2.0 ...). A bonus drop rolls its own quality and quantity.
- **Node state** (`gather/node_board.*`, shared by every player, kept for the worldd run): Available -> Channeling(player, start, length) -> Gathered(until) -> Available, and Channeling -> Available when it is cancelled. It writes the shared actor (`gather/node_wire.*`): `bGathered`; the InteractableComponent's `InputEffectStatuses[0]` (the status 0x08000001 at the start, then +3 in the counter's top five bits every 100 ms, the done update's 0x20000004 and the next counter step), `PlayerGuidsActiveChanneling`, `CanInputActivate`, `CurrentInputStates[0]`. `InteractingPlayer` is the harvester's pawn, which only its own client has: the registry holds none and `ActorView::view_of` shows the pawn to the harvester's connection while `WorldActor::gather.show` (at the start and in the done update, off from the first heartbeat, as capture 1427 shows). The heartbeats are unreliable (position-style) updates, the start and the done update reliable. A gathered node stays on its channel (retail closes it by Dormancy about 120 ms after the result frame: not modelled). `advance` (any session's tick) brings a node back at its respawn and frees a channel that no session completed 30 s after it was due.
- **Respawn**: the actor def's `OverrideRespawnDuration` when it is more than 0, else the foliage data's `EmptyDuration`, times `--gather-respawn-mult` / `AOC_GATHER_RESPAWN_MULT` (1; the in-game test uses 0.002: a tree's 14,400 s becomes 28.8 s), at least a second. The seasonal def is ignored. No capture shows a node return (the longest window was 49 min): this is modelled.
- **The start** (`PlayerSession::on_gather_interact`, `gather/rules.*`), each check logged (`[gather] <name>: the harvest of <def> (node <id>) is refused (<kind>): ...`) and counted (`harvest_refusals()`): the node's def has a gather row; the client has the node Awake; within the interaction's radius + tolerance + 100 cm (retail's players stood 42 to 175 cm from the node); this player is not channeling already; the node is Available (busy: another player; gathered); certification: the character's tier in the profession reaches the node's and its level the tier's first level; the tool in the interaction's slot is a gathering tool of that profession whose tier reaches the node's, with durability left, and the character may use it (the T3 tools need Journeyman: worldd's equip rules do not check that); combat where the interaction does not allow it. A refusal sends nothing (the client checks the certification itself and sends nothing when it fails).
- **The channel**: `Gathering::channel_seconds` (base x the Speed curve of the ratings: 11.25 s a plant, 15 s a large node, 7.5 s a medium one at rating 0). Every tick checks the player is alive, on foot, still has the same tool, is within the reach + 100 cm and the node is still in the world; the heartbeat is due every 100 ms; at the end the result is made in ONE step: the rolls, the node Gathered, then, in the captured order, `ClientUpdateItemDurability` (the tool's slot, minus the wear formula's 1 or 2), a `ClientReceiveStorageEvent` for each stack, the XPTracker (the profession's XP total and the Adventuring debt/XP: the debt is paid first), `BroadcastPlayLevelUpEffects` for a level; the inventory and the progress are handed to the saver at once.
- **The pawn's ChannelingData** (the fix after the user's test 1: "no chopping animation", no bar, walking did not cancel): retail sets the pawn's `InteractInfo.ChannelingData` (an `FChannelingData`, `channeling_codec.h`) in the packet that wakes the node, 87 ms after the interact, and clears it 2 ms after the result frame; the client plays the gather animation, shows the bar and sends its own `ServerInterruptChannel` from it. `PlayerSession::on_gather_interact` now sends it (`PlayerBootstrap::pawn_channeling`: the node's netguid, Flags 6, active, the start time in whole seconds on the effects' synchronized clock, the channel's length, the worn tool's item record and instance guid) just before the node's own update; `complete_gather` clears it between the items and the XPTracker (retail's order in packet 674) and `cancel_gather` clears it in the same form. Inferred, not captured: what Flags 6 / 0x106 and the active flag mean, that the start time is floored to whole seconds because all ten captures are, that the clock base is the effects' (1427's ChannelingData 13788648.0 against CurrentEffects' 13788648.65 at +121 ms), and that a cancel clears the way the result does.
- **Cancels** (modelled, no capture except the foliage's callout "GatheringInterruption_Movement"): the client's `ServerInterruptChannel`, moving beyond the reach, **moving more than 60 cm (horizontally) from where the channel began** (a stand-still safety net for a client that does not cancel itself; retail's harvesters stood still: 0 cm in 8 of 10 captures), a new ability, mounting, death, taking off or swapping the tool, the node leaving the world, the session ending: no reward, the node Available at once, each logged (`[gather] <name>: the harvest of <def> (node <id>) ends with no reward: <why>`).
- **The node's CanInputActivate is never empty**: the client's `UInteractableComponent::CanInputActivate` indexes the replicated array without a bound, so an EMPTY array is an access violation (the user's test 1: the client died 487 ms after the Daffodil cancel; the respawn and the stuck-channel release wrote the same). `gather/node_wire.cpp` has one writer of the property: four entries, the first false in the done update (retail's 33 bits), all four true when the node is available again (the allowed form of `world::allow_inputs`).
- **Foliage**: `ServerStartInteractionWithFoliage` (a painted tree or plant) had no handler in stage 2; since Gathering stage 3 it is handled ("Foliage gathering" below). With `--no-gathering` the first one of a session logs `[gather] <name>: a tree or plant of the world's foliage was interacted with ...: foliage gathering is off in this world`.
- **A full bag**: the stacks that fit go in, the rest is not lost: a callout "Unable to loot, not enough inventory space." (3 s) and 100 ms after the experience frame a public `APlayerLootPile` where the player stands (`PlayerWorld::drop_harvest_pile` -> `Aftermath::drop_pile`, the pile a death makes; capture 1461 packets 897 and 904). Since Gathering stage 3 "the bag" is the character's resource bag (Resource0) and a stack tops up an existing one of the same record and quality (see "Resource storage" below); the pile is still the death's look (the user's backlog wants a spawn look of its own).
- **Not done**: the Dormancy close of a gathered node; the 70 nodes only ever seen gathered (not placed); Hunting (Gathering stage 3), Fishing; node growth experience (the `NodeActivity` line, a citizen's); skills and talents that give ratings.
- **Knobs**: `--gather-respawn-mult` / `AOC_GATHER_RESPAWN_MULT` (1), `--no-gathering` / `AOC_NO_GATHERING` (the nodes are seen and not harvested), `--gather-quality-literal` / `AOC_GATHER_QUALITY_LITERAL`.
- **Log lines** (the user's test reads them): `[gather] on: 339 node defs, 87 tools, N reward tables; a gathered node returns after the data's respawn x M`; per session `[gather] <name>: starts harvesting <def> (node <id>): a <Tier> <Profession> node, with <tool>, a channel of S s (base B s), D cm from it`; `[gather] <name>: harvested <def> (node <id>): <item> xN qQ ...; <Profession> +X XP (now ..., level ..); Adventuring +A XP offered (...); the tool's durability a -> b; the node returns in T s`; the refusals and cancels above; `[gather] <name>: the bag was full: N stack(s) of the harvest of <def> are in a pile where it stands`; `[gather] the channel of player P on node N is S s overdue: its session never completed it; the node is available again`.
- **Tests**: `test_repl_gather` (libaocnet: the durability codec on the ten captures' payloads, the captured node blocks read with the typed codecs, the facts, the pawn's ChannelingData: ten sets and ten clears read, typed and written back to the exact bits), `test_worldd_gather` (the data of the real content.db, capture 1427 replayed to the bit: the open, 96 heartbeats, the done update, 1454's open; the board and its exploits; the requirements; the rarity of 100,000 harvests per case against the analytic tables, standard and `_T1_Only`, ratings 0 and 8409; the experience 80/90/100/135; the quantity under both readings; 1470's bonus drops reproduced with forced draws; the channel's length) and `test_worldd_gather_session` (sessions with fake clients on the real content.db: a harvest from the interact to the node's return, the refusals, a repeated/flooded/replayed interact, a gathered node, two players on one node, a channel that ends with no reward in each of the ways above, a full bag, a profession's level-up). All three need `dist/content/content.db` (`test_worldd_gather*` skip without it, and say so).

### Resource storage: where a gathered item goes (Gathering stage 3, part "Resource storage"; an uncommitted draft)

The client keeps every item whose gameplay tags hold a child of `item.resource` (1,824 of the 10,568 items: raw wood, plants, stone, gems, metal, carcasses, fish, reagents, Glint certificates ...) in up to three **resource containers**, the resource bags worn in Equipment slots 23, 24 and 25 (`EItemLocation` Resource0 to Resource2), and refuses them in the Backpack (`LogAoC_Inventory: An invalid item type was added to this Backpack` was the client log of every stage 2 harvest). Every other item stays in the Backpack. The rule is the exe's (`UItemStorageComponent`'s item check, `UItemMovementSubsystem::ValidateTargetContainers`).

- **Data** (contentc 14, `item.inv_w`/`inv_h`/`resource`, `resource_bag`, `resource_bag_cell`): an item's dimension in cells (never turned; 10,192 items are 1 x 1, none larger than 4 x 2) and the resource flag; the 70 items with a `ResourceBagDefId` (64 worn bags and 6 warehouse tab definitions) with the grid (`BagDimension` columns x rows, `maximum_items` = their product, blocked cells included) and the def's matrix (a list of columns of -1 usable / -2 blocked), which applies only when `bCustomDimension` (the Sanctus bag's def stores a 4-column matrix its 6 x 4 grid ignores). Every class's starter kit wears `Bag_Resource_Sanctus` in slot 23, so a new character has Resource0, 6 x 4 = 24 cells, no blocked cell; the Mining T2 bag is 8 x 5 with 16 blocked cells, the Lumberjacking T2 bag 6 x 5 with 12, the largest worn gathering bag 8 x 8 (T3 Mining, 28 blocked), the Livestream promotion bag 8 x 30 = 240. Six resource reagents (Ashlar, sugar cane seeds) have a `DefaultQuality` of -3 or -2 in the data: `ContentDb::item` reads it as 1 (they were unknown records and went to the Backpack).
- **The grid** (`items/resource_grid.*`, pure): an item takes `inv_w` x `inv_h` cells and the slot the client is told is the row-major index of its top-left cell, `y * columns + x`; a new stack goes to the **first free rectangle scanning rows, then columns**, skipping blocked and covered cells. The 12 slots of five retail captures fit it (1198's Resource1 `0 3 6 8` and Resource2 `1 3 5`, 1470's `0 3 6`, and 1469's 24 and 1471's 27 as free rectangles): `test_worldd_resource`.
- **One routing** (`Inventory::grant`, `grant_what_fits`; no caller picks a container): a resource item first **tops up** the stacks of the same record and quality that have room (up to the record's `MaxStackSize`, 20 for the raw items; the bag's `Artisan_Gathering_<Prof>_Add_MaxStack` modifiers are not replicated, they are 46 / 42 on 1198's Mining / Lumberjacking T2 bags), container by container, then starts new stacks in the first container with room, in index order; a top-up is the Remove and Add of the same instance that `consume` sends (modelled: no Fill event is captured, but 1198's containers hold a stack of 15 Weeping Willow and one of 10 Halcyonite that harvests of 5 grew, so retail does merge). Everything else takes the lowest free Backpack slot. `grant` is all or nothing; `grant_what_fits` places what fits and returns the rest (the harvest sends it to the pile); `can_grant` asks. The paths: the harvest (`complete_gather`), the loot window's take (a pile mixes both kinds; a stack stays in the corpse when ITS storage has no room), the GM vendor, an item's use (the room is tried on a copy), and the death's material loss (`is_material` reads the resource containers too: about half of each stack of materials falls into the pile).
- **The bags** are the ones worn when the inventory is built (`Inventory::from_items(items, error, catalog)` takes the grids from the Equipment slots 23 to 25 and refuses a resource item that is outside its grid, on a blocked cell or on another item). A bag worn later gets its container at the next login (`[session] ...: a resource bag is worn in Equipment slot N: its container opens at the next login`); a bag whose container is open cannot be taken off or replaced (refused; the container's items and the client's layout belong to it).
- **Moves**: a Move inside a resource container or between two is made (Remove and Add at the top-left cell, which must lie in the grid on free cells; a stack may slide over its own old cells); a Swap, a Fill by dragging onto a stack, a Remove (destroying an item by dragging it out; the backlog has it) and any exchange with the Backpack are refused with a log line, and the client is sent the containers again.
- **The wire** (`bootstrap/`): one dynamic `UResourceStorageComponent` per worn bag (`ItemLocationType` 11, 12, 13) with `OwnerInfo.ResourceContainers[i]`; each container's payload has its `columns x rows` slots, its `Dimension` and, for a custom bag, the `OverrideMatrix` (columns of int32 -1 / -2); the events target the container's guid (`ItemStorage`/`StorageKind` Resource0..2). At the join each container's Adds come right before its payload, after the Quest storage's (retail sends all the Adds first). Capture 1198's three payloads and its 16 resource Adds are rebuilt bit for bit by `test_worldd_items`. The result event of a harvest is the retail one up to the optional text (see below).
- **Persistence**: character service migration 0011 adds ONE table, `character_resource_items` (the columns and constraints of `character_items`, `container IN ('Resource0','Resource1','Resource2')`, slot 0 to 255, the instance guid unique per character in the table; across both tables the service's Go code checks it); `character_items`' container CHECK is not touched. `SaveInventory` replaces both tables in one transaction, `GetCharacter` returns both. The rollback block is in `services/README.md`.
- **The login's repair** (`Inventory::repair_resources`, in `build_player_setup`): the resource items a character holds in the Backpack (its stage 2 harvests) move to the resource containers by their grids, in the order of their Backpack slots, and the layout is saved; those no container has room for **stay in the Backpack** and are counted in the note (`resource items in the Backpack ...: N moved ..., M stay`); nothing is ever deleted. A character whose bag is missing keeps the starter's empty 6 x 4 container the client always had, and nothing is placed in it.
- **The pick-up popup** (the fix after the user's retest 2: "The icons of the gathered material dont show" -- the popup that shows what you just got, not the bag): the retail server sends an `FCalloutQueueInfo` with the Add of every harvested item, and the client draws the popup and its icon from it; without it the item arrived in the bag and nothing popped up. `items/acquired.h` makes it (`acquired_callout(item, name, ticks)`): the message "Acquired <a id="Item" style="Item_<band>" metaType="ItemLink" metaGUID="<record>" metaItemQuality="<quality>" ...> [Coconut Wood] </>x5" (for a quantity of 1: `[Name]</>`, no "x1"), twice (`Message` and `FormattedOnScreenMessage`), the stored item again (`ItemAssociated`: the icon), callout type 6, point size 36, chat category 141 and the time it was made. `deliver_harvest` puts it on every Add of a harvest, a hunt and a foliage cutting (`ItemEvent::callout`); a stack that was topped up (the Remove and the Add of the new total) is told the quantity that was added (inferred; no capture has a top-up). The item's name is `item.display_name` (contentc version 16, the record's `ItemName`; the 78 records without one fall back to the record name, and a content.db older than 16 opens and does the same). The wire is `protocol/repl/item_codec.h` (`CalloutQueueInfo`, the exe's order read and proven on the 24 retail events), the goldens `test_repl_resource` (the 24 events as captured read and written to the exact bits) and `test_worldd_items` (the callout made by `acquired_callout` equals the capture's, 24 of 24), `test_worldd_gather_session` (the daffodil harvest's event carries 1427's text). Not covered: the loot window's Adds (a corpse's loot taken through `ServerRequestLootStorageEvents`; no retail capture shows a callout there, and none of the 24 events is one) and the GM items.
- **Not done**: the bags' stat modifiers on a stack's size; a profession-preferred container (a bag with a matching `Artisanship.Gathering.<Prof>` tag is the research's inference, unproven: the first container with room takes the item); a bag worn mid-session opening its container; destroying items; the Foliage trees and plants and Hunting (the next parts of Gathering stage 3).
- **Tests**: `test_worldd_resource` (the grid against the captured slots, the catalog on the fixture and the real file, `from_items`, the routing, the top-up, the full container, the moves, the bags that stay, a partial consume, the repair, a round trip, and every one of the 10,568 items of the real content.db routed to exactly one storage), `test_repl_resource` (libaocnet: the 24 retail events that fill the Resource containers, read, typed and written back to the exact bits; `capture_resource.h` from `tools/capture/gen_resource.py`), `test_worldd_gather_session` (a harvest lands in Resource0 at slot 0 with the event on the container's guid and the Backpack untouched; the second harvest tops the stack up; a full Backpack blocks nothing; a full container makes the pile and the callout; a relog keeps the bag), `test_worldd_items` (the login's resource payloads and Adds, bit for bit).

### Foliage gathering: the painted trees, plants and stones (Gathering stage 3, part "Foliage gathering"; an uncommitted draft)

The Riverlands' painted foliage is harvestable: the 84,218 instances of `dist/content/region_foliage.tsv` (`mapc extract`; 73 defs, of which 69 have a harvest: the `Riverlands_*` pillars have none) in 671 owners (the foliage actors of 637 cells of 25,600 cm). A tree falls, a plant or a stone is gone on every client that sees it, the bag gets the rewards, and the instance grows back. The retail wire is from the twelve foliage harvests 1405 1410 1428 1429 1451 1459 1463 1464 1465 1468 1515 1519 (1428 and 1463: a whole cut and its cell actor; 1464: the refused cut with the callout and the interrupt) and 1198 (the login snapshot of the cells' states); `tools/capture/gen_foliage.py` writes them into `libs/aocnet/tests/capture_foliage.h`.

- **Data** (contentc 15, `services/cmd/contentc/foliage.go`, `services/internal/content/foliage.go`): `foliage_def` (402: the empty duration, the paint state, the three state slots), `foliage_state` (743: the state, its `LifecycleDuration`, the collision radius, the override interaction distance, the interaction), `gather_interaction` (236: every `GatheringInteractionData`, the foliage's and the 53 Hunting ones: the profession, the certification tier, the tool slot, the base duration and the formula ids, the reward table) and `gather_interaction_def` (194: the `PlayerInteractionDef`s that name one, with their radius and tolerance). `Gathering::interaction(guid)` turns a `gather_interaction` row into the `GatherDefRow` the stage-2 rules and rolls already take, so the requirements, the channel's length, the rolls, the wear and the experience run unchanged. `kMinContentcVersion` is 15.
- **The map** (`foliage/foliage_map.*`): the TSV's rows keyed by (owner, component, index) = what the client sends. The owner is the cell's `IFA` name (`FName` text + number: `libs/aocnet/protocol/repl/fname.h`), the component its `IntrepidGatherableFISMC_<n>`. 6,007 rows have a def id 0 or a state record's id: the def is resolved by `def_name`. A def name that two records repeat (`Stone_Limestone_Large_2`, alike in every number) uses the first by guid. The towns' runtime trees (the node layouts) are not in the map: a start on one is refused as `unknown instance`.
- **The state** (`foliage/foliage_store.*`, pure): only the deviations from the painted state are kept (a harvest leaves 1 in 10,000). A harvest makes the instance Empty (`EFoliageState` 3); then **Empty -> (EmptyDuration) -> Infancy -> (its LifecycleDuration) -> Juvenile -> (its) -> Adult**, ending at the def's paint state, where the instance is whole again and leaves the list (an Oak: 300 s + 1800 s + 1800 s; a plant: 1800 s + 300 s; a stone has only Adult: Empty for its EmptyDuration, then whole). [modelled from the data: no capture spans a regrowth.] `--foliage-regrow-mult` scales every phase; the seasonal factor of the data is ignored. One cutter per instance (a per-instance lock released however the cutting ends).
- **The cell actors** (`foliage/foliage_cells.*`, `ActorKind::Foliage`): **every cell has an `AIntrepidStateIFA` from the start** (`make_all`: 671 actors, 10,613 components, 22 ms and about 17 MB at start), with one `UIntrepidFoliageStateComponent` for each of the owner's FISMCs (byte-sorted), `bInitialized` true, and in it `IndexedStates`, a fast array of {Key = the index, States = the state} that is empty until something is cut; schemas generated by `tools/ghidra/schemagen.py`. **Why up front (the fix after the user's test 1):** the client drops a foliage hit unless `FoliageStateActorLookup[the IFA's FName]` holds a state actor whose `FoliageStateComponentLookup[the FISMC's FName]` has `bInitialized` (the exe: `0x1433dd7b0`, the lookup `0x1433dbb40`, the target scan `0x146b456f0`); the first draft made an actor at a cell's first cut, so no untouched tree, plant or stone had a prompt. Retail sends them at login (capture 1198, packets 244 to 313, 7 cells right after the pawn). An untouched cell's open is retail's **to the bit** (tests: `test_worldd_foliage`, the ten painted cells of 1198's login and 1428, 166 component blocks) but for `AuthServerIDReplicated`, a retail game-server id (0, 24, 37 or 48 in the captured cells) that the emulator, one server, sends as 0 like every actor [R: constants.h]: the actor block is an AActor's (ReplicatedMovement in whole cm, scale 1), the cell's centre in x and y and, in z, the middle of its instances' lowest and highest (SerializeNewActor's header at scale 10, the block at the nearest cm with a tie down: ten of ten). A harvest is an item `{Key, 3}`; regrowth changes it (0, 1); an instance back at its paint state is **deleted** from the array, as 1428 packet 139 does. [R: 1428, 1463] The actor never stays open: it is `snapshot_only` (every connection with the cell within the snapshot ring is sent it, opened and then closed with Dormancy **265 ms** later, as capture 1463's open to its close) and the view **keeps each component's fast-array shadow** across the close, so a re-open sends only what changed since that connection last had it (the retail re-opens carry the one changed component's delta). A connection's first look at a cell lists the Empty instances as **11** (3 + 8, capture 1198's login snapshot: 620 of 948 items) unless they were cut within the last 10 s (then 3, the live harvest the client plays as a fall). [modelled: the 11 is read from one capture; the 10 s window and 3-vs-11 rule are ours.]
- **The start** (`PlayerSession::on_foliage_interact`, `session/player_foliage.cpp`): `ServerStartInteractionWithFoliage` (the index omitted when 0; the 3-bit state). Refused, each logged `[foliage] <name>: the cutting of <what> is refused (<kind>): ...` and counted (`foliage_refused()`, `foliage_refusals()`): `dead`, `mounted`, `already channeling` (a node or another tree), `unknown instance`, `wrong state` (the client's state is not the world's), `no interaction`, `moving`, `out of range`, `instance busy`, then the stage-2 checks (certification, the tool in the interaction's slot, its tier and durability, the tool's profession). **Moving**: the player moved less than 250 ms ago [R: 1464's first start, 44 ms after a moving move, was refused; the second, 695 ms after, was not; the threshold is modelled]; the refusal is the retail one: the pawn gets the callout and `ClientInterruptInteractionWithFoliage`; every other refusal sends the same call without the callout (the client sets its own FoliageData with the start: see the trees' retest 4 below), and so does `already channeling` while a NODE's or a hunt's channel runs (the pre-merge review's A1: nothing on the client's way from the key to the start reads ChannelingData); only a start while this player's own TREE channel runs is not answered (the client's FoliageData then is that channel's, and a retail client does not send it: it answers "Unable to interact with Item while gathering!" itself). Side effect on the client, read from the exe and unplayed: the call also broadcasts the HUD's "channeling ended" event (the same delegate that starts a channel's bar), which may hide the bar of the node or hunt that runs; the channel itself goes on on the server and completes. **Reach**: max(the state's `OverrideInteractionDistance`, the interaction's `InteractRadius`) + `InteractTolerance` + the state's `CollisionRadius` + 100 cm horizontally [modelled: the three captured starts stood 148 and 166 cm from an Oak and 75 cm from a Daffodil, each with 50 cm or more to spare].
- **The channel**: the length is the stage-2 `channel_seconds` (14.7 s for a large tree at rating 0 with a T1 axe, retail 1428: 14.71 s). 116 ms after the start `UPlayerInfo.FoliageData` is set on the pawn (the plain-commands struct: the owner and component FNames, the index, the state, the duration, the start time on the effects' clock unfloored, the tool) [R: 1428, 1463]; the client plays the animation and shows the bar. It ends early by the client's own `ServerInterruptChannel` (`on_interrupt_channel`; no capture shows what the client sends to cancel a cutting, so the stage-2 path serves), a drift of more than 60 cm from where the cutting began (the stage-2 safety net), the reach, a new ability, mounting, death, the tool taken off or swapped, the instance leaving the world (never), and the session ending. A cancel gives no reward, frees the instance and clears `FoliageData` (duration 0, index 0xFFFFFFFF, no names, state 3, tool 0 [R: 1428]); the movement, death, mounted and generic cancels also send the callout `Economy` / `GatheringInterruption_<Movement|Death|Mounted|Generic>` (type 4, point size 32, ChatCategory 4 [R: 1464 packet 191, every field but the time and the key]; only Movement is captured, the other keys are the exe's).
- **The result**: `deliver_harvest` (factored out of stage 2's `complete_gather`, shared with Hunting): the durability event, the storage events of the rewards (`Inventory::grant_what_fits`; a full bag drops a pile as in stage 2) and the `ClientConfirmMultiInteractFoliage` (1 bit), the XP tracker, then the cell's state update and `FoliageData` cleared [R: 1428's frame order]; the frame's tag changes the log prefix (`[foliage] ...`) and the line's tail (`the instance is whole again in S s`). The log lines the user's test reads: `[foliage] on: N instances of D defs in O owners with C components; D defs, H harvestable, U unresolved; ... regrowth xM`, `[foliage] 671 state actors made, one for every cell of the map (10613 components, each initialized) ... (N ms)`, `[foliage] state actor open for player P on channel C: <owner> (first look at the cell | the changes since the last look, B bits)` (the first 100 opens of untouched cells, and every open of a cell that was ever cut or grew), `[foliage] <name>: starts cutting <what> (...)`, `[foliage] <name>: harvested ...`, `[foliage] <instance> (<owner> <component> #<i>) grows: now <state>` (the first 60), and the refusals and cancels above.
- **Knobs**: `--foliage-regrow-mult` / `AOC_FOLIAGE_REGROW_MULT` (1; the in-game launcher uses 0.01: an Oak is Empty 3 s, a sapling 18 s, medium 18 s), `--foliage-map` / `AOC_FOLIAGE_MAP` (`dist/content/region_foliage.tsv`; a missing file or a content.db without the foliage tables: `[foliage] harvesting is off: ...`, the foliage is seen and not harvested), `--no-gathering` / `AOC_NO_GATHERING` switches the foliage off with the nodes (the old `[gather] ... foliage gathering is off in this world` line).
- **Not done / modelled** (the report and the backlog list them): stones as foliage (20,892 instances, 18 defs: no capture of one; they follow the tree's path with the data's numbers); the regrowth end to end (the data's durations; no capture spans one); the 11 vs 3 rule; the reach and the 250 ms thresholds; the callout keys other than Movement; the client's own cancel of a foliage cutting (no capture; the stage-2 `ServerInterruptChannel` path is used); the seasonal factor; the towns' runtime trees (the node layouts' foliage); `FoliageData`'s start time and clock are inferred from two captures.
- **Tests**: `test_repl_foliage` (66 checks; libaocnet: the captured FoliageData sets and clears, the cell actors' opens and deltas of 13 captures and the RPCs read, typed and written back to the exact bits; `capture_foliage.h`; and the client's call on a subobject it names by path beside a retail start, `capture_in_place.h`), `test_worldd_foliage` (97; the map on the real TSV, the store's regrowth and locks, the cells' states and the first-open form, the data of the real content.db, the reach against the three captured starts), `test_worldd_foliage_session` (146 checks when the whole file ran on 2026-10-16: 135 before the pre-merge review's A1 test, which adds 11; it was 83 before the trees' retest 4, then T1's 22 on top; a whole cutting from start to regrowth with a second client watching, every refusal [each answered with the interrupt], the cancels, one cutter per tree, a flood of 400 starts, a full bag, a world without foliage, T1: a start in the bunch of the client's call on a subobject it names by path, and A1 [`test_a_tree_start_during_a_node_channel`]: a tree's start while a node's channel runs is refused and answered with one interrupt, the node untouched, a tree cut after it), and the existing `test_worldd_actor_view` (`test_state_actor`: the snapshot-only open, the 265 ms close, the kept shadows), `test_worldd_gather_session` (the node's Movement callout) `test_worldd_content_db` (version 15) and `test_generated_schemas` (52 schemas with the two new ones).

### Hunting: the creatures that are hunted with a Bow, standing (Gathering stage 3, part "Hunting" and its fix; an uncommitted draft)

The 53 `Hunting_<kind>` creatures of the data (17 kinds spawn in the Riverlands today: 759 of the area's 10,554 NPCs, 7.2 %, the chance being the asset sets' weights that `spawn_point` already applies) are huntable: a huntable is the friendly, standing version of an enemy; you walk up to it, interact with it **alive**, and the Bow in Equipment slot 38 hunts it for a carcass or a trainable and Hunter XP; it dies with the done update (the user, 2026-10-12: "you go to it, while its standing still, you interact with it, and it either drops as a carcass or tameables"). The retail wire is from the five hunts 1456 (a Bear), 1457 (a Raven), 1458 (a Wolf), 1178 (an Anvilhorn Ram) and 1179 (a Hawk), which start at the client's interact with no kill before it, and the living huntables' opens of 914 (a Raven), 758 (a Spider) and 1458 (a Tidesnapper): `tools/capture/gen_hunting.py` -> `libs/aocnet/tests/capture_hunting.h`.

- **Data**: no new table (content.db stays version 15): `gather_interaction` (the 53 Hunting interactions among its 236: the Bow, 15 s, radius 200 / tolerance 30 cm, the tier Novice 13 / Apprentice 22 / Journeyman 18, the reward table) and `gather_interaction_def` (a `PlayerInteractionDef` -> its interaction). `gather::hunting_defs(FoliageData)` is the set of defs whose interaction is the Hunting profession's (`content::kProfessionHunting`); `Gathering::hunt_kind(def)` the kind of one (`HuntKind`: the def, its name, the interaction row, `gives_nothing`).
- **The living creature** (`spawns/generic_npc.*`): an asset whose `interaction_def` is a hunting def gets an eighth block, a dynamic `UInteractableComponent`: the Raven's captured one (914 packet 1561) with `ComponentDefId` = the asset's `interact_component` and `InteractionInputData[0].PlayerInteractDefinition` = its `interaction_def` (the three captured blocks differ in those two ids alone; the Spider's and the Tidesnapper's are rebuilt from the Raven's to the bit in `test_worldd_hunting`). **Its other blocks are a living huntable's, not a hostile creature's** (capture 914 sends both from the one `Pop_Raven` instance): the UNPCInfo carries the `CharacterName` the nameplate shows (`gather::hunting_names`: the def's name without `Hunting_` and with a space before each capital; "Raven", "Spider", "Tidesnapper" and the Bear of 1458 packet 316 are the captures', the multi-word ones are inferred); the one Blueprint alignment is the Friendly one (`6064632310208593979` in `npc_motion.tsv`, `alignment_relation`: Friendly to the Player alignment; a hostile Raven lists two others; the client's `ABaseCharacter::IsFriendly/IsEnemy/IsHuntingPrey` read the relationship flags, and only the CanTame Grem prototype's alignment is `Hunting_Prey` to the players); the CombatInfo writes `bIsDead`, `bInvulnerable` and `bNotTargetable` (all 0); the AbilityComponent is empty (it fights nothing).
