The CombatInfo and AbilityComponent are copied from the captured Raven, the name put in; `test_worldd_hunting` proves all four against the three captures. **A huntable stands**: `npc::SpeedModel::standing_defs` (the world's hunting defs) makes `make_setup` give it `Ambient::Stationary` whatever its instance says (the instances of retail's huntables are `Default` = a wander; the user saw them wander and the client refused the hunt of one that walked, "can't tame right now"). The template is kept per (Blueprint, component, def): `HuntingTest` and `Hunting_Wolf` share one Blueprint and only the second is huntable. `instantiate` mints the component's id and keeps the def in `NpcInfo::interact_def` (every instance with an interactable block does, a vendor's too). `GenericConfig::hunting_defs` and `hunting_names` (filled by `PlayerWorld::load_area`; `GenericConfig::hunting = false` by worldd `--no-gathering`) switch it on: with none every creature keeps its seven blocks, so the other tests' templates are as before. The Raven, the Spider and the Bear have captured opens already (eight blocks, their ids are the assets'). Log: `[hunt] huntable creatures in the area: N of M NPCs (K kinds ...)` and `[hunt] by kind: ...`.
- **Its end** (`combat/aftermath.*`): `Aftermath::set_huntable` (the world says a creature is huntable when its `interact_def` is a hunting def and gathering is on). `hunt_started` makes a `living` record for the channel (until it is over and 5 s more), `hunt_cancelled` takes it away (the creature is alive as it was; a record nobody ends is dropped by `tick`, never the creature), `hunt_done` is the end: the done update killed it for its clients, the record is a hunted one, the creature goes 4.0 s later ([modelled like an emptied loot]; the five captures end 0.7 to 2.1 s after the done update with it still open) and its spawn point refills it as after a kill (`schedule_respawn`). A friendly huntable cannot be hit (the damage abilities reach the hostile only), but one that dies anyway (a GM's kill, the tests') dies as any other: its experience, **no loot** (the Glint certificate and Bezoar rolls of `Hunting_Base` are not dropped: flagged for the user; 1457 has only the hunting interactable) and no pile, a corpse that waits 120 s (`hunt_corpse_seconds`, [modelled]) and is hunted the same way. The line `[loot] ... a huntable: its corpse is hunted with a Bow (no loot)`.
- **The hunt** (`PlayerSession::on_hunt_interact`, `begin_channel`, `session/player_gather.cpp`): the component of a huntable creature goes to the hunt, not to the loot window, **alive** (nothing is refused as `alive` any more; only a creature whose hunt is done is `corpse hunted`). The checks and the start are the node's (`begin_channel`, shared): in reach (330 cm), not busy (`already channeling`, `corpse busy`, `corpse hunted`), the stage-2 requirements (`not certified`, `level too low`, `no tool`, `tool broken` ...; a Novice hunts a Novice kind only), the channel's length (the Speed equation: 15.00 s at rating 0, the captured hunts 13.9 to 14.8 s with their ratings), the board. The tool is the Bow in Equipment slot 38: 1457's `ChannelingData` carries the tool guid that the result frame's `ClientUpdateItemDurability` then names, and all five hunts' durability updates name slot 38. The pawn's `ChannelingData` is the node's (the creature's actor, the Bow, the clock) [R: 1457 packet 134]. Every refusal is logged `[hunt] <player>: the hunt of <kind> (<name> <id>) is refused (<kind>): ...` and counted with the harvests'. The cancels are the node's (the client's `ServerInterruptChannel`, 60 cm, a death, mounting, an ability, the Bow off, a disconnect): no reward, the creature alive as it was, hunted again at once.
- **The corpse's wire** (`gather/node_wire.*` `corpse_begin/beat/done/reset`, `NodeBoard` for an `ActorKind::Npc`): the same component and properties as a node and **not** `bGathered`. Start: the status 0x08000001, `bWasInteractedWith`, the harvester's pawn (its own view only), who channels. A heartbeat every **67 ms** (a node's: 100) whose counter (the status word's top five bits) goes up by **2** (a node's: 3); 204 to 213 in a hunt. The done update: phase 4, the counter one tick (33 ms) on from the last heartbeat's when the update follows it within 50 ms, else two (5 of 5 captures), nobody channeling, `CanInputActivate` four entries of which the first is false, **all four** interaction states 0, the corpse dead for its clients (`combat::mark_dead`: bIsDead, Health 0, a `bNotTargetable` it had cleared; the kill wrote them already) [R: 1456 1457 1458 1178 1179]. A cancelled hunt: status 0, `CanInputActivate` four entries (the first true again: an empty array crashes the client), as a living huntable's open.
- **The result** (`deliver_harvest`, tag `hunt`): the `Hunting_<kind>` reward table through the stage-2 roller, the carcass or trainable into Resource0 on the grid (a carcass 1x1 to 2x2, a trainable 2x2; a full bag: the stage-2 pile and callout), the Bow's wear, Hunter XP (160 / 200 / 240 by tier, +50 % of that for each rarity above Common; the five captures +160) and Adventuring XP. **The data's `NumberOfSubtablesToSelect` of 2 is read as 1 for the roots with two subtables** (a hunt gives the carcass OR the trainable, 80 % / 20 %; read as 2 it gave both and the XP twice, and every captured hunt gave one item and one XP line). Ten kinds give no item (`HuntKind::gives_nothing`, found at start by a dry run of every table with a level-25 Bard's stats): seven with only `RETIRED_`/`NOTUSED_` items (Ebon Omen, Kirin, Midnight Mare, Obsidian Stallion, Plumed Haleberder, Runic Steed, Terrapin), the empty `Hunting_ShellOfTheAncients`, the Wyrmling (its only subtable has weight 0) and the CanTame Grem prototype (predicates on the creature's wounds, which the roller answers with "no"): their corpses are hunted all the same, the items are dropped, and the log says `this kind gives no item`. None of the Riverlands' 17 kinds is among them. Log: `[hunt] <player>: starts hunting ...`, `[hunt] <player>: harvested <kind> (...): ...; the corpse goes in 4 s`, `[hunt] <player>: the hunt of ... ends with no reward: <why>`, `[hunt] N hunting kinds: K give no usable item ...`.
- **Not done / modelled** (the backlog lists them): whether the retail client offers the prompt on a living huntable the same way (the draft arms the interactable at the spawn, as the captured living ones are, and the 2026-10-12 retest sent four interacts on a living `Hunting_Rabid`); that a huntable can be attacked at all (the captures show no hit on one: the CombatInfo says neither invulnerable nor untargetable, the draft's relation says friendly, so none lands); what a huntable does after the done update beyond the dead look (the five captures end 0.7 to 2.1 s after it: the draft removes it 4 s later); who may hunt (any player; retail's rights are not captured); the Glint and Bezoar drops; the done counter's one-or-two rule (5 captures, the 50 ms split between 46 and 68 ms); the creature's wounds (the CanTame prototype's tables); the multi-word nameplate names; no saving of a half-hunted creature across a restart; the Silkworm Larvae walk in retail (capture 1398, 94 cm/s) and stand here; the Riverlands' huntable chance is the data's, there is no knob.
- **Tests**: `test_repl_hunting` (43 checks; libaocnet: the three living blocks read, written back, and the Spider's and Tidesnapper's made from the Raven's with two ids put in, to the bit; capture 1457's corpse channel, 204 heartbeats; the five runs' facts; `capture_hunting.h`), `test_worldd_hunting` (99; the template for a Wolf (7 blocks), a Hunting_Wolf (8), HuntingTest, the captured Raven, the Spider's and Tidesnapper's blocks to the bit; **the three living huntables' name, alignment, CombatInfo and AbilityComponent against 914, 758 and 1458 to the bit, the nameplate names, a huntable standing where a plain Wolf of the same instance wanders**; the 53 hunting interactions' tiers, tool, reach, tables, the ten that give nothing, 300 rolls of each of the 43 others with their XP, the five captured items among the rolls; capture 1457 replayed through the board and the corpse writers to the bit (the start, 204 heartbeats, the done update with the corpse's CombatInfo); the board's second player, cancel and done-counter rule), `test_worldd_hunting_session` (74; fake clients on the combat world: a living creature hunted with no kill, a whole hunt to its end (the frame's order, Resource0, the Bow, Hunter XP, the done update, dead for its clients), the refusals, a plain corpse that still opens its loot, the cancels (the creature alive as it was), two players, a flood of 400, a full bag, a kind that gives no item) `test_worldd_aftermath` (69; the huntable corpse's lifetimes and the kill switch) and `test_worldd_region` (63; the 10,554 golden once more with the hunting creatures made: 759 huntable of 17 kinds, each with the eighth block, no other generic creature with an interactable).
- **Retest 3 (who attacks first; the end of a hunt)**: (1) *Aggro.* An instance that overrides the aggro radius with **0** (`override_aggro` on, `aggro_radius` 0: all 31 instances of the `Pop_TimidGrem` set, the Timid Raven, Spider and Deer sets, and 440 instances in all) is the data saying "never notices a player"; the loader used to read it as "no data" and the combat world gave it the default 800 cm, so Timid Grems attacked on sight. `NpcInfo::aggro_off` now carries it (`spawns/area_loader.cpp`), and `combat::notices_players(hostile, npc)` decides `npc::ai::Params::proximity_aggro`: a creature friendly to players (a huntable) or with its aggro off never starts a fight by noticing someone; it still fights back what hurts it. An instance with no override and no radius keeps the default (the Blueprint's radius is not in the data). The huntable's friendly alignment did not leak onto Timid Grems: their open lists the Blueprint's two alignments, Rabid and NPC_PlayerEnemy, as capture 623's Rabid does (no capture opened a Timid creature). (2) *Stats.* A huntable creature gets its stats list filled (`CombatWorld::set_huntable`, `fill_huntable_stats`) the first time a player is near, as a combatant's is, so the hunt's done update carries the stats block the five captured hunts have (278 bits: the Health item to 0) beside the CombatInfo's and the interactable's; before, the creature died for its clients with Health untouched. (3) *Diagnostics.* A client bunch that does not read now says so every 10th time (it was silent after the third, and takes every RPC in the bunch with it); a call worldd ignores is named at info once; a foliage start whose parameters do not decode is warned. The client sets its own `UPlayerInfo.FoliageData` the moment it sends a foliage start and refuses every other gatherable ("Unable to interact with Item while gathering!") until the server's `FoliageData` or `ClientInterruptInteractionWithFoliage` resets it (exe: the setter 0x146c471f0 copies the 64-byte struct into the pawn's UPlayerInfo at +0x208; the check reads its OriginalInstanceIndex at +0x218), so a foliage start worldd does not answer leaves the client stuck. T1 (`test_worldd_hunting_session` with `HUNT_ONLY_END=1`) pins our end of a hunt against 1456, 1457, 1458, 1178 and 1179; T2 (`test_worldd_hunting`, `test_timid_grem`) the Timid Grem.
- **The trees' retests 3 and 4 (a tree's bar runs and never finishes; 0 foliage starts in worldd's log)**: (1) *The dropped bunch.* The start never reached `on_foliage_interact` because `repl::read_client_bunch` dropped the whole bunch it came in. The client calls an RPC on a PlayerController subobject that worldd does not replicate, so it has no GUID for it and `UPackageMapClient` names the subobject IN PLACE in the content block's header: the default GUID (ObjectId 1), FExportFlags 5 (path + checksum), the outer's GUID, the path, the checksum (`BlockHeader::in_place`, `InPlaceName`). `read_content_block` refused ObjectId 1, and the bunch ended as "a bunch of 550 bits on channel 3 (PlayerController) does not read: ignored" (three at every login; ten a second in the 13:34 run, 210 in half a minute; the 550, 2200, 4950 and 7150 bits of the older logs are 1, 4, 9 and 13 of the same block, so the client writes one per 0.1 s interval that a long frame spans [inferred: a looping timer], and a start sent in that frame shares the bunch). No retail capture has such a block (333,369 client blocks read), so what the call is stays open: only the first 24 bytes were ever logged; the log of the next run names it (the path, the outer, the field's bits and the whole first bunch, once per name: `note_unserved_blocks`). Now the reader reads the name to its last bit and goes on by the block's bit count (`ClientBunch::in_place`), and an RPC field the class has no RPC for is skipped the same way (`skipped_fields`); a name that does not read, a payload past the bunch and more than 1024 calls still refuse the bunch, and the server's direction (`server_form`) still refuses ObjectId 1. The session counts them and says so at its end ("the client's calls the reader did not serve"). (2) *Always unlock.* The client's own FoliageData is not in worldd's shadow, so a clear of ours is an empty diff and only `ClientInterruptInteractionWithFoliage` gives the client its gathering back (the exe's `ClientInterruptInteractionWithFoliage_Implementation` sets the pawn's FoliageData to its cleared value, index -1 and state 3, and reads no parameter): every refusal sends it (the callout stays the walking start's), a start that does not decode and a world without foliage too, and a cancel before FoliageData went out too; not the refusal of a second start while this player's own TREE channel runs (the client's FoliageData then is that channel's); a start while a node's or a hunt's channel runs IS answered (the pre-merge review of 2026-10-16, A1: it was silent here and left the client "gathering"; test `test_a_tree_start_during_a_node_channel`). (3) Tests: `test_repl_foliage` (66 checks: the block's header against the 24 logged bytes, the start beside the call in both orders and behind thirteen of them, the bounds, the unknown fields) and T1, `test_worldd_foliage_session` with `FOLIAGE_ONLY_UNNAMED_CALL=1` (22 checks: a retail start in the bunch of the call reaches the handler; the refusals and the early cancel unlock the client; the second start does not; a new kind of bunch that does not read is said even after the budget of three; the session's summary counts the calls by name).

## Run

`scripts\services.ps1 run -World worldd` starts it with the Go services, and
`scripts\launch_go.bat worldd` starts the whole stack with the game client. Build it first:

```powershell
scripts\build.ps1 -Target worldd
```

Run from the repo root, as `services.ps1` does, so the default token path resolves:

```
worldd --bind 127.0.0.1:7777 --public-address 127.0.0.1:7777 --mesh 127.0.0.1:50053
       --gateway 127.0.0.1:50054 --character 127.0.0.1:50052
       --service-token-file dist/services/service.token [--log-file worldd.log] [--max-sessions 1024]
       [--content-db dist/content/content.db] [--gamestate-mode tier1|tier2|tier3|captured] [--max-move-speed 2135]
       [--area-radius -1|1000] [--gm-garden] [--no-zone-filter]
       [--world-admin 127.0.0.1:50056] [--no-world-admin] [--gm-commands]
```

At start it binds the UDP port, registers with mesh (retrying until mesh answers) and sends
heartbeats. If mesh answers a heartbeat with NotFound, worldd exits: its ServerId, and every
NetGUID minted under it, are dead (mesh.proto). Ctrl+C tells connected clients the server is
shutting down.

**GM travel tools.** worldd serves `WorldAdminService` (`proto/internal/aoc/worldadmin/v1`) on `--world-admin` / `AOC_WORLD_ADMIN_ADDR` (default `127.0.0.1:50056`;
`--no-world-admin` turns it off): `ListPlayers`, `ListPlaces`, `Teleport(character, place | x y [z])` and `SetMoveSpeed(character, multiplier)`, which `dist\services\worldctl.exe`
(`players`, `places`, `tp`, `speed`) and the admin app to come call. Calls carry the service token and arrive on a gRPC thread; they are queued (`svc/world_admin.h`'s
`AdminQueue`) and applied by the network thread's loop between two ticks, where `session/gm_travel.h`'s `GmTravel` moves the player the way a respawn does (the move checker
rebased, `ClientAdjustPosition` to the client; a mounted player is dismounted first, its mount desummons by distance) or sets `MoveSpeedMult` (the pawn's replicated stat, the
mount's while riding; the checker's limits scale with it; until relog). `--gm-commands` / `AOC_GM_COMMANDS=1` (off by default) makes the client's own `ServerTeleportXYZ`,
`ServerTeleport` and `ServerTeleportPlayer` do the same; each one the client sends is logged whatever the switch says (a control character in the text a player sends, a newline above all, is shown as `?` in that line and in the warnings that name the key or the names: `log_text`, so a player cannot start a log line of its own). The whole description, the places rule and the numbers are in
`services\README.md`, "GM travel tools"; `test_worldd_gm_travel` is its test (the session, the client RPCs, the admin API over a real gRPC server).

What the log shows for a login:

```
[endpoint] 127.0.0.1:52065 connected for character bbec5c86eb101fe5f38c87f0fa22e7d0
[login] character cyb (bbec5c86eb101fe5f38c87f0fa22e7d0) of account 3 logged in
[endpoint] character cyb (bbec5c86eb101fe5f38c87f0fa22e7d0) finished loading and joined
```

The join ticket is never logged. A refused login shows the player a message (`login_text` in
`login/login_service.h`) and closes the connection. A second login of a character that is
already on worldd drops the first client.

## Test

`test_worldd_*` build with the rest (`scripts\build.ps1`), land in `dist\Release`, and run in the
unit gate (`scripts\run_tests.ps1`). `test_worldd_endpoint` drives a whole login in memory from the
retail client's captured Initial (and checks the rate limit: a flood of 100 KB is cut to the 20 KB bucket, a session has its own bucket,
the retail client's pace is never touched); `test_worldd_services` runs the gRPC clients against in-process
fakes. `test_worldd_channels` sends chains on actor channels and compares the parts with the original
server's bunches (ch 3 pkt#22, ch 6 pkt#28..#45, ch 14 pkt#62/#63, ch 21 opens and closes), and checks closing
channels and ack events; `test_worldd_repl_acks` runs the libaocnet replicator on a real client/server pair of
connections, with a dropped packet.

`test_worldd_bootstrap` builds the captured character's bootstrap and compares each chain with the original's bits (the
golden half), then decodes the whole join sequence of every GameState mode with libaocnet's readers and checks
Connection's chain limits (the offline harness).

`test_worldd_session` runs worldd after Join (`tests/test_worldd_session.cpp`): the endpoint rig takes a fake client through
the handshake, the login (with the retail client's `?Name=` and unique id) and Join, and the client's received chains (each
whole: its export bunches and its content, reliable bunches in order, partial bunches put together; `tests/fake_client.h`)
are compared with the sequence an independent `PlayerBootstrap` builds from the same inputs; it decodes cyb's name and Guid
from the pawn and the login's name and unique id from the PlayerState. A bare `Connection` with the fake client checks the
acknowledgements (exports leave the replies once acknowledged, stay while not), the phase-in, the captured `ServerMovePacked`
bunches (TimeStamps equal the legacy server's parse of the same bunch), the retail client's possession bunch (combat-settings
echo, the load's six replies), storage requests, unknown and malformed bunches, a join whose first chains do not fit the
window, and a client that stops acknowledging while it asks for its load over and over (replies wait, none is lost or
reordered). A missing content.db refuses the login with the text, and so does one built before plan B2 (no item tables: it does not open, and the log says to rebuild it); a character the bootstrap refuses is told "Login failed".

`test_worldd_actor_view` (plan E1) is the actor system on a simulated clock and two real `Connection`s: the registry (a duplicate or odd id, the grid across cell edges and at negative coordinates, a move that changes cells, the counters, listeners, the component index, an owner), the rings and their hysteresis, the view (the first channel, an awake open, a snapshot and its Dormancy close, a dormant actor opened again with the same bits, an update with only the changed handles, reliable or not, Relevancy and Destroyed closes, a channel held until its close is acknowledged, the cap and nearest-first order, exports sent once acknowledged, another player's actors never sent, a wide actor (a town's replicator) that leaves the registry query's radius alone) and 1,800 actors around a walking player for a minute (time and bandwidth measured). `test_worldd_area` runs the area on the real content.db (the working tree's `dist\content\content.db`, or `AOC_CONTENT_DB`): the captured spawns parse, every template instantiates and differs from its capture only in the instance's values and writes an open, the population (the research's counts and 3,929 points, the same NPCs every time, a smaller radius has fewer), `load_area` (ids, positions, the GM vendors, the radius), and the GM vendors on the real records (26 defs, stacks of `MaxStackSize`, every refusal). `test_worldd_area_session` plays it on a connection with the fake client: nothing before the phase-in, the rings after it, a removed actor, the vendor flow (interaction, window, purchase with its events), the hostile requests (unknown, unseen, far and non-vendor actors; no window, an unlisted item, another vendor, bad quantities, a full bag, bits that are no cart, a vendor that is gone, a flood) and the crowd of 1,500 with its measurements.

`test_worldd_combat` (plan E1, stage 3, part 1) runs the combat core on the REAL `content.db` and `npc_motion.tsv` of the working tree (skipped, and says so, without them): the NPC stat engine against the research's retail numbers, the damage functions and the hit rules, the combat data (the alignments' relations), the NPC replicator (stat items, CombatInfo), the AI on a simulated clock through the combat world (aggro, chase, bites, a player's hit on the NPC, the leash and the reset, a lethal hit and the one death-hook call), a player's cast through the ability pipeline (target, range, line of sight, dead and unknown targets, the item's Targets, the hit request and its confirmation with targets) and an NPC's hit on the player. `test_worldd_npc_ai` is the AI and the threat table as pure logic. `test_worldd_spawn_filter` holds the filter to its conditions. `test_worldd_ability_session` has the session's side (`test_e1_combat_session`): a cast naming a Wolf, the hit request, the Wolf's Health, its bite on the player and the in-combat flag on and off. The shared stat corpus is `stats/testdata/corpus.txt` (regenerate it with `go run ./internal/statprog/cmd/statprog corpus --out <file>` in `services`: the Go and the C++ engine must give the same bits).

`test_worldd_tooltips` (plan E1, stage 3 fix, Part C) holds the ability runtime to the game client's tooltips for all 255 abilities (`tests/tooltip_key.h`, written by `tools/capture/gen_tooltips.py --check`; the listed differences in `tests/tooltip_known.inc`, exact): on a character of each class at level 25 with no gear, `AbilityPipeline::inspect()` gives the cooldown, the charges, the enabled costs and the cast time, the catalog the target mask, the hits' stat-mod terms and the effects reached; the report counts per group the abilities whose every line matches, those whose every number matches (the cast time and the range listed), the listed and the unlisted ("The tooltip as the truth" above).

`test_worldd_talents` (plan D1, stage 2) is the talent rules as pure logic: on a synthetic tree the points and costs, every gate (level, points spent in the tree, any or all of
`SkillsRequired`, a requirement that names nothing, mutually exclusive skills both ways round, the ranks of a skill in order), what a request may do (judged whole, refunds first, a swap, a respec,
an auto-granted rank, a rank twice, an unknown id, a tree that cannot be bought, too many entries), the lists, abilities and mods; on the real trees of `tests/talent_fixture.sql` (the real
rows of content.db, written by `tools/worldd/gen_talent_fixture.py`) the catalog, the points by level, the Ranger's auto-grant order, **a level-1 Bard's KnownAbilities and records equal to the
captured Bard's element for element** and real Bard purchases; `talent_data` on files whose rows are of the wrong storage class; and the action bar. `test_worldd_items_session` has the session's side
(its header lists it): the join's trees, lists and default bar (the captured payload), a purchase's reply by its tags (`A L U`, `E+ L U`, `A L`, `E- L`, `A E- L`, `L`), a marker's end, a
swap and a respec, refused requests (nothing changes, the list is sent again), the bar, a level's class grants, the live class change and back, a relog, a weapon swap and regeneration.

`test_worldd_ability_pipeline` (plan D2) runs the generic ability runtime on a simulated clock on the REAL `content.db` (`tests/ability_rig.h`: a Bard of a level, A7, the pipeline and a `Pawn` that takes the events with `apply_ability_change`'s key rules; it is skipped, and says so, without the file): the catalog, a cast's timeline and charges, every Mana cost as k x 5.6875, Resonance and Fortissimo, Glee's heal, ticks, refresh and expiry, the engine's forms of a mod, a song and a cast at the air, the refusals, the clock, regeneration, the movement windows, and Sprint's 1.3 beside the generic cooldowns. `test_worldd_ability_retail` replays the 23 captured ServerTriggerAbility of a Bard's abilities (`capture_ability_replies.h`, written by `tools/capture/gen_ability_replies.py`) and holds the runtime to retail's reply: accepted, the ability item and the Cooldowns item in retail's order within 0.2 s, Mana to the decimal, Resonance, and the effects with retail's duration, tick and stacks. `test_worldd_ability_session` has the session's side (`test_d2_*`): Glee heals, Flourish's slide is not corrected, a Dodge after other casts leaves their cooldowns, the Sprint's 1.3 and hostile triggers.

`test_worldd_move_checker` (plan A6) is the checks as pure logic: a walk, a sprint, a dodge and a jump accepted; a teleport,
a non-finite number, a TimeStamp going back, a location outside Verra and a climb or fall that is too fast each refused with
their own reason; a refused move changing nothing; the time a move may claim; the slack as a credit; the upward burst (the in-game ledge is accepted, a sustained climb is held to the speed once the burst is spent, and the burst refills); the limits configurable;
the pacing against the server's clock (a client that races its TimeStamp, 36 racing moves at one instant and a client whose
clock runs 8x fast are held to 1.25 x the speed limit over real time; a stall refills the bank only to 5 s); the Verra box (the
eight corners of the content envelope are inside, a centimetre beyond any of the six faces is `OutOfWorld`); and every one of
the 2,483 captured moves accepted at the defaults when they arrive at the pace of their own TimeStamps, in 0.2 s and 1 s
bursts, and with a client clock 15% fast (and 1,040 of the native run's refused at 900 cm/s).
`test_worldd_movement` runs the rest of A6: `PlayerBootstrap::correction` decoded back with the client-faithful reader;
`CharacterSaver` against a fake character service (latest wins, a hung service never blocks the caller, the bound, retries,
refusals, a throwing call, bounded flush and destructor, and a million saves from a producer thread against a service that
fails a third of its calls: never an older position after a newer one, every character ends with its last); sessions with a
fake client (every captured move acknowledged, with the server's clock following the capture's TimeStamps; corrections equal
to the bootstrap's bits, one per datagram, never in mode 0; pending and old moves; a TimeStamp that does not rise dropped
silently; 36 racing moves in one datagram; a damaged parameter; `--max-move-speed`; the log lines); saving through a
recording sink (the end of the session, the 60 s tick, never a refused position, the end after a position that came back to
the record's); and through the Endpoint with a real `CharacterSaver`: the client quits, times out, is kicked for another
login, worldd stops, the 60 s save fails three times and the player quits standing still (the position still reaches the
service), and a duplicate login (the new session starts at the old one's last good position, which is saved first).
`test_repl_move_packed` (libaocnet) reads every captured move and writes it back, refuses an `end` beyond the buffer and runs a
seeded fuzz (40,000 reads, about 0.3 s) of mutated captures, random containers and hostile NumBits from exact-size buffers.

`test_worldd_abilities` (plan A7) reads the ability rules from the fixture (and, with `AOC_CONTENT_DB`, from the real file, which
must hold the same numbers); runs the `AbilityRuntime` on a simulated clock (a Sprint takes its first charge with the activation, empties a
full bar in 80 charges and ends with `ClientEndAbility` 163 ms later, charges only while the player moves and not at all once the
last move is 0.75 s old, a cancel stops it without `ClientEndAbility`, a Dodge does not end it, a Rest does, MoveSpeedMult is 1.2
while it runs, a bar of 101 never goes below 0; regeneration is off while sprinting and moving; Stamina is back 20 s after the end, 1 s pause then
5 a second; a Dodge costs 50, is refused under 50 and in its 0.65 s cooldown to the tick, keeps regeneration off for 1.5 s and names its
montage and MeshYaw by AnimationIndex; `ServerInstanceId` is the server's counter; Rest's effect, its regeneration, its end and the
replacement of one Rest by another; anything else is refused; one jump of the clock gives the events of many steps and the clock never goes
back); rebuilds the original server's six `General_Rest` packets (pkt#8175-8320) with `PlayerBootstrap::apply_ability_change` and
`ability_update`, each bit for bit, the montage's export included, plus a Stamina delta, the `Cooldowns` item, `ClientEndAbility` and what
`apply_ability_change` refuses; and compares the retail server's Sprint and Dodge replies (capture 745, decoded to their last bit) with the
items the runtime sends, field by field: one chain with the ability item, MoveSpeedMult, the effect and the first charge, the DisableRegen effect
with MoveSpeedMult 1.0 at the end, the Cooldowns item (Charges 1), and a Sprint started with 1.25 Stamina (retail's last case).
`test_worldd_ability_session` takes a fake client through a session (the Harness of `test_worldd_movement`) that sends the
retail client's own `ServerTriggerAbility`, `ServerNotifyHeldAbilityCancelled` and `ServerMovePacked`, and rebuilds the client's view from
the replies alone: a 20 s Sprint on the HUD in 80 steps of 1.25, its items and `ClientEndAbility` after the removal, the refill; a
cancel; a Sprint standing still; Dodge, its cooldown and its refusals; Rest with the montage's export, the effect and the regeneration;
abilities worldd does not run, a trigger that does not read, the budget; a client whose acknowledgements are late (the export rides
in both updates until acknowledged; every Sprint step arrives in order), a server loop that stalls for 15 s (one update, not ten), and a
whole Sprint through the `Endpoint` (its own ticks run the runtime); a Dodge in the middle of a Sprint (both go on), a Rest after one (the Sprint is ended
with `ClientEndAbility`), the client's ids against the server's instance numbers, the dash against the move checker (2,800 cm/s accepted for the
length of a Dodge, refused outside it), a trigger before the join is on the wire and an update behind a full window (both wait, nothing is lost), the log
(the first 100 transitions at info; abilities off with the pawn's state drifted: the warning) and a seeded hostile client (random operations with hostile
ids, stalls and held acknowledgements, and triggers and cancels damaged bit by bit, 3 seeds each: Stamina never outside [0, max] on the server or the client,
nothing fails, every chain reads, the client's view is the runtime's once settled). With `AOC_CONTENT_DB` set the session test runs on the real file.
`test_repl_ability_rpc` (libaocnet) decodes all 54 captured trigger and cancel calls of the two captures in the repo to their last bit
and writes them back to the client's bits, refuses export flags the inline-object form does not define on the way in, then reads 16,254
mutated copies (a seeded fuzz) without a crash. `test_worldd_move_checker` also replays the retail Rogue's dash (capture 742: 2,237 cm/s) with
and without the Dodge's window.

`test_worldd_items` (plan B2) checks the rules (`items/inventory.h`: the events in the retail order and what each says, the slot the server picks, two-handers and what they displace, a full bag, every refusal leaving
the inventory as it was, moves and swaps), against 20 captures of the retail server's answers (`tests/capture_equip.h`, 84 events from the equip and unequip captures: the same events in the same order from the state each answer needs, the `HeldItems` and
`AppearanceIDs` after, and the array key moving by 23 for each change of the Equipment storage), the storage RPCs bit for bit (the retail login's three payloads and 109 events and the Bard's four payloads and 13 events, rebuilt from the items they hold; the pawn's
second state of the captured Bard built from its kit equals pkt#107 as before), the setup with items (and a record that cannot be played refused), `content.db`'s `item` (the fixture's, and with `AOC_CONTENT_DB` the real file's Bard kit and equip facts) and a random run
of 18,000 requests (60 inventories) that never breaks an invariant, then the review's longer fuzz (three seeds on the fixture's catalog and, with `AOC_CONTENT_DB`, two on the real file's records: no item made or lost, worn only where the record lists, a two-hander's slots never shared, the events replayed on the earlier inventory give the later one; a few seconds), 40,000 buffers of noise through the item readers, and `restore(checkpoint)` putting the `HeldItems` array and its key back
(a reply built again after the connection's window was full moves the key once, not twice). `test_worldd_items_session` plays it through a fake client that rebuilds its view from the server's replies alone (the PlayerController's events and payloads, the pawn's `HeldItems` and `AppearanceIDs`): the join; the chest
taken off and put on (two events, `HeldItems` with the key 23 on, `AppearanceIDs` with three pieces and back, no stats, all in one packet, the request sent as the retail client sends it: both RPCs in a datagram); a two-hander over a main hand and an off hand; a ring; moves and swaps;
43 requests of every refusal each leaving everything as it was, a full bag, an unknown record; the budgets (12 requests at once, the corrections four times and then two seconds apart); 2,500 hostile requests (valid, wrong and damaged bit by bit; the inventory stays valid and the
client agrees); saving (never touched: nothing; the end; the 60 s tick only when dirty; none after finish; a second login); `CharacterSaver` with inventories (latest wins, retries, refusals, a position and an inventory for one character); through the `Endpoint` with a real saver
(the client quits, times out, is kicked, worldd stops, the 60 s save lost three times, a second login whose old session's final save was lost: the new session saves the carried inventory when it ends); the `HeldItems` update waiting for a full window on the pawn's channel; the review's probes (a join with nothing worn and then every item put on, everything off and on twice, a full bag, a character with no items, the periodic and final saves); an item-bearing `ServerTriggerAbility`; and, with `AOC_CONTENT_DB`, the same session on the real file. `test_repl_item_rpc` (libaocnet) reads `ServerItemUsed` and `ServerActivateItem` from four
of the retail client's own calls, writes them back to the same bits and fuzzes them.

Plan B3's tests. The content.db fixture now holds the real stat program (`tests/stats_fixture.h` takes it from the shared corpus `stats/testdata/corpus.txt`), the real rows of the eight classes' starting kits (`tests/gear_fixture.h`, generated from the real
file: the items, their stat blocks, quality curve, tags, the EEquipSlot names and the classes) and a few made-up items with stat blocks (the heavy chest: Constitution and Mentality +100, ring A: MaxStamina +50, the mace: Strength +10), so a `PlayerWorld` on it runs
the real formulas on the real kits. `test_worldd_stats` runs the gear rules and `CharacterStats` on tables (interpolation in every band, half-up rounding, sets and tiers, the tags, the clamp, the base and equipment stats). `test_worldd_character_setup` proves that the captured
Bard in its kit gives the whole captured setup, that a bare Bard's attributes are `class_stat_base`'s, that each class's kit has its own stats and the layout rules (`stats_setup_for`), that each class's level-1 kit gives Go's mods, tags and digest of every
stat from the fixture and, with `AOC_CONTENT_DB`, that all 18 gear cases of the corpus do on the real tables. `test_worldd_content_db` pins the version refusal, the stat program row and the item data reads. `test_worldd_session` pins a damaged,
absent or foreign stat program (the world is not ready). `test_worldd_items_session` plays it on a fake client that rebuilds the StatsComponent from the replies: the join's arrays are the session's stats; the chest off and on sends exactly the engine's delta
(the starter chest has one stat, so its physical mitigation rating and the percentage derived from it) in the chain of the gear update and restores every stat to the bit; Health and Mana clamp and stay; a costume sends nothing; the deltas wait for a full window; MaxStamina goes through the runtime and a MaxStamina item taken off after two Dodges does not refill the Stamina; the fishing rod's base and equipment travel; two earrings of Hook_Rate (clamped to 100) change the equipment contribution and not the value, and the client still hears it; a stat run that faults for the new gear (the fixture's shirt: Constitution +1e39) sends nothing, as the join refuses such a character; a change the pawn's arrays cannot take is not committed to the session's stats nor to the ability runtime (the session's stats are always what the client has). `PlayerWorld` also refuses a stat program whose row crc32 is not its header's.
`test_worldd_abilities` pins `tracks` and `gear_stat` (the raise under a Rest and a Sprint, the clamp).

`test_worldd_content_db` builds a small SQLite file (`tests/content_db_fixture.h`, the tables and columns the reader
uses) and checks the reader, the refusals (a missing file is not created, an empty or foreign file, another schema
version, a missing table) and that opening and querying never write; with `AOC_CONTENT_DB` set to a real `content.db`
it also checks the Bard (archetype 17747) at level 1: Strength 24, Dexterity 24, Constitution 30, Mentality 30,
Wisdom 36, Intelligence 39 (the capture has 60, plan C1's known difference). `test_worldd_character_setup` proves the
mapping: the capture's own customization -> the client's JSON -> back equal to the last bit; cyb's JSON (the client's
text and the jsonb text, `tests/cyb_appearance.h`) maps without error and round-trips through the codec; the capture's
character record gives a setup equal field by field to `captured_character_setup()` but the Intelligence item and
the equipped items; cyb-like records of Kaelar, Dunir and Empyrean, male and female, build a setup a `PlayerBootstrap`
accepts; and the error cases. Both skip their real-database half without `AOC_CONTENT_DB`.
